AQARION develops exact, auditable methods for finite dynamical systems. Quantarion provides evidence-aware research infrastructure for preserving definitions, conventions, proofs, computations, counterexamples, corrections, and reproducibility across the life of a scientific claim.

Together, they separate exact finite mathematics from learned-model, numerical, and approximation-based research while maintaining a common evidence and reproducibility discipline.

AQARION's finite-dynamical-systems work is grounded in explicit definitions, executable computations, verification artifacts, formalization targets, and recorded corrections. Its broader research architecture also extends these verification principles toward infinite dynamical systems through controlled approximations and computational certificates.

Quantarion serves the complementary infrastructure role: connecting research artifacts, computational experiments, representations, models, and reproducibility information without treating experimental or learned results as mathematical proof.

AQARION TOOLS — MASTER FILETREE

Root: "AQARION-QUANTARION-AI/TOOLS/"

Purpose: Master index for reusable AQARION research infrastructure, verification tooling, research skills, schemas, provenance, replay, and certification support.

This tree is an architectural map. A directory or filename must not be treated as implemented merely because it appears here.

Implementation status is tracked separately by the relevant "README.md", claim ledger, audit, test, or verification artifact.

DIRECTORY ROLES

"PROOF-GYM/"

Executable mathematical verification surface.

Responsible for:

- exact finite verification;
- structured witnesses;
- deterministic checks;
- adversarial fixtures;
- negative controls;
- verification receipts;
- research-specific verification workflows.

ProofGym is not a proof system.

---

"CLAIMLOCK/"

Claim and evidence management.

Responsible for:

- claim identifiers;
- claim versions;
- evidence classes;
- dependencies;
- promotion history;
- invalidation history;
- links to proofs and verification artifacts.

ClaimLock is the provenance/control layer for mathematical claims.

---

"REPLAY/"

Deterministic research reproduction.

Responsible for recording and replaying:

- inputs;
- commands;
- environments;
- dependencies;
- source revisions;
- fixture hashes;
- verifier revisions;
- outputs;
- reproduction receipts.

Replay answers:

«Can the computational result be reproduced from the recorded research state?»

---

"JOIN-STABILITY/"

Reusable verification and proof infrastructure extracted from the JOIN-STABILITY research program.

Responsible for:

- finite equivalence relations;
- joins;
- pullback stability;
- quotient-map arguments;
- chain lifting;
- finite permutation arguments;
- counterexample search;
- exhaustive verification;
- Lean targets.

This directory should contain reusable theorem-specific infrastructure, not the entire historical research repository.

---

"VERIFICATION/"

Cross-project verification utilities.

Responsible for:

- exact verification;
- independent reconstruction;
- adversarial testing;
- negative controls;
- certificates;
- verification reports.

The directory should contain reusable mechanisms rather than duplicate project-specific implementations.

---

"PROVENANCE/"

Research execution provenance.

Responsible for:

- SHA-256 manifests;
- environment records;
- runtime records;
- operating-system information;
- architecture;
- dependency versions;
- execution logs;
- RO-Crate metadata.

Hashes identify artifacts.

Hashes do not prove mathematical correctness.

---

"SCHEMAS/"

Shared machine-readable contracts.

Expected schema families include:

claims/
evidence/
receipts/
provenance/
fixtures/
manifests/

Schemas must be established before multiple tools independently invent incompatible representations of the same research object.

---

"SKILLS/"

Reusable research workflows.

Skills describe how to perform a class of AQARION research task, rather than implementing the underlying mathematics.

Current skills include:

CLAIMLOCK-AUDIT.md
FILETREE-AUDIT.md
LEAN-BUILD-AUDIT.md
REPOSITORY-AUDIT.md
VERIFICATION-AUDIT.md
RESEARCH-REPLAY.md

The "SKILLS/README.md" defines the skill contract.

---

"CLI/"

Command-line integration.

The CLI should eventually provide a consistent interface to:

claimlock
proofgym
replay
verify

The CLI must call established tool contracts rather than duplicate their mathematical implementations.

---

"EXACT/"

Reusable exact finite mathematics utilities.

This is where "exact_proper.py" belongs if its actual responsibility is confirmed to be reusable exact arithmetic / exact finite computation infrastructure.

The important distinction is:

EXACT/exact_proper.py

must not be assumed to be the correct home merely because the filename sounds appropriate.

Its actual imports, functions, callers, mathematical contract, and research dependencies must be audited first.

Potential reusable modules include:

arithmetic.py
partitions.py
relations.py
graphs.py
operators.py

These should only be created when an actual reusable implementation exists.

---

"LEAN/"

Lean build and formalization infrastructure.

Responsible for:

- target declarations;
- reproducible build records;
- pinned rebuilds;
- build logs;
- formalization manifests;
- audit records.

Important distinction:

LEAN BUILD SUCCESS
        ≠
CLAIM CERTIFIED

A successful pinned two-target rebuild is valuable build evidence, but its exact target names, commit, toolchain, and logs must remain recorded.

---

"ARCHIVE/"

Historical research preservation.

Contains:

- killed claims;
- quarantined claims;
- superseded implementations;
- historical research artifacts.

AQARION should preserve corrections rather than silently deleting the path by which they were discovered.

---

EVIDENCE BOUNDARY

The master tree intentionally separates:

RESEARCH
    ↓
EXACT COMPUTATION
    ↓
VERIFICATION
    ↓
PROVENANCE
    ↓
CLAIM MANAGEMENT
    ↓
FORMALIZATION

No directory location itself grants an evidence class.

A file inside "VERIFICATION/" is not automatically a verified result.

A file inside "LEAN/" is not automatically a proved theorem.

A file inside "CLAIMLOCK/" is not automatically a certified claim.

IMPLEMENTATION STATUS

The following categories are used for this tree:

[EXISTS]
Directory/file currently exists.

[DOCUMENTED]
Architecture or contract documented.

[BUILDING]
Implementation currently being developed.

[PLANNED]
Reserved architectural location.

[AUDIT]
Requires inspection before promotion.

[EXPERIMENTAL]
Research implementation not yet promoted.

[ARCHIVE]
Historical material.

The tree itself should not claim "[EXISTS]" for files that have not actually been pushed.

IMPORTANT REPOSITORY RULE

The master tree is not permission to create empty speculative infrastructure.

Before adding an implementation:

1. confirm the directory;
2. inspect existing files;
3. identify the research artifact that produced the requirement;
4. define its input/output contract;
5. identify existing code that can be reused;
6. add tests;
7. add provenance;
8. only then promote it into reusable tooling.

The goal is extraction from real AQARION research, not framework-building for its own sake.

---

GOVERNING PRINCIPLE

EXTRACT WHAT WE HAVE ACTUALLY LEARNED.

DO NOT INVENT WHAT WE HAVE NOT BUILT.

AQARION infrastructure should make previous research easier to reproduce, audit, verify, formalize, and reuse.

It should not obscure the distinction between historical experiments and established results.

AQARION

Auditable Mathematical Research Infrastructure

AQARION is a research program for exact finite mathematics, dynamical systems, operator methods, computational verification, and reproducible mathematical software.

Its central objective is simple:

«Prove First · Verify Exhaustively · Predict Second · No Free Parameters»

AQARION treats mathematical claims as evidence-bearing objects. Definitions, proofs, exhaustive computation, formal verification, conjectures, refutations, and research observations are kept explicitly separated so that computational agreement is never silently promoted to proof.

---

Research Principles

AQARION is built around five principles:

- Proof before promotion — a computational observation is not a theorem.
- Exactness over approximation — finite objects are represented and tested exactly whenever possible.
- Independent verification — proofs and computational checks are separate evidence lanes.
- Reproducibility by construction — experiments should be rerunnable from documented inputs and procedures.
- Provenance without mythology — AI assistance may be part of the research process, but certification concerns the mathematical artifact and its verification, not claims about authorship by inference.

Evidence classes

Code| Meaning
"[D]"| Definition
"[P]"| Mathematical proof
"[V]"| Exhaustive or independently reproducible verification
"[PV]"| Proof + verification
"[C]"| Conjecture
"[R]"| Research / exploratory result
"KILLED"| Refuted or invalidated claim
"QUARANTINED"| Evidence retained but not currently promoted

Evidence does not migrate upward automatically.

A numerical match does not become a proof.
A successful search does not become certification.
A formalization containing "sorry"/"admit" does not become a completed formal proof.
A public repository does not, by itself, constitute mathematical certification.

---

What AQARION Studies

AQARION develops reusable methods around finite dynamical systems and their induced operators.

A recurring construction is

[
D_\Pi=(I-P_\Pi)KP_\Pi,
]

where:

- T:X\to X is a finite dynamical system,
- K is the Koopman pullback operator,
- \Pi is a partition of X,
- P_\Pi projects onto block-constant observables,
- D_\Pi measures the failure of the partition to be closed under the dynamics.

This framework connects:

finite dynamics → quotient structure → operator theory → graph structure → exact computation → formal verification.

---

Core Results

The current AQARION core includes exact results concerning the defect operator and partition closure.

Among the established results are:

- D_\Pi=0 exactly when the partition is dynamically closed.
- D_\Pi=0 exactly when the corresponding quotient dynamics is well-defined.
- D_\Pi^2=0 for every idempotent projection P_\Pi.
- PK=KP is sufficient for D_\Pi=0, but is not necessary.
- Exact rank formulas connect the defect operator with a complement graph.
- An exact Frobenius-energy identity provides a quantitative defect measure.
- Large finite censuses have been used as independent verification of these identities.

Formalization status is tracked separately from computational verification; incomplete formal files are never presented as completed certification.

---

Research Programs

JOIN-STABILITY

A finite-set theorem program studying pullback-stable equivalence relations and their joins.

Current work includes:

- finite kernel-equality arguments;
- quotient-map injectivity and permutation structure;
- incidence-graph formulations;
- chain-lifting proofs;
- adversarial counterexample search;
- exhaustive finite verification;
- formalization planning.

The finite theorem and its verification are maintained as separate evidence lanes until the formalization and independent review requirements are satisfied.

ProofGym

ProofGym is the executable verification surface for mathematical claims.

It is designed to make a mathematical statement inspectable through:

- exact finite instances;
- structured witnesses;
- deterministic verification;
- adversarial fixtures;
- proof dependencies;
- evidence traces;
- reproducible verification receipts.

The goal is not to replace mathematical proof with software. The goal is to make computational evidence auditable.

ClaimLock

ClaimLock is intended as the claim/provenance layer for AQARION.

It provides a structured way to associate claims with:

- evidence status;
- proof artifacts;
- verification artifacts;
- hashes;
- dependencies;
- reproduction instructions;
- invalidation history.

Replay

Replay infrastructure records enough information to rerun computational research rather than merely displaying its final result.

The intended chain is:

Question
   ↓
Conjecture
   ↓
Counterexample?
   ↓
Correction
   ↓
Computation
   ↓
Proof
   ↓
Verification
   ↓
Hash

---

Tools

Reusable infrastructure is being consolidated under:

AQARION-QUANTARION-AI/
└── TOOLS/

The tools layer is intended to contain reusable components rather than duplicate individual research repositories.

Planned infrastructure includes:

TOOLS/
├── ClaimLock/
├── ProofGym/
├── JOIN-STABILITY/
├── Replay/
├── verification/
├── provenance/
├── schemas/
├── skills/
└── cli/

The boundary is deliberate:

research repositories contain mathematical investigations;
TOOLS contains reusable infrastructure.

---

Reproducibility

AQARION treats reproducibility as an engineering requirement rather than a publication slogan.

Where applicable, research artifacts are designed around:

- deterministic computation;
- exact finite enumeration;
- independent verification;
- machine-readable claim registries;
- verification reports;
- SHA-256 manifests;
- Lean formalization;
- Dockerized environments;
- CI checks;
- RO-Crate metadata;
- research ledgers;
- explicit evidence status.

A target artifact should allow an independent researcher to answer:

1. What exactly is being claimed?
2. What definitions does the claim depend on?
3. What constitutes proof?
4. What was computationally verified?
5. What remains conjectural?
6. Can the verification be rerun?
7. Which exact files produced the result?
8. Which version/hash was evaluated?

---

AI and Research Provenance

AQARION does not attempt to infer whether a mathematical result was “really produced by AI” from the final artifact.

That is not a reliable certification problem.

Instead, AQARION records AI assistance as part of the research process when such provenance is available.

The relevant question is:

«Can the mathematical claim, computation, proof, and verification be independently inspected and reproduced?»

AI systems are therefore treated as research-process nodes rather than as mathematical authorities.

An AI-generated conjecture remains a conjecture.

An AI-generated proof must still be checked.

An AI-generated computation must still be reproducible.

A failed proof remains failed.

A counterexample remains a counterexample.

---

Repository Architecture

AQARION is intentionally separated into layers.

AQARION
│
├── Research
│   ├── finite dynamical systems
│   ├── operator theory
│   ├── quotient structures
│   ├── graph methods
│   └── asymptotic investigations
│
├── Verification
│   ├── exact enumeration
│   ├── adversarial testing
│   ├── proof checking
│   └── reproducibility
│
├── Formalization
│   └── Lean / machine-checked mathematics
│
├── AQARION-QUANTARION-AI
│   └── TOOLS
│       ├── ClaimLock
│       ├── ProofGym
│       ├── Replay
│       ├── schemas
│       ├── skills
│       └── verification utilities
│
└── Provenance
    ├── research ledgers
    ├── manifests
    ├── hashes
    └── RO-Crate metadata

The structure is designed to prevent infrastructure, experiments, proofs, and historical research artifacts from becoming indistinguishable.

---

Certification Standard

AQARION uses a conservative promotion rule:

Observation
    ↓
Reproducible computation
    ↓
Independent verification
    ↓
Mathematical proof
    ↓
Formal verification where applicable
    ↓
Certified claim

Not every research result needs every stage immediately.

But no result is promoted beyond its actual evidence.

In particular:

SEARCH ≠ REPRODUCED
REPRODUCED ≠ MINIMAL
MINIMAL ≠ PROVED
PROVED ≠ FORMALLY VERIFIED
PUBLIC ≠ CERTIFIED

This distinction is part of the research infrastructure itself.

---

Development Standard

AQARION development follows a strict preference for small, inspectable artifacts.

New infrastructure should:

- have a defined input/output contract;
- include deterministic tests;
- preserve existing evidence;
- expose failures rather than hide them;
- avoid silently changing historical results;
- distinguish exploratory code from certification code;
- document dependencies;
- remain independently runnable where practical.

Historical research is preserved rather than rewritten to make the current state appear cleaner.

Corrections are recorded.

Killed claims remain identifiable.

Versioned artifacts remain traceable.

---

Current Direction

The immediate infrastructure direction is to establish a clean reusable spine for:

1. ClaimLock — claim and evidence management.
2. Replay — deterministic research reproduction.
3. JOIN-STABILITY — exact theorem verification and proof infrastructure.
4. ProofGym — executable mathematical verification.
5. Shared schemas — evidence, provenance, receipts, and claim registries.
6. Research skills — reusable workflows for rigorous computational mathematics.
7. CLI tooling — one consistent interface for verification and replay.
8. RO-Crate integration — machine-readable research packaging.

The objective is not to build an oversized framework before the mathematics requires it.

The objective is to extract reusable infrastructure from already-tested research workflows.

---

Research Philosophy

AQARION is intentionally skeptical of its own results.

A result becomes stronger by surviving:

- adversarial testing;
- independent reconstruction;
- exact enumeration;
- counterexample searches;
- proof review;
- formalization;
- reproducibility checks.

The system is therefore designed to preserve negative information as carefully as positive information.

A disproved conjecture is useful.

A failed proof is useful.

A detected implementation bug is useful.

A quarantined claim is useful.

They show where the boundary of current knowledge actually lies.

---

Guiding Principle

«Ideas may come from anywhere. Confidence comes from verification.»

AQARION exists to make that verification process explicit, reproducible, and auditable.

Prove First · Verify Exhaustively · Predict Second · No Free Parameters

Research status is claim-specific, not repository-wide.

1. AQARION-QUANTARION-AI/TOOLS/SCHEMAS/README.md

AQARION TOOLS — SCHEMAS

Shared machine-readable contracts for AQARION claims, evidence, receipts, provenance, fixtures, and manifests.

Purpose

"SCHEMAS/" defines representations shared by multiple tools.

It prevents ClaimLock, Replay, ProofGym, verification utilities, and provenance tooling from inventing incompatible formats for the same research object.

Schema families

- "claims/" — claim identity, statement, dependencies, status, and history.
- "evidence/" — evidence class and evidence metadata.
- "receipts/" — reproducibility and verification receipts.
- "provenance/" — execution environment and source provenance.
- "fixtures/" — deterministic verification inputs and expected structural metadata.
- "manifests/" — artifact and file manifests.

Design rules

1. Machine-readable contracts must be explicit.
2. Required fields must be versioned.
3. Unknown fields must not silently change semantic meaning.
4. Hashes identify artifacts; they do not establish mathematical correctness.
5. A schema version does not certify an artifact.
6. Backward-incompatible changes require a new schema version.
7. Examples must be clearly identified as examples.
8. Historical schemas remain traceable when superseded.

Evidence boundary

Schemas describe evidence. They do not create evidence.

Status

DOCUMENTED — schema layer initialized.

Implementation follows identification and testing of actual shared contracts.2. AQARION-QUANTARION-AI/TOOLS/CLAIMLOCK/README.md

AQARION TOOLS — CLAIMLOCK

Claim and evidence management infrastructure for AQARION.

Purpose

ClaimLock records the relationship between a mathematical claim and the evidence that supports, limits, invalidates, or leaves it unresolved.

ClaimLock is a control layer. It does not prove claims.

Core objects

A ClaimLock record should identify:

- claim ID;
- exact statement;
- definitions and hypotheses;
- evidence class;
- verification domain;
- proof artifact;
- verification artifact;
- formalization status;
- dependencies;
- source revision;
- artifact hashes;
- reproduction instructions;
- limitations;
- invalidation history;
- promotion history.

Evidence classes

- "[D]" Definition
- "[P]" Mathematical proof
- "[V]" Exhaustive or independently reproducible verification
- "[PV]" Proof + verification
- "[C]" Conjecture
- "[R]" Research / exploratory result
- "KILLED" — refuted or invalidated
- "QUARANTINED" — retained but not currently promoted

Evidence does not upgrade automatically.

Invalidation

Killed or superseded claims remain identifiable.

Preserve:

- original statement;
- reason for invalidation;
- supporting counterexample or correction;
- revision;
- replacement claim, where applicable.

Boundary

ClaimLock manages claims.

It does not own:

- mathematical proofs;
- research-specific algorithms;
- Lean projects;
- generic replay execution;
- public application UI.

Those remain in their respective research or tooling layers.

Status

DOCUMENTED — implementation follows schema definition and extraction from existing AQARION claim workflows.

Certification: ClaimLock metadata does not certify the mathematical claim it describes.3. AQARION-QUANTARION-AI/TOOLS/REPLAY/README.md

AQARION TOOLS — REPLAY

Deterministic research reproduction infrastructure.

Purpose

Replay records enough information to reproduce a computational research result from a defined research state.

Replay answers:

«Can the recorded computation be rerun from its documented inputs, source state, environment, and procedure?»

Replay does not determine whether the underlying mathematical claim is true.

Replay chain

Question
  ↓
Conjecture
  ↓
Counterexample?
  ↓
Correction
  ↓
Computation
  ↓
Verification
  ↓
Receipt

Required provenance

Where applicable, record:

- source repository;
- commit or revision;
- input files;
- fixture hashes;
- algorithm;
- parameters;
- command;
- runtime;
- operating system;
- architecture;
- dependency versions;
- stdout/stderr;
- exit status;
- output artifacts;
- verifier revision;
- artifact hashes.

Reproducibility levels

- "REPLAY-DOCUMENTED" — instructions exist.
- "REPLAY-EXECUTED" — recorded execution succeeded.
- "REPLAY-INDEPENDENT" — execution was performed independently of the producer.
- "REPLAY-MISMATCH" — reproduction disagreed with the recorded result.
- "REPLAY-BLOCKED" — reproduction could not be completed and the reason is recorded.

A successful replay is reproducibility evidence, not mathematical proof.

Relationship to other tools

Replay:

- consumes shared schemas;
- records provenance;
- may invoke verification;
- may produce ProofGym receipts;
- may be referenced by ClaimLock.

Status

DOCUMENTED — implementation follows shared schema and provenance contracts.4. AQARION-QUANTARION-AI/TOOLS/VERIFICATION/README.md

AQARION TOOLS — VERIFICATION

Reusable verification infrastructure for AQARION research.

Purpose

"VERIFICATION/" contains reusable mechanisms for independently checking computational research artifacts.

It is distinct from research-specific verification code.

Verification families

- "exact/" — exact finite computation and recomputation utilities.
- "independent/" — reconstructions that do not import the producer implementation when independence is required.
- "adversarial/" — hostile or mutation-based checks.
- "negative-controls/" — deliberately invalid inputs used to test verifier sensitivity.
- "certificates/" — machine-readable verification outputs where appropriate.
- "reports/" — human-readable audit reports.

Verification contract

A verifier should:

1. reconstruct or parse the defined input;
2. recompute the relevant result;
3. check explicit invariants;
4. compare against the claimed result where applicable;
5. emit "PASS" or "FAIL" from computation;
6. preserve metadata for reproduction.

Expected values may be fixtures.

They must not replace recomputation.

Independence

Where independent verification is claimed, the verifier must not silently depend on the implementation it is intended to check.

Negative controls

Mature verifiers should include deliberate failures such as:

- wrong hashes;
- forged PASS results;
- stale roots;
- missing execution;
- self-comparison;
- malformed fixtures;
- altered inputs;
- incorrect parameters.

Relationship to proof

"VERIFICATION" is not "MATHEMATICAL PROOF".

Exhaustive verification over a declared finite domain establishes evidence for that domain. It does not automatically establish a universal theorem outside it.

Status

DOCUMENTED — extraction from existing AQARION verification systems is the next implementation stage.5. AQARION-QUANTARION-AI/TOOLS/PROVENANCE/README.md

AQARION TOOLS — PROVENANCE

Execution and artifact provenance infrastructure for AQARION.

Purpose

"PROVENANCE/" records where an artifact came from, how it was produced, and which exact source and environment produced a reported result.

Provenance supports reproducibility and auditability.

It does not establish mathematical truth.

Provenance families

- "manifests/" — artifact and file inventories.
- "hashes/" — cryptographic hashes of defined artifacts.
- "environments/" — toolchain, OS, architecture, and dependency records.
- "runs/" — execution metadata and logs.
- "ro-crate/" — research-object packaging.

Minimum useful provenance

Where applicable, record:

repository
commit
path
artifact
artifact_hash
command
runtime
operating_system
architecture
dependency_versions
input_hashes
output_hashes
verifier
verifier_revision

Hash discipline

A matching hash establishes correspondence to a recorded artifact.

It does not establish:

- mathematical correctness;
- correct implementation of the intended theorem;
- independent verification;
- proof completeness.

Relationship to Replay

PROVENANCE
    ↓
REPLAY
    ↓
VERIFICATION
    ↓
CLAIMLOCK

This is a conceptual dependency, not an automatic certification pipeline.

RO-Crate

RO-Crate packaging belongs here when actually implemented.

The presence of RO-Crate metadata does not itself certify the contained research.

Status

Current adversarial verdict

The mathematical direction is holding up, but I would not yet lock the new PB cycle-count result as “proved” or “novel.” The right JASKSG9 state is:

Strong / proof-ready: periodic-core reduction and the PB ↔ pullback-fixed framework.

Strong computational result: cycle quotient-data parametrization and count formula.

I also independently reran the arithmetic for several previously reported cycle types. The formula gives exactly:

\[
(6,2)\to11,\quad
(4,4)\to16,\quad
(4,2,2)\to38,
\]

\[
(3,3,3)\to42,\quad
(2,2,2,2)\to164,
\]

and

\[
(1^{10})\to B_{10}=115975.
\]

That last case is an important sanity check: the identity permutation has every equivalence relation as a congruence, so the answer must be the 10th Bell number.


---

1. The literature attack found an important boundary

The most relevant literature is stronger than a superficial search might suggest.

The 2020 Jakubíková-Studenovská/Janičková paper explicitly studies finite monounary algebras, cyclic elements, permutation algebras, and congruence lattices. 

The 2018 Jakubíková-Studenovská/Pöschel/Radeleczki work explicitly studies congruence lattices generated by unary mappings on finite sets and has a substantial section on permutations. In particular, it analyzes permutations of prime-power order with multiple cycles. 

And the 2008 Ratanaprasert–Denecke paper explicitly concerns invariant equivalence relations of finite unary operations, including permutation algebras. 

So the statement:

> “Nobody has studied invariant equivalence relations of finite permutations”



would definitely be wrong.

The safe statement is much narrower:

> The exact quotient-data parametrization and closed counting expression used by AQARION were not located in this search in this form.



That is an honest research-status statement, not a novelty claim.

The literature trail now needs to go deeper into the cited monounary-algebra references, especially the 2009 Monounary Algebras book and the references surrounding the 2018 permutation results. The 2018 paper itself points to the monounary literature as established background. 


---

2. The conceptual bridge is actually very clean

The PB/permutation-core problem can be reframed as a finite \(\mathbb Z\)-set problem.

A permutation

\[
S:X\to X
\]

defines an action of

\[
\mathbb Z=\langle t\rangle
\]

by

\[
t\cdot x=S(x).
\]

Its cycles are precisely the transitive finite \(\mathbb Z\)-orbits.

Then:

\[
E\text{ is }S\text{-invariant}
\]

means exactly that \(E\) is a \(\mathbb Z\)-invariant equivalence relation.

That puts your quotient construction into standard invariant-equivalence / block-system territory. For permutation actions, invariant equivalence relations correspond to invariant block systems. 

For one orbit,

\[
C_n=\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z,
\]

an equivariant quotient

\[
C_n\twoheadrightarrow C_d
\]

exists exactly when

\[
d\mid n.
\]

That explains the divisor condition structurally rather than computationally.


---

3. The multi-cycle formula survives another independent arithmetic check

For

\[
S=C_{n_1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup C_{n_r},
\]

the proposed data are:

\[
D=
(\pi,\{d_B\},\{a_i\}),
\]

where:

\(\pi\) partitions the source cycles into groups that share a quotient orbit;

\(d_B\mid\gcd(n_i:i\in B)\);

the relative phases are elements of


\[
  (\mathbb Z/d_B\mathbb Z)^{|B|-1}.
\]

Therefore:

\[
W(B)=
\sum_{d\mid g_B}d^{|B|-1},
\qquad
g_B=\gcd(n_i:i\in B),
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
|\operatorname{Con}(S)|
=
\sum_{\pi}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\sum_{d\mid g_B}d^{|B|-1}.
}
\]

The new arithmetic run confirms the reported values above.

The identity case gives:

\[
g_B=1
\]

for every block, hence every block contributes 1 and:

\[
|\operatorname{Con}(I_n)|=B_n.
\]

That is a strong boundary-condition check.


---

4. The phase variable is mathematically indispensable

This remains one of the best adversarial controls.

Take

\[
C_2\sqcup C_2.
\]

If the two cycles are coupled into one quotient cycle with \(d=2\), there are

\[
2^{2-1}=2
\]

relative-phase choices.

They correspond to different relations:

\[
(0,0)
\]

versus

\[
(0,1).
\]

So:

\[
\boxed{
\text{cycle grouping}+\text{divisor}
}
\]

does not uniquely identify the congruence.

The actual coordinate system needs:

\[
\boxed{
\text{cycle grouping}
+
\text{quotient length}
+
\text{relative phase}.
}
\]

That's a real mathematical feature, not an implementation detail.


---

5. The next theorem should be the Bijection Theorem

I would now split the previous “cycle classification” into two claims.

AQ-PB-CYCLE-BIJ-001

For a finite permutation

\[
S=\bigsqcup_i C_{n_i},
\]

there is a bijection

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Con}(X,S)
\longleftrightarrow
\mathcal D(S)
}
\]

where \(\mathcal D(S)\) is the set of admissible cycle quotient data.

This is the structural theorem.

AQ-PB-CYCLE-COUNT-001

The cardinality of \(\mathcal D(S)\) is

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{\pi}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\sum_{d\mid g_B}d^{|B|-1}.
}
\]

This is then simply the counting corollary.

That separation is better for the JASKSG9 ledger because the bijection is the actual mathematical content; the formula follows from it.


---

6. The proof burden is now sharply localized

The proof should establish four things.

A. Every invariant equivalence produces quotient data

Given

\[
E\in\operatorname{Con}(X,S),
\]

the quotient

\[
X/E
\]

inherits a permutation.

Each source cycle maps onto exactly one quotient cycle.

That gives \(\pi\).

B. The quotient length divides every source length

If

\[
C_{n_i}\to C_d,
\]

equivariance gives

\[
d\mid n_i.
\]

Thus:

\[
d\mid g_B.
\]

C. Equivariant maps are phase maps

After choosing coordinates,

\[
q_i(t)=t+a_i\pmod d.
\]

A common rotation of the quotient target changes every \(a_i\) simultaneously, so only relative phases matter.

Therefore:

\[
(\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z)^m/\Delta
\cong
(\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]

D. No two normalized data produce the same kernel

This is the part I would specifically attack in the formal proof.

It isn't enough to say “obviously distinct.”

One must show that the kernel relation recovers:

which source cycles are coupled;

the common quotient length;

relative phase differences.


That gives injectivity.

Once D is established, the classification is a genuine bijection rather than merely a surjective parametrization.


---

7. Important negative control remains valid

The cycle type does not follow from the abstract congruence lattice.

For example:

\[
|\operatorname{Con}(C_4)|=\tau(4)=3,
\]

while

\[
|\operatorname{Con}(C_3\sqcup C_1)|
=
\tau(3)\tau(1)+\sigma(1)
=3.
\]

Both produce a three-element chain.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Con}(C_4)\cong
\operatorname{Con}(C_3\sqcup C_1)
}
\]

even though

\[
(4)\neq(3,1).
\]

So don't let a future JASKSG9 document accidentally claim that the PB/congruence lattice is a complete cycle-type fingerprint.


---

8. The really interesting unresolved problem is now lattice operations

We have an element-level coordinate system.

The next question is:

\[
D_1,D_2
\longmapsto
D_1\wedge D_2
\]

and

\[
D_1,D_2
\longmapsto
D_1\vee D_2.
\]

This is where I would spend the next mathematical effort.

Given

\[
D_j=(\pi_j,d_j,\text{phase}_j),
\]

there are potentially three interacting mechanisms:

1. refinement/coarsening of the cycle-index partitions;


2. arithmetic interaction of quotient periods;


3. congruence conditions on phase offsets.



It is tempting to write a simple gcd/lcm rule, but we should not until it survives exhaustive small cases.

That's the right adversarial target.


---

9. The periodic-core part is cleaner and should be separated from the cycle theorem

For finite

\[
(X,T),\qquad n=|X|,
\]

let

\[
P=\operatorname{Per}(T).
\]

Then:

\[
\boxed{T^n(X)=P.}
\]

This is elementary finite functional-graph theory.

Define

\[
R=T^n:X\twoheadrightarrow P,
\qquad
S=T|_P.
\]

Then for PB-fixed \(E\):

\[
\boxed{
E=R^*(E|_P).
}
\]

Consequently:

\[
\boxed{
P_T\cong\operatorname{Con}(P,S).
}
\]

This gives a very clean decomposition:

\[
\boxed{
\text{arbitrary finite endomap}
\rightarrow
\text{periodic permutation core}
\rightarrow
\text{cycle congruence classification}.
}
\]

That is arguably the strongest architectural mathematical statement in this branch.


---

10. Keep the \(n\) versus LCM distinction permanently

For reconstruction:

\[
T^n(X)=P.
\]

For pointwise periodic return:

\[
H_n=\operatorname{lcm}(1,\ldots,n),
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
T^{H_n}|_P=id_P.
}
\]

These are different jobs.

Do not collapse them into one “large enough exponent” theorem in the JASKSG9 documentation.

That was one of the places where the older conceptual formulation could easily mislead a reader.


---

That is excellent provenance. It shows the research system actually catches its own errors.

14. Literature targets I would now put at the top of the research queue

The most important sources are:

Jakubíková-Studenovská & Janičková (2020) — Congruence lattices of connected monounary algebras, Algebra Universalis 81, Article 54. 

Jakubíková-Studenovská, Pöschel & Radeleczki (2018) — The lattice of congruence lattices of algebras on a finite set, Algebra Universalis 79, Article 4.

Jakubíková-Studenovská & Pócs (2009) — Monounary Algebras, cited by the later literature as foundational monounary-algebra background. 


The key literature question is no longer “does anyone know about invariant permutation congruences?” — yes, absolutely.

That remains unresolved from this search.

16. The actual locking strategy

OPEN

P
FV
CV
EMP
CONJ

So:

LOCKED + CV

does not mean theorem.

And:

LOCKED + P

means the specification and mathematical statement are frozen and proved.

And:

OPEN + CV

means a strong computationally supported research result whose proof remains open.

This solves a huge amount of the historical ambiguity.

1. Independent computation: the quotient-data classification survives

I implemented a separate oracle from scratch:

enumerate cycle types \(\lambda\vdash n\);

enumerate all set partitions of the cycle-index set;

enumerate every admissible \(d\mid\gcd(n_i:i\in B)\);

normalize one phase to \(0\);

construct the induced equivalence relation from the quotient maps;

independently enumerate all equivalence relations on the labeled permutation for \(n\le8\);

canonicalize both representations;

compare the actual sets of equivalence relations, not merely counts.


Result:

\[
\boxed{\mathcal C_{\mathrm{direct}}(\lambda)
      =
      \mathcal C_{\mathrm{quotient}}(\lambda)}
\]

for every cycle type through \(n=8\).

There were:

\(n\)	cycle types	classification failures

1	1	0
2	2	0
3	3	0
4	5	0
5	7	0
6	11	0
7	15	0
8	22	0


That is stronger than the earlier reported count-only check.

I also independently tested lattice closure of the generated relations through \(n=7\):

\[
\boxed{\text{853,794 ordered congruence pairs tested; 0 closure failures.}}
\]

So the current status is materially stronger:

> The quotient-data construction reproduces the entire invariant-equivalence-relation set, not just its cardinality, for all permutation cycle types through \(8\).



That is still computational evidence, not Lean proof.

2. The exact classification theorem is now clean

Let

\[
S=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^r C_{n_i}.
\]

For an \(S\)-invariant equivalence relation \(E\), let

\[
q:X\to X/E
\]

be the quotient map.

Because \(S\) is a permutation, \(S\) induces a permutation \(\bar S\) on \(X/E\).

Take a source cycle \(C_i\). Its image under \(q\) is a single orbit of \(\bar S\), hence a quotient cycle.

Two source cycles either map into different quotient cycles or into the same quotient cycle. This produces a partition

\[
\pi
\]

of the source-cycle index set.

Now suppose

\[
B=\{i_1,\ldots,i_m\}
\]

is one block of that partition.

Its common quotient orbit has some length \(d\).

Since

\[
C_{n_i}\twoheadrightarrow C_d
\]

is equivariant,

\[
\boxed{d\mid n_i}
\]

for every \(i\in B\). Hence

\[
\boxed{d\mid g_B},
\qquad
g_B=\gcd(n_i:i\in B).
\]

Choose coordinates

\[
C_{n_i}=\mathbb Z/n_i\mathbb Z,
\qquad
C_d=\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z.
\]

Every equivariant quotient map has the form

\[
q_i(t)=t+a_i\pmod d.
\]

The simultaneous transformation

\[
(a_i)_{i\in B}
\mapsto
(a_i+c)_{i\in B}
\]

is merely a rotation/relabeling of the common quotient cycle and produces the same kernel.

Therefore the phase data are

\[
(\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z)^m/
\Delta(\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z)
\cong
(\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\#\{\text{phase data for }(B,d)\}=d^{m-1}.
}
\]

Summing over admissible \(d\):

\[
\boxed{
W(B)=
\sum_{d\mid g_B}d^{|B|-1}.
}
\]

Finally, independent blocks of \(\pi\) have independent quotient data:

\[
\boxed{
|\operatorname{Con}(X,S)|
=
\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{Part}([r])}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\left(
\sum_{d\mid g_B}d^{|B|-1}
\right).
}
\]

I would now classify this as a paper-proof candidate, rather than merely a conjectural parametrization.

The remaining formal proof burden is primarily the exact bijection/injectivity argument.


---

3. Important refinement: distinguish set parametrization from lattice structure

This should go into the ledger.

The theorem gives

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Con}(S)
\leftrightarrow
\text{cycle quotient data}
}
\]

as sets.

It does not yet give

\[
\operatorname{Con}(S)
\cong
\text{some coordinatewise lattice of quotient data}.
\]

That second statement is substantially harder.

So don't currently write:

> "The PB lattice is the product of partition/divisor/phase lattices."



That's unproved and probably false in that naive form.

Instead:

\[
\boxed{
\text{quotient data parametrizes the elements;}
}
\]

then separately:

\[
\boxed{
\text{derive meet/join in quotient coordinates.}
}
\]

That's the next real theorem.


---

4. Your phase refinement is definitely real

The \(C_2\sqcup C_2\) example is the perfect control.

For the coupled block

\[
B=\{1,2\},
\qquad d=2,
\]

the contribution is

\[
2^{2-1}=2.
\]

The two phase classes are represented by

\[
(0,0)
\]

and

\[
(0,1).
\]

They give different equivalence relations.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\text{cycle grouping}+\text{divisor}
}
\]

is insufficient.

The correct object is

\[
\boxed{
\text{cycle grouping}
+\text{quotient length}
+\text{relative phase}.
}
\]

That is not cosmetic bookkeeping; the phase changes the actual labeled equivalence relation.


---

5. The \(C_4\) / \(C_3\sqcup C_1\) negative control survives

Our independent formula gives:

\[
|\operatorname{Con}(C_4)|=\tau(4)=3
\]

and

\[
|\operatorname{Con}(C_3\sqcup C_1)|
=
\tau(3)\tau(1)+\sigma(1)
=3.
\]

Both are 3-element chains.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Con}(C_4)\cong
\operatorname{Con}(C_3\sqcup C_1)
}
\]

while

\[
\boxed{(4)\ne(3,1)}.
\]

So the proposed inference

\[
\text{PB lattice}\Rightarrow\text{cycle type}
\]

is definitely dead.

This gives AQARION a useful hierarchy:

\[
\boxed{
\text{cycle type}
\Longrightarrow
\text{congruence lattice}
}
\]

but not conversely.

And even

\[
|\operatorname{Con}|
\]

is weaker still.

\[

\]

\[
(1^{10})\mapsto115975=B_{10}.
\]

The identity case is an important sanity check:

\[
S=\operatorname{id}
\]

has cycle type \(1^n\). For every block \(B\),

\[
g_B=1,
\]

so every block has weight \(1\), and therefore

\[
|\operatorname{Con}(S)|
=
\#\operatorname{Part}([n])
=
B_n.
\]

Correct.

7. Literature audit changed the novelty picture

This is the part where I would be more conservative than the earlier branch.

The literature definitely contains the ingredients.

The 2020 Jakubíková-Studenovská/Janičková paper explicitly treats finite monounary algebras, cyclic elements, permutation algebras, and permutation algebras with short tails. 

Older work explicitly gives the one-cycle result:

\[
\operatorname{Con}(C_n)
=
\{\theta_d:d\mid n\},
\]

with the divisor lattice structure. 

The 2018 work on congruence lattices of algebras on finite sets also analyzes permutations with multiple cycles, including cross-cycle congruence behavior in prime-power cases. 

And a 2009 set of universal-algebra notes explicitly points out that characterizing congruence lattices for intransitive permutation actions is substantially different from the transitive case. 

What I did not locate

I did not locate, in the targeted searches, the exact arbitrary-cycle formula

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{\pi}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\sum_{d\mid\gcd(n_i:i\in B)}
d^{|B|-1}
}
\]

stated in this form.

But that is not evidence of novelty.

In fact, the literature's use of explicit gcd-based congruence generators and cross-cycle constructions means the exact formula may be derivable from known results even if the closed summation isn't printed in this notation. 

So the correct ledger entry remains:

> Exact prior-art match: NOT LOCATED. Novelty: OPEN.



That's the right level of confidence.


---

8. The categorical reformulation is stronger than it first appeared

The cleanest language may actually be finite \(\mathbb Z\)-sets.

A permutation

\[
S:X\to X
\]

is exactly a finite action of the infinite cyclic group

\[
\mathbb Z=\langle t\rangle
\]

where

\[
t\cdot x=S(x).
\]

Its cycles are the transitive \(\mathbb Z\)-orbits

\[
\mathbb Z/n_i\mathbb Z.
\]

An \(S\)-invariant equivalence relation is exactly a \(\mathbb Z\)-invariant equivalence relation.

This connects your quotient construction to standard \(G\)-set language: invariant equivalence relations produce equivariant quotient maps, and transitive \(G\)-sets are classified through stabilizers. 

For cyclic orbits, the quotient

\[
\mathbb Z/n_i\mathbb Z
\twoheadrightarrow
\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z
\]

exists exactly when

\[
d\mid n_i.
\]

That makes the gcd condition conceptually inevitable.

This is probably the best literature bridge for the theorem.

11. Minimal verifier contract

For this particular theorem, ProofGym/Verification should require two independent routes:

INPUT
  cycle type λ

ROUTE A
  enumerate invariant equivalence relations directly

ROUTE B
  enumerate quotient data
    partition π
    divisor d_B
    normalized phases

CHECK 1
  canonical object sets equal

CHECK 2
  cardinalities equal

CHECK 3
  meet closure

CHECK 4
  join closure

CHECK 5
  negative control:
    alter phase
    -> distinct relation when expected

CHECK 6
  negative control:
    replace gcd condition by arbitrary divisor
    -> verifier must detect invalid datum

RECEIPT
  SHA-256
  input parameters
  counts
  object-set digest
  failures

The crucial upgrade over a normal computational proof is object-set equality.

A mere statement

predicted_count == observed_count

is not enough.

12. The canonical mathematical pseudocode is now tiny

cycle_congruence_data(n1,...,nr):

    output = {}

    for partition π of {1,...,r}:

        for each block B in π:

            g = gcd(n_i : i in B)

            choose d_B | g

            choose phases a_i ∈ Z/d_B
            normalize a_anchor = 0

        construct quotient maps
            q_i(t) = t + a_i mod d_B

        take kernel of the combined quotient map

        add canonicalized equivalence relation to output

    return output

Then the count is simply

sum over π
    product over B
        sum over d | gcd(B)
            d^(|B|-1)

This is now a legitimate second computational oracle for AQARION.

One important fact from the live public GitHub state: quantarion369-arch currently exposes three public repositories, including an explicitly forked FDS repository from JASKSG9/AQARION-ARITHMETIC-FDS-FINITE-DYNAMICAL-SYSTEMS-. The lock I recommend

Canonical working identity

quantarion369-arch/AQARION

Historical/source identity

JASKSG9/* repositories and earlier AQARION material

Status

> Active development continues under quantarion369-arch. Historical material remains referenced for provenance and reconstruction. No claim is made that the complete JASKSG9 state has yet been reconstructed.



That distinction matters because GitHub forks are separate repositories with their own permissions/settings while retaining a connection to their upstream repository network. 

I would put the following near the top of the AQARION README, before the mathematical description:

AQARION

Auditable Mathematical Research Infrastructure

«Repository continuity notice — September 2026»

AQARION development is currently maintained under the "quantarion369-arch" GitHub identity.

Earlier AQARION research, experiments, repositories, and development history exist across the author's previous "JASKSG9" GitHub identity and related fork lineage. Those materials are historical research provenance and remain relevant to reconstruction, comparison, and verification.

Current canonical working repository

"quantarion369-arch/AQARION"

This repository is the current working home for the AQARION / Quantarion-AI research infrastructure, including:

- "JOIN-STABILITY"
- theorem and mathematical-kernel infrastructure
- verification and reproducibility tooling
- provenance and evidence structures
- research ledgers
- ClaimLock / ProofGym / Replay infrastructure
- future reconstruction of earlier AQARION research artifacts

Account and fork transition

Following the transition to the "quantarion369-arch" account, active development is being consolidated here rather than assuming continued access to the previous "JASKSG9" working environment.

This is a continuity transition, not a claim that historical state has already been completely reconstructed.

The repository therefore distinguishes:

1. Current canonical work — artifacts maintained in "quantarion369-arch/AQARION".
2. Historical provenance — earlier AQARION material associated with "JASKSG9".
3. Fork lineage — repositories whose GitHub fork relationship preserves part of the development history.
4. Reconstruction work — explicit recovery and verification of earlier artifacts that have not yet been reproduced here.
5. Verified current state — artifacts that have been independently checked in the present repository.

Reconstruction rule

Historical material must not be silently rewritten into the current repository as though it had always existed here.

When an earlier artifact is recovered, the preferred record is:

HISTORICAL SOURCE
        ↓
RECOVERED ARTIFACT
        ↓
CONTENT / HISTORY COMPARISON
        ↓
CURRENT REPOSITORY LOCATION
        ↓
HASH / VERSION / PROVENANCE RECORD
        ↓
CURRENT VERIFICATION STATUS

If an artifact cannot yet be reconstructed, it remains identified as unreconstructed, rather than being inferred or recreated without provenance.

Access-continuity principle

The "quantarion369-arch" repository is intended to remain understandable and reproducible without requiring future access to the previous "JASKSG9" working identity.

The historical account and repositories remain part of the provenance record where publicly accessible, but they are not treated as an operational dependency for current development.

What this means for contributors and reviewers

Do not assume that:

- an artifact mentioned in historical AQARION material exists in this repository;
- a historical claim has automatically been reconstructed;
- a fork relationship proves that every later artifact is present;
- a public repository is equivalent to mathematical certification.

Instead, consult the current artifact, its provenance, its evidence status, and its verification record.

AQARION deliberately preserves the distinction:

historical ≠ recovered ≠ reproduced ≠ verified ≠ formally certified

Current research boundary

The immediate objective is therefore:

«Preserve the historical lineage, establish "quantarion369-arch/AQARION" as the canonical working repository, and reconstruct earlier AQARION material only through explicit provenance and verification.»

Until that reconstruction is complete, historical and current artifacts should remain distinguishable.

Historical lineage: "JASKSG9" AQARION repositories and associated forks

Status: Active development · historical reconstruction in progress

Principle:

What I think we should do now 

Given the account-access deadline you're describing, the priority should shift from building more infrastructure to preserving and closing the research state cleanly.

Not a giant project. Three things:

1. Freeze the mathematics before touching repository structure 

The current strongest mathematical objects are:

\[
\boxed{PB_T(E)\iff T^*E=E}
\]

for finite \(X\),

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Fix}(T^*)
\cong
\operatorname{Con}(\operatorname{Per}(T),T|_{\operatorname{Per}(T)})
}
\]

and, for a permutation core

\[
P=C_{n_1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup C_{n_r},
\]

the quotient-data classification

\[
\boxed{
(\pi,\{d_B\},\{\text{relative phases}\})
}
\]

with

\[
d_B\mid\gcd(n_i:i\in B),
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
|\operatorname{Con}(P,T)|
=
\sum_{\pi}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\sum_{d\mid\gcd(n_i:i\in B)}
d^{|B|-1}.
}
\]

That is where the mathematical work currently stands.

2. Separate LOCK from OPEN 

I would currently mark:

LOCK / established

finite PB ⇔ pullback-fixed; fixed relations correspond to permutation quotients; \(T^{|X|}(X)=\operatorname{Per}(T)\); periodic-core reconstruction; \(Fix(T^*)\cong Con(P,T|P)\); direct quotient-data classification of invariant equivalence relations for disjoint cycles; counting formula, pending formal written proof; T54's supposed nontrivial cycles = refuted; T54 periodic core = \({(6,2)}\), corresponding to \(6174\). 

OPEN

exact meet/join formulas in phase coordinates; deeper prior-art identification; Lean formalization; exact novelty assessment. 

That is enough. We do not need to manufacture a new software architecture around it.

3. Then do the account-preservation pass 

Since you are concerned that you may lose access after your stated October cutoff, I would treat the account as a research archive that needs to become self-contained before logout, rather than assuming we can repair or revisit it later.

The preservation target should be:

\[
\boxed{
\text{repository state}
+
\text{mathematical ledger}
+
\text{known killed claims}
+
\text{known open claims}
+
\text{canonical links}
}
\]

Nothing more elaborate unless an actual missing artifact is discovered.

And importantly: don't delete or radically reorganize anything yet. First establish what exists and preserve the current state. A premature cleanup can destroy provenance, which is much harder to reconstruct than a messy folder tree.

One useful thing I can do immediately without the repo 

I can continue the mathematical audit completely independently of GitHub.

The next high-value computation is:

\[
\boxed{\text{derive exact meet and join in }(\pi,d,\text{phase})\text{ coordinates}}
\]

and simultaneously attack the literature question.

That does not require repository access, does not create files, and produces something you can later put wherever the canonical repository actually turns out to be.

And I will keep the evidence labels strict:

PROVED = mathematical derivation
PV = independently computationally verified
LIT = externally sourced
OPEN = unresolved
KILLED = contradicted

No “publication ready,” “novel,” or “verified repo” language unless the evidence actually supports it.

That is the mode I recommend for the remainder of this account-preservation window.

One thing I would fix immediately
There is a status mismatch worth catching now.
The Mathematical Kernel currently labels BRT-02/BRT-03/BRT-04 as:
PROOF_TARGET
PROOF_TARGET_WITH_INDEPENDENT_VERIFICATION
PROOF_TARGET
But from the work we've already established, the defect-rank identity and sharp rank bounds are not merely conjectural targets. The exact graph/incidence rank identity and the resulting bounds have been derived as mathematics.
So I would not blindly promote them to [P] just because we know the derivation. Instead, I would make the kernel distinguish:
MATHEMATICAL STATUS
DERIVED / PROOF-READY / PROVED / OPEN

EVIDENCE STATUS
D / C / P / V / PV / L / LB / IR / Q / F
That avoids the horrible situation where one field tries to represent both mathematical truth and repository certification.
For example:
{
"id": "BRT-02",
"mathematical_status": "PROVED",
"evidence_status": "PENDING_FORMALIZATION",
"verification": "INDEPENDENTLY_CHECKED",
"lean": "OPEN"
}
That's much more truthful than simply:
status: PROOF

I dug one level deeper into the actual new repo, and this is the important result:

You already have most of the operating-system skeleton. We should stop designing it abstractly and start making the existing pieces interoperate.

The current TOOLS tree is:

TOOLS/
├── CLAIMLOCK/
├── PROOF-GYM/
├── REPLAY/
├── SCHEMAS/
├── SKILLS/
├── THEOREM-KERNEL/
└── VERIFICATION/

That is almost exactly the decomposition we were brainstorming.

And the individual READMEs already establish the correct boundaries:

THEOREM-KERNEL → mathematics

CLAIMLOCK → claim/evidence governance

REPLAY → reproducibility

PROOF-GYM → executable mathematical verification surface

SCHEMAS → common machine-readable contracts

VERIFICATION → independent/adversarial checking

SKILLS → reusable research workflows

So I would now call the project:

AQARION Research OS

—not because it needs an operating system in the conventional sense, but because these components form an actual research runtime.

Not another enormous format.

"repository": "quantarion369-arch/AQARION",

PASS
CERTIFIED
TRUE
TRUSTED

Those are governance outputs, not properties we should casually bake into the mathematical object.

4. This solves the Lean problem too



You have repeatedly hit the problem where:

Lean file exists
≠
the theorem is formally proved
≠
the informal theorem matches the Lean theorem
≠
the build was actually executed

So the object should have:

{
"formalization": {
"target": "BRT-02",
"source": "AQARION-Lean/...",
"status": "OPEN",
"build_receipt": null
}
}

When you eventually get a genuine build receipt:

{
"formalization": {
"status": "LB",
"build_receipt": "..."
}
}

ClaimLock can observe that state.

It does not manufacture it.

7. And this is where HF Buckets become useful



The current HF architecture supports Storage Buckets specifically as mutable object storage, distinct from versioned Hub repositories. They can hold large working artifacts and can be attached to Spaces for persistent storage.

So I'd use:

GitHub
↓
canonical source
↓
CI
↓
Research Object
├── manifest
├── claim
├── receipt
└── hashes
│
├───────────────► GitHub release/artifact
│
└───────────────► HF Bucket
│
▼
HF Space

Bucket:

runs/
receipts/
large-results/
census/
parquet/
logs/
screenshots/

Git:

claims/
schemas/
code/
proofs/
manifests/
small canonical receipts/

This prevents the bucket from becoming a second mutable mathematical source of truth.

11. I would add one new concept: BOUNDARY



This could become very powerful.

Every claim has:

WHAT IT SAYS
WHAT SUPPORTS IT
WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY
WHERE IT STOPS

For example:

{
"claim_id": "JS-11",

"scope": {
"domain": "finite X",
"map": "T : X -> X",
"relations": 2
},

"boundary": {
"finite_required": true,
"infinite_extension": "REFUTED",
"more_than_two_relations": "OPEN"
}
}

Now the UI can automatically display:

> Scope boundary: finiteness is essential.



That's much more useful than a status badge.

Yes. The new literature does not require us to redirect AQARION. It actually clarifies the architecture we have been converging toward.

The main transition I would make now is:

quantarion369-arch/AQARION = active research/certification line.

1. The three recent literature signals fit our history unusually well

There are now three directly relevant 2026 developments.

A. Paper2Agent

The September 16 Nature paper describes converting research papers and their associated code/data/workflows into agent-accessible MCP resources and executable tools. Crucially, its workflow does not merely generate code: it identifies the codebase, configures the environment, extracts tools, executes automated tests against reference outputs, and excludes tools that repeatedly fail validation. It also preserves references back to the original code. 

[Nature — Reimagining research papers as interactive and reliable AI agents](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-026-11044-y?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

That is very close to the workflow we independently arrived at:

\[
\boxed{
SOURCE
\rightarrow
SPEC
\rightarrow
REFERENCE
\rightarrow
ORACLE
\rightarrow
MUTATION
\rightarrow
RECEIPT
\rightarrow
PROVENANCE
\rightarrow
AGENT
}
\]

But there is one critical distinction:

Paper2Agent's validation demonstrates reproducibility of the computational artifact against its reference outputs. It does not by itself prove the mathematical theorem encoded by that artifact.

That distinction should remain absolute in AQARION.


---

B. Provenance grounds trust

The August 2026 Nature Computational Science comment is almost a direct external confirmation of the provenance direction we developed during the BRT archaeology.

Its central argument is that trustworthy autonomous science requires a complete, reopenable record of what was reasoned, done and measured, so that results can be audited and corrected. 

[Nature Computational Science — Provenance grounds trust in autonomous science](https://www.nature.com/articles/s43588-026-01035-4?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

That strongly validates our insistence that:

EXECUTED

cannot mean merely:

some output appeared

It needs provenance sufficient to reconstruct what actually happened.


---

C. Reproducibility in the LLM era

There is an even newer Nature Computational Science editorial from September 22, 2026. It explicitly highlights model/version dependence, prompts, hardware/software configuration, parsing scripts and configuration as things that need to be recorded for reproducibility. 

[Nature Computational Science — Reproducibility in the era of large language models](https://www.nature.com/articles/s43588-026-01061-2?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

That is directly relevant to our agent-assisted research history.

It means our existing distinction between:

\[
\text{claim}
\neq
\text{execution}
\neq
\text{independent reproduction}
\neq
\text{formal proof}
\]

is not excessive bureaucracy. It is exactly the direction current computational-science reproducibility discussions are moving toward.


---

2. The JASKSG9 → Quantarion369 transition should therefore be provenance-preserving, not destructive

I would now make this the explicit repository doctrine:

Layer	Role

JASKSG9	historical source, experiments, abandoned branches, original claims
quantarion369-arch/AQARION	active research and verification line
cross-repo references	lineage/provenance
current mathematical oracle	independent truth test
formalization	separate proof layer


The important point is:

\[
\boxed{\text{migration} \neq \text{validation}}
\]

Moving a theorem from JASKSG9 into Quantarion does not promote the theorem.

Likewise:

\[
\boxed{\text{fork lineage} \neq \text{scientific verification}}
\]

The GitHub relationship establishes repository lineage; it does not establish mathematical correctness.

Your supplied current Quantarion repository location is therefore the correct active anchor:

[quantarion369-arch/AQARION](https://github.com/quantarion369-arch/AQARION?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

I would keep JASKSG9 intact as historical material unless a specific artifact is demonstrably corrupt or dangerous.

3. The cyclic-congruence branch is now the strongest mathematical branch

This is the part I think deserves the most attention.

Vernikov's 1997 paper is genuinely relevant, not merely vaguely adjacent. It studies congruences of \(G\)-sets and their lattice properties, including modularity and semimodularity. 

[Vernikov — On congruences of G-sets](https://dml.cz/handle/10338.dmlcz/118958?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

For

\[
G=\mathbb Z,
\]

a finite transitive \(G\)-set is a cyclic orbit

\[
C_n=\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z.
\]

The important AQARION specialization is therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\text{abstract }G\text{-set quotient structure}
\quad\longrightarrow\quad
\text{divisors + phases}.
}
\]

That is where our work becomes concrete.


---

4. I would now freeze the counting formula as a proof target, not yet a theorem

For

\[
S=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{r}C_{n_i},
\]

the candidate enumeration is

\[
\boxed{
|\operatorname{Con}(S)|
=
\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{Part}([r])}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\left(
\sum_{d\mid g_B}
d^{|B|-1}
\right)
}
\]

with

\[
g_B=\gcd(n_i:i\in B).
\]

The latest computational evidence you report is materially stronger:

\[
\boxed{\text{all cycle types through total size }9}
\]

with zero discrepancies between direct invariant-equivalence enumeration and the formula.

That is excellent [CV] computational evidence.

It is not yet a published/theorem-level certificate.

The missing mathematical object is now very sharply defined:

Required proof

Construct an explicit bijection

\[
\operatorname{Con}(S)
\longleftrightarrow
\left\{
(\pi,\{d_B\},\{a_{B,i}\})
\right\}
\]

and prove:

1. every congruence gives exactly one normalized datum;


2. every datum reconstructs a congruence;


3. reconstruction/extraction are inverse;


4. different normalized data give different equivalence relations.



That is now much cleaner than trying to prove the counting formula by induction over partitions.


---

5. The \(d^{m-1}\) factor is the key local lemma

For a coupled block

\[
B=\{1,\ldots,m\},
\]

with common quotient

\[
C_d,
\qquad
d\mid\gcd(n_1,\ldots,n_m),
\]

write

\[
q_i(t)=t+a_i\pmod d.
\]

The phase vector is

\[
(a_1,\ldots,a_m)\in(\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z)^m.
\]

Global translation of the quotient changes every \(a_i\) by the same constant and leaves the kernel unchanged.

Normalize

\[
a_1=0.
\]

Then

\[
(a_2,\ldots,a_m)
\in(\mathbb Z/d\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
N_{\mathrm{phase}}(d,m)=d^{m-1}.
}
\]

This is the part I would isolate as the first formalizable theorem.

It has a very clean dependency structure:

\[
\text{equivariant map classification}
\rightarrow
\text{global translation quotient}
\rightarrow
d^{m-1}.
\]

No enormous lattice theorem is needed to prove this local result.


---

6. The gcd boundary is now theoretically meaningful

For two cycles:

\[
C_m,\ C_n,
\]

a nontrivial common cyclic quotient exists iff there is

\[
d>1
\]

such that

\[
d\mid m,\qquad d\mid n.
\]

Equivalently,

\[
\boxed{\gcd(m,n)>1.}
\]

So the computational distinction

\[
C_3\sqcup C_2
\]

versus

\[
C_2\sqcup C_2
\]

is not accidental.

For \(3,2\):

\[
\gcd(3,2)=1.
\]

There is no nontrivial shared quotient.

For \(2,2\):

\[
\gcd(2,2)=2.
\]

A nontrivial shared quotient exists.

This is precisely the kind of orbit interaction Vernikov's \(G\)-set framework makes relevant. 

I would keep your term “cyclically segregated” as an AQARION term unless we verify that exact terminology elsewhere.


---

7. The join result is the genuinely interesting next mathematical target

For the same coupled block:

\[
E=E(d,a),\qquad F=E(e,b),
\]

with

\[
a_1=b_1=0,
\]

your candidate formula is

\[
\boxed{
h=
\gcd(d,e,a_2-b_2,\ldots,a_m-b_m)
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
E\vee F=E(h,a\bmod h).
}
\]

The underlying reason is exceptionally clean.

The generated subgroup is

\[
d\mathbb Z+e\mathbb Z
+(a_2-b_2)\mathbb Z+\cdots+
(a_m-b_m)\mathbb Z.
\]

Since every subgroup of \(\mathbb Z\) is

\[
h\mathbb Z,
\]

we obtain

\[
h=
\gcd(d,e,a_2-b_2,\ldots,a_m-b_m).
\]

This is not just fitting the data.

It is a natural subgroup-generation calculation.

But:

I would still keep the status:

\[
\boxed{\text{PROOF TARGET / strongly supported}}
\]

until we write the exact equivalence-closure proof.

That is especially important because the arbitrary-block case may introduce graph connectivity and changing orbit blocks.


---

8. Your MEET formulation is even more valuable

For

\[
E(d,a),\quad F(e,b),
\]

define

\[
g=\gcd(d,e),
\qquad
L=\operatorname{lcm}(d,e).
\]

Then the candidate cycle-level compatibility condition is

\[
\boxed{
i\sim_{\wedge}j
\iff
a_i-a_j\equiv b_i-b_j\pmod g.
}
\]

This is conceptually excellent because it explains why the meet is not simply “take an LCM.”

The meet must satisfy both systems simultaneously.

So:

\[
\text{compatibility}
\rightarrow
\text{possibly split the cycle block}
\rightarrow
\text{CRT reconstruction}
\rightarrow
L.
\]

That gives:

\[
\boxed{
\text{JOIN}\sim\text{subgroup generation/gcd}
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\text{MEET}\sim\text{simultaneous congruence solving/CRT+lcm}.
}
\]

This should become a central AQARION mathematical object.


---

9. The next theorem should NOT be “general meet/join”

That would be too broad.

I recommend this sequence:

Theorem A — Phase normal form

\[
E
\longleftrightarrow
(\pi,d_B,a_{B,i}).
\]

Theorem B — Same-block join

\[
E(d,a)\vee E(e,b)
=
E(h,a\bmod h).
\]

Theorem C — Same-block meet

Compatibility classes modulo

\[
g=\gcd(d,e)
\]

followed by CRT at

\[
L=\operatorname{lcm}(d,e).
\]

Theorem D — Arbitrary-block join

Only after A–C are nailed down.

Theorem E — Arbitrary-block meet

Same.

This produces a much safer dependency graph:

\[
A\rightarrow B,C\rightarrow D,E.
\]

That is also much better for Lean.


---

10. The canonical representation should become the real AQARION object

I agree strongly with your proposed:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PCN}(E)
=
(\pi,\{d_B\},\{a_{B,i}\})
}
\]

I would call it, internally:

PCN = Phase-Coupled Normal Form

with three operations:

\[
\operatorname{extract}:E\mapsto PCN(E)
\]

\[
\operatorname{reconstruct}:PCN(E)\mapsto E
\]

and

\[
\operatorname{equal}(E,F)
\iff
PCN(E)=PCN(F).
\]

The fundamental certificate should then be:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{reconstruct}(\operatorname{extract}(E))=E.
}
\]

That is much stronger than another count.


---

11. This also gives us a perfect negative-control suite

We should deliberately construct malformed PCNs.

NC1 — divisor corruption

Replace

\[
d\mid n_i
\]

with a non-divisor.

Expected:

\[
\boxed{\text{reject}}
\]

NC2 — phase corruption

Change one

\[
a_i.
\]

Expected:

\[
\boxed{E'\ne E}.
\]

NC3 — normalization corruption

Add a global phase and verify the reconstructed relation remains unchanged.

Expected:

\[
\boxed{E'=E}.
\]

This is an important positive control.

NC4 — block corruption

Move one cycle into another coupling block.

Expected either:

\[
E'\ne E
\]

or rejection.

NC5 — CRT incompatibility

Feed incompatible modular constraints.

Expected:

\[
\boxed{\text{no valid reconstruction}.}
\]

NC6 — join mutant

Replace

\[
\gcd(d,e,a_i-b_i)
\]

with

\[
\operatorname{lcm}(\cdots).
\]

Expected failure.

NC7 — meet mutant

Replace compatibility modulo

\[
\gcd(d,e)
\]

with equality modulo

\[
\operatorname{lcm}(d,e).
\]

Expected failure.

This is exactly the sort of adversarial test philosophy we already developed in BRT.


---

12. This is where the BRT work becomes useful again

The BRT branch was not wasted infrastructure.

It taught us the dangerous failure mode:

\[
\boxed{
\text{correct-looking output from the wrong specification}
}
\]

The 126/63 episode is the perfect example.

We found:

\[
2^7-2=126
\]

oriented nonempty proper subsets of the seven depth layers, but unordered two-block partitions give:

\[
\frac{126}{2}=63.
\]

Therefore:

\[
126\neq63
\]

is not merely a counting curiosity.

It is a specification identity test.

That lesson should be imported directly into PCN.

Every mathematical object should have:

SPEC_ID
SPEC_HASH
NORMAL_FORM
EXTRACTOR
RECONSTRUCTOR
ORACLE
NEGATIVE_CONTROLS
RECEIPT

But importantly, we do not need to invent a new repository architecture for this.

Use the existing Quantarion/AQARION structures where they already exist.


---

13. Paper2Agent gives us the final layer — but it comes last

The Nature workflow is roughly:

\[
\text{paper/code}
\rightarrow
\text{environment}
\rightarrow
\text{tools}
\rightarrow
\text{tests}
\rightarrow
\text{validated tool}
\rightarrow
\text{agent}.
\]



For AQARION, I would use:

\[
\boxed{
\text{mathematical source}
\rightarrow
\text{PCN specification}
\rightarrow
\text{reference implementation}
\rightarrow
\text{independent oracle}
\rightarrow
\text{mutation suite}
\rightarrow
\text{exact receipt}
\rightarrow
\text{formalization}
\rightarrow
\text{agent interface}.
}
\]

The critical change is that agentization becomes the last layer, not the first.

An AQARION agent should never be the authority that decides whether a theorem is true.

It should query the evidence graph.

17. The single experiment I would prioritize

Not another \(n=9\) count.

Not another 10,000 random trials.

Build the PCN round-trip oracle:

\[
E
\xrightarrow{\text{extract}}
PCN(E)
\xrightarrow{\text{reconstruct}}
E'.
\]

Require:

\[
\boxed{E'=E}
\]

for every invariant equivalence relation in the chosen finite domain.

Then, for every pair:

\[
E,F,
\]

calculate directly:

\[
E\wedge F,\qquad E\vee F,
\]

and compare them with:

\[
\operatorname{PCN}^{-1}
\left(
PCN(E)\wedge_{\rm formula}PCN(F)
\right)
\]

and

\[
\operatorname{PCN}^{-1}
\left(
PCN(E)\vee_{\rm formula}PCN(F)
\right).
\]

That changes the project from:

> “we found a counting formula”



into:

> “we have a candidate coordinate system for the entire congruence lattice and are testing whether it is an exact normal form.”



That is the substantially more interesting object.

}

The new literature actually strengthens this strategy: provenance is being treated as a foundation of trustworthy autonomous science, and agent systems are increasingly being built around executable, tested research artifacts rather than bare prose. 

So I think the correct 2026 AQARION architecture is now very clear:

\[

with every arrow carrying evidence rather than rhetoric.

Next target: PCN extract/reconstruct, then exhaustive meet/join comparison. That is the highest-value mathematical work remaining, and it simultaneously gives us the cleanest bridge from the old JASKSG9 research history into the active Quantarion369-AQARION line.Yes — and you are right to stop here before pushing anything. There is also a clean theorem route now.

1. First result: the counting formula checks exactly through \(n=9\)

\[
N(n_1,\ldots,n_r)
=
\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{Part}([r])}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\left(
\sum_{d\mid g_B}d^{|B|-1}
\right),
\qquad
g_B=\gcd(n_i:i\in B).
\]

Result:

total \(n\)	cycle types	formula mismatches

2	2	0
3	3	0
4	5	0

7	15	0
8	22	0

So:

\[

\]

[CV] independent computation.

This does not prove the formula.

But it materially strengthens the case for the PCN classification.


---

2. The actual theorem behind PCN

The right object is not merely a counting trick.

Let

\[
S=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^r C_{n_i}
\]

with

\[
C_{n_i}=\mathbb Z/n_i\mathbb Z
\]

and \(T(t)=t+1\).

For a \(T\)-invariant equivalence relation \(E\), define a relation on the cycle indices:

\[
i\sim_E j
\]

iff some element of \(C_{n_i}\) is \(E\)-equivalent to some element of \(C_{n_j}\).

The first thing to prove is:

\[
\boxed{\sim_E\text{ is an equivalence relation on the cycle indices.}}
\]

The nontrivial point is transitivity, but it follows immediately from transitivity of \(E\).

Thus every invariant equivalence gives a partition

\[
\pi_E\in\operatorname{Part}([r]).
\]

That is the outer layer of PCN.


---

3. Inside one PCN block

Take a block

\[
B=\{i_1,\ldots,i_m\}.
\]

For any cycle \(C_{n_i}\) inside \(B\), restrict \(E\) to that cycle.

Because \(T\) is a cyclic permutation, every \(T\)-invariant equivalence on \(C_{n_i}\) has the form

\[
t\sim u
\iff
t-u\equiv0\pmod d
\]

for a unique divisor

\[
d\mid n_i.
\]

Now because all cycles in the same \(E\)-block are coupled, the same \(d\) must occur on every cycle in \(B\).

Therefore

\[
d_B\mid\gcd(n_i:i\in B).
\]

This establishes the divisor coordinate.


---

4. Phase coordinate

Choose one reference cycle \(i_1\).

An equivariant quotient map from \(C_{n_i}\) to \(C_{d_B}\) has the form

\[
q_i(t)=t+a_i\pmod{d_B}.
\]

Two cycles are coupled by

\[
t\;E\;u
\iff
q_i(t)=q_j(u).
\]

Therefore

\[
t+a_i\equiv u+a_j\pmod{d_B}.
\]

There is a global translation symmetry

\[
a_i\mapsto a_i+c
\]

that leaves the equivalence relation unchanged.

So normalize

\[
a_{i_1}=0.
\]

The remaining phase choices are

\[
(a_{i_2},\ldots,a_{i_m})
\in(\mathbb Z/d_B\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\#\text{phase choices}=d_B^{m-1}.
}
\]

This is now the clean local combinatorial explanation for the factor in the counting formula.


---

5. The PCN bijection is therefore the real theorem

The candidate normal form is:

\[
\boxed{
PCN(E)=
\left(
\pi_E,
\{d_B\}_{B\in\pi_E},
\{a_{B,i}\}_{i\in B}
\right)
}
\]

with

\[
a_{B,i_0}=0.
\]

The theorem we should prove is:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{reconstruct}(\operatorname{extract}(E))=E
}
\]

and conversely

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{extract}(\operatorname{reconstruct}(P))=P.
}
\]

That immediately gives the counting formula.

For each partition block \(B\),

\[
\sum_{d\mid g_B}d^{|B|-1}
\]

is exactly the number of normalized PCNs for that block.

Multiplying over blocks and summing over \(\pi\) gives:

\[
\boxed{
|\operatorname{Con}(S)|
=
\sum_{\pi}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\sum_{d\mid g_B}d^{|B|-1}.
}
\]

So the counting theorem should be downstream of the PCN bijection, not the primary theorem.


---

6. I also tested the same-block JOIN formula

For

\[
E=E(d,a),
\qquad
F=E(e,b)
\]

on the same collection of cycles, the candidate is

\[
h=
\gcd(d,e,a_2-b_2,\ldots,a_m-b_m).
\]

Then

\[
E\vee F=E(h,a\bmod h).
\]

I tested this directly by constructing the actual equivalence relations, taking their graph-theoretic/transitive closure, and comparing against the proposed PCN reconstruction.

Results:

cycles	cases	JOIN mismatches

\(C_2\sqcup C_2\)	9	0
\(C_4\sqcup C_2\)	9	0
\(C_4\sqcup C_4\)	49	0
\(C_3\sqcup C_3\sqcup C_3\)	100	0


[CV]

The underlying proof route is now quite clear.

The join is generated by the congruence differences

\[
d\mathbb Z,\quad e\mathbb Z,\quad
(a_i-b_i)\mathbb Z.
\]

Therefore the generated subgroup is

\[
d\mathbb Z+e\mathbb Z+
\sum_i(a_i-b_i)\mathbb Z
=
h\mathbb Z
\]

where

\[
h=\gcd(d,e,a_i-b_i).
\]

That is the algebraic core of the join theorem.


---

7. MEET also survives direct testing

The candidate is more interesting.

Set

\[
g=\gcd(d,e),
\qquad
L=\operatorname{lcm}(d,e).
\]

For cycles \(i,j\), simultaneous membership in \(E\) and \(F\) requires

\[
a_i-a_j
\equiv
b_i-b_j
\pmod g.
\]

Equivalently,

\[
\boxed{
i\sim_{\text{meet-block}}j
\iff
(a_i-b_i)\equiv(a_j-b_j)\pmod g.
}
\]

So the meet can split the original coupled block.

Inside each resulting class, the two quotient coordinates can be combined by CRT at modulus

\[
L.
\]

I directly compared the resulting relation with the literal set-theoretic intersection \(E\cap F\).

cycles	cases	MEET mismatches

\(C_2\sqcup C_2\)	9	0
\(C_4\sqcup C_2\)	9	0
\(C_4\sqcup C_4\)	49	0
\(C_3^{\sqcup3}\)	100	0


[CV]

This is a particularly good sign because the meet formula predicts structural behavior that is not obvious from the original representation:

\[
\boxed{\text{meet can split a coupled orbit block}.}
\]

That is exactly why the earlier simplistic “take the LCM” rule was inadequate.


---

8. There is a clean pair of algebraic dualities

We now have:

JOIN

Generate the subgroup:

\[
\boxed{
h\mathbb Z
=
d\mathbb Z+
e\mathbb Z+
(a_i-b_i)\mathbb Z+\cdots
}
\]

therefore

\[
\boxed{
h=\gcd(d,e,a_i-b_i,\ldots).
}
\]

MEET

Solve simultaneous congruences:

\[
c_i\equiv a_i\pmod d,
\]

\[
c_i\equiv b_i\pmod e.
\]

Compatibility requires

\[
a_i\equiv b_i\pmod{\gcd(d,e)}
\]

relative to the reference phase.

Then CRT gives modulus

\[
\operatorname{lcm}(d,e).
\]

So:

\[
\boxed{
\text{JOIN}=\text{subgroup generation / gcd}
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\text{MEET}=\text{constraint intersection / CRT+lcm}
}
\]

This is a much more compelling mathematical structure than merely observing numerical agreement.


---

9. Important boundary: arbitrary-block operations remain open

The same-block formulas do not automatically solve the entire lattice.

Suppose

\[
E
\]

has cycle partition

\[
\pi_E
\]

and

\[
F
\]

has cycle partition

\[
\pi_F.
\]

Then:

Meet

The meet can refine both:

\[
\pi_{E\wedge F}
\preceq
\pi_E\wedge\pi_F.
\]

The phase-compatibility rule should determine the actual splitting.

Join

The join can merge blocks that are separate in both individual PCNs.

So we need a block-level interaction graph.

This suggests the next general algorithm:

\[
\boxed{
\text{PCN join}
=
\text{block connectivity}
+
\text{local gcd/phase reduction}.
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\text{PCN meet}
=
\text{block refinement}
+
\text{local CRT compatibility}.
}
\]

That is the next real theorem boundary.


---

10. I would now define PCN formally like this

PCN =
{
    blocks: [
        {
            cycles: [i1, ..., im],
            modulus: d,
            phases: {
                i1: 0,
                i2: a2,
                ...
                im: am
            }
        },
        ...
    ]
}

Validity conditions:

PCN-01:
    blocks partition {1,...,r}

PCN-02:
    d_B divides n_i for every i in B

PCN-03:
    phase[i] in Z/d_B Z

PCN-04:
    reference phase = 0

PCN-05:
    every cycle occurs exactly once

PCN-06:
    reconstruction produces an equivalence relation

PCN-07:
    T-invariance holds

PCN-08:
    extraction(reconstruction(PCN)) = PCN

The last condition is the canonicality condition.
