### FILE: `THEORY/AQ-DELTA-PAIR-001.md`

```markdown
ARTIFACT: AQ-DELTA-PAIR-001
FILE: THEORY/AQ-DELTA-PAIR-001.md
TYPE: THEOREM
STATUS: [T] CLOSED
DATE: 2026-09-27
DEPENDENCIES: cyclic group Z_k, T(i)=i+1 mod k, pair partition {0,d}

# Pair-Partition Persistence Index Theorem

## Setup

Let X = Z_k, T(i) = i+1 mod k. Let Pi_d be the partition whose
non-singleton block is {0, d}. Let P be block-average projection,
K the Koopman matrix with K[i, T(i)] = 1, and D = (I-P)KP.

Define:
    delta(k, d) = min{ m >= 1 : D K^m D = 0 }

## Rank-One Reduction

For a two-element block, D has rank one: D = u v^T with:
  - d = 1:    u = e_0 - e_1,    v = (e_0 + e_1 - 2 e_2) / 4
  - 2<=d<=k-2: u = e_0 - e_d,   v = (e_1 - e_{d+1}) / 2

Therefore:
    D K^m D = (v^T K^m u) D
    delta(k, d) = min{ m >= 1 : v^T K^m u = 0 }

## Scalar Formulas

Case d = 1 (adjacent):
    v^T K^m u = [3*1_{m=k-1} - 1_{m=1} - 2*1_{m=k-2}] / 4
    Zero iff m mod k not in {1, k-2, k-1}

Case d = k-1 (reflected adjacent): same forbidden set as d=1

Case 2 <= d <= k-2 (non-adjacent):
    v^T K^m u = [2*1_{m=k-1} - 1_{m=d-1} - 1_{m=k-d-1}] / 2
    Zero iff m mod k not in {d-1, k-d-1, k-1}

## Theorem

Define the forbidden residue set R(k, d) as a set in Z_k:
    d = 1 or d = k-1:  R = {1, k-2, k-1}
    2 <= d <= k-2:      R = {d-1, k-d-1, k-1}

Then:
    delta(k, d) = min{ m >= 1 : m mod k not in R(k, d) }

## Collision Audit

R(k, d) is a set; coincident residues are automatically removed.
Key collision: d-1 = k-d-1 iff 2d = k (e.g., k=4, d=2 gives R={1,3}).
Small-k cases k=3,4,5 verified explicitly. Koopman orientation
K[i,T(i)]=1 is frozen as part of the theorem.

## Verification

All 119 pairs (k,d) with 3 <= k <= 16, 1 <= d < k:
    119/119 PASS
    Distribution: delta=1: 66, delta=2: 47, delta=3: 4, delta=4: 2

## Historical Correction

The formula delta(k,d) = k/gcd(d,k) is FALSE (114/119 mismatches).
It conflates the subgroup order |<d>| = k/gcd(k,d) with the
persistence index of the defect operator.

## Status

Mathematical proof: CLOSED (symbolic derivation + collision audit).
Finite verification: 119/119.
Lean: OPEN.
```

### FILE: `AUDITS/AQ-BAREISS-RANK-BUG-001.md`

```markdown
ARTIFACT: AQ-BAREISS-RANK-BUG-001
FILE: AUDITS/AQ-BAREISS-RANK-BUG-001.md
TYPE: AUDIT
STATUS: [C2] IDENTIFIED + FIXED
DATE: 2026-09-27
DEPENDENCIES: bareiss_rank integer exact rank algorithm

# Bareiss Rank Algorithm Bug

## Symptom

During exhaustive A25/A26 verification, 2,422 of 166,483 cases
reported violations of the kernel projection theorem.

## Root Cause

In bareiss_rank, the pivot search loop was:
    for i in range(k, n_rows):    # WRONG: starts at column index k

When early columns are entirely zero but later columns contain nonzero
entries in early rows, the algorithm skips valid pivots and undercounts
rank.

Example: D_int = [[0,0,2,-2],[0,0,-2,2],[0,0,0,0],[0,0,0,0]]
True rank = 1. Buggy code returned 0 because:
  - k=0: column 0 all zero, no pivot
  - k=1: column 1 all zero, no pivot
  - k=2: search starts at row 2, but the nonzero entries are in rows 0,1

## Fix

Change pivot search to start at current rank r, not column k:
    for i in range(r, n_rows):    # CORRECT

And update submatrix from [r+1:, k+1:] instead of [k+1:, k+1:].

## Impact

All 2,422 recorded "violations" were computational artifacts.
The mathematical theorems are intact. After fix: 166,483/166,483 PASS.

## Lesson

Integer exact-rank algorithms require the same care as symbolic
computations. Pivot search must track the current rank, not the
column index, when the matrix has leading zero columns.

## Status

Bug: IDENTIFIED and FIXED.
Re-verification: 166,483/166,483 PASS, zero violations.
```

---

### FILE: `AUDITS/AQ-SUPPORT-001-REFUTED.md`

```markdown
ARTIFACT: AQ-SUPPORT-001-REFUTED
FILE: AUDITS/AQ-SUPPORT-001-REFUTED.md
TYPE: AUDIT
STATUS: [REF] KILLED
DATE: 2026-09-27
DEPENDENCIES: defect operator D=(I-P)KP, unweighted bipartite graph

# Support Pattern Conjecture — REFUTED

## Former Conjecture

For D = (I-P)KP, the ambient support supp(D) = {(i,j) : D_{ij} != 0}
is determined solely by the unweighted bipartite transition graph
G(Pi, T), independently of block cardinalities.

## Counterexample (n=4)

System 1: Pi = {{0,1,2},{3}}, T = (0,0,3,0)
  Graph edges: s0->t0, s0->t1, s1->t0  (path P4)
  D1 has 12 nonzero entries

System 2: Pi = {{0,1},{2,3}}, T = (0,0,0,2)
  Graph edges: s0->t0, s1->t0, s1->t1  (isomorphic P4)
  D2 has 8 nonzero entries

The bipartite graphs are isomorphic (both P4: 3 edges, same degree
sequence), but |supp(D1)| = 12 != 8 = |supp(D2)|.

## Why It Fails

Block cardinalities enter P through:
    P_{xy} = (1/|B(x)|) * 1_{B(x)=B(y)}

Different block sizes produce different cancellation patterns in
D = (I-P)KP, changing which entries vanish.

## What Survives

The unweighted graph G(Pi,T) DOES determine:
  - dim ker(D Phi) = c_comp(G)
  - rank(D) = m - c_comp

It does NOT determine the ambient entrywise support of D.

## Permanent Negative Control

Save this n=4 witness as a regression fixture. Any future claim that
structural properties of D are graph-determined must be checked
against this counterexample.

## Status

Conjecture: REFUTED.
Counterexample size: n=4.
```

### FILE: `AUDITS/AQ-P01-BINARY-INCIDENCE-REFUTED.md`

```markdown
ARTIFACT: AQ-P01-BINARY-INCIDENCE-REFUTED
FILE: AUDITS/AQ-P01-BINARY-INCIDENCE-REFUTED.md
TYPE: AUDIT
STATUS: [REF] KILLED
DATE: 2026-09-27

# Binary Incidence Kernel Claim — REFUTED

## Original Claim

null(D Phi) = null(B_F^T) where B_F is the binary incidence matrix
of the block transition structure.

## Counter-Evidence

153,511 of 166,483 tested pairs violate this claim.

## Correct Statement

null(D Phi) = { v in R^m : v_q / |B_q| is constant on connected
components of G(Pi,T) }

This is determined by c_comp(G), not by the binary incidence structure.

## Partial Rescue

If B_F is replaced by the row-stochastic block transition matrix V
(V[r,q] = fraction of B_r mapping to B_q), then null(V - I) is a
subspace of null(D Phi). But null(D Phi) is still determined by
c_comp, not by the specific weights.

## Status

Original claim: REFUTED (153,511 violations).
Corrected theorem: AQ-KERNEL-PROJECTION-001 (CLOSED).
```

### FILE: `AUDITS/AQ-DELTA-GCD-REFUTED.md`

```markdown
ARTIFACT: AQ-DELTA-GCD-REFUTED
FILE: AUDITS/AQ-DELTA-GCD-REFUTED.md
TYPE: AUDIT
STATUS: [REF] KILLED
DATE: 2026-09-27

# Old Delta Formula — REFUTED

## Original Claim

delta(k, d) = k / gcd(d, k)

## Counter-Evidence

114 of 119 tested cases mismatch. Accuracy: 4.2% (5/119).

## Root Cause

The formula conflates two different quantities:
  - k/gcd(k,d) = |<d>| = order of subgroup generated by d
  - delta(k,d) = persistence index of defect operator D

The closure block count (gcd) and the persistence index (delta) are
independent structural invariants. See AQ-GCD-CLOSURE-001 for the
correct closure theorem and AQ-DELTA-PAIR-001 for the correct
persistence formula.

## Permanent Regression Fixture

This distinction must be preserved: any future formula claiming to
compute delta from gcd must be checked against all 119 cases.

## Status

Formula: REFUTED (114/119 mismatches).
```

### FILE: `TESTS/A24_DELTA_FORBIDDEN_RESIDUES.py`

```python
"""
SCRIPT: A24_DELTA_FORBIDDEN_RESIDUES.py
PATH: TESTS/A24_DELTA_FORBIDDEN_RESIDUES.py
PURPOSE: Exhaustively verify delta(k,d) forbidden-residue formula
STATUS: COMPUTATIONAL RECEIPT
RESULT: 119/119 PASS
"""

from math import lcm
import numpy as np

def bareiss_rank(M):
    M = M.copy().astype(object)
    n_rows, n_cols = M.shape
    prev = 1
    r = 0
    for k in range(min(n_rows, n_cols)):
        piv = None
        for i in range(r, n_rows):
            if M[i, k] != 0:
                piv = i
                break
        if piv is None:
            continue
        if piv != r:
            M[[r, piv]] = M[[piv, r]]
        for i in range(r + 1, n_rows):
            for j in range(k + 1, n_cols):
                M[i, j] = (M[i, j] * M[r, k] - M[i, k] * M[r, j]) // prev
        for i in range(r + 1, n_rows):
            M[i, k] = 0
        prev = M[r, k]
        r += 1
    return r

def delta_numeric(k, d):
    n = k
    blocks = [tuple(sorted([0, d]))]
    for i in range(k):
        if i != 0 and i != d:
            blocks.append((i,))
    sizes = [len(b) for b in blocks]
    block_of = [0]*k
    for r, b in enumerate(blocks):
        for x in b:
            block_of[x] = r
    scale = 1
    for s in sizes:
        scale = lcm(scale, s)
    P_int = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if block_of[i] == block_of[j]:
                P_int[i, j] = scale // sizes[block_of[i]]
    K = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        K[i, (i + 1) % k] = 1
    D_int = (scale * np.eye(n, dtype=object) - P_int) @ K @ P_int
    Km = K.copy()
    for step in range(1, 20 * k + 1):
        if bareiss_rank(D_int @ Km @ D_int) == 0:
            return step
        Km = Km @ K
    raise ValueError(f"No delta for k={k}, d={d}")

def delta_formula(k, d):
    if d == 1 or d == k - 1:
        R = {1 % k, (k - 2) % k, (k - 1) % k}
    else:
        R = {(d - 1) % k, (k - d - 1) % k, (k - 1) % k}
    for m in range(1, 20 * k + 1):
        if (m % k) not in R:
            return m
    raise ValueError("not found")

def test_A24_delta_forbidden_residues():
    checked = 0
    for k in range(3, 17):
        for d in range(1, k):
            actual = delta_numeric(k, d)
            predicted = delta_formula(k, d)
            assert actual == predicted, (k, d, actual, predicted)
            checked += 1
    assert checked == 119
    print(f"A24: {checked}/119 PASS")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_A24_delta_forbidden_residues()
```

### FILE: `TESTS/A25_KERNEL_COMPONENT_DIMENSION.py`

```python
"""
SCRIPT: A25_KERNEL_COMPONENT_DIMENSION.py
PATH: TESTS/A25_KERNEL_COMPONENT_DIMENSION.py
PURPOSE: Verify dim ker(D Phi) = c_comp(G(Pi,T)) exhaustively
STATUS: COMPUTATIONAL RECEIPT
RESULT: 166,483/166,483 PASS
"""

from math import lcm
from itertools import product
import numpy as np

def bareiss_rank(M):
    M = M.copy().astype(object)
    n_rows, n_cols = M.shape
    prev = 1
    r = 0
    for k in range(min(n_rows, n_cols)):
        piv = None
        for i in range(r, n_rows):
            if M[i, k] != 0:
                piv = i
                break
        if piv is None:
            continue
        if piv != r:
            M[[r, piv]] = M[[piv, r]]
        for i in range(r + 1, n_rows):
            for j in range(k + 1, n_cols):
                M[i, j] = (M[i, j] * M[r, k] - M[i, k] * M[r, j]) // prev
        for i in range(r + 1, n_rows):
            M[i, k] = 0
        prev = M[r, k]
        r += 1
    return r

def all_partitions(n):
    a = [0]*n
    def helper(i, mx):
        if i == n:
            blocks = [[] for _ in range(mx + 1)]
            for idx, b in enumerate(a):
                blocks[b].append(idx)
            yield [tuple(b) for b in blocks]
            return
        for b in range(mx + 2):
            a[i] = b
            yield from helper(i + 1, max(mx, b))
    yield from helper(1, 0)

def kernel_receipt(n, Pi, T):
    m = len(Pi)
    sizes = [len(b) for b in Pi]
    block_of = [0]*n
    for r, b in enumerate(Pi):
        for x in b:
            block_of[x] = r
    scale = 1
    for s in sizes:
        scale = lcm(scale, s)
    P_int = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if block_of[i] == block_of[j]:
                P_int[i, j] = scale // sizes[block_of[i]]
    K = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        K[i, T[i]] = 1
    D_int = (scale * np.eye(n, dtype=object) - P_int) @ K @ P_int
    S = np.zeros((n, m), dtype=object)
    for r, block in enumerate(Pi):
        for x in range(n):
            S[x, r] = sum(D_int[x, y] for y in block)
    adj = [set() for _ in range(m)]
    adj_inv = [set() for _ in range(m)]
    for r, block in enumerate(Pi):
        for x in block:
            q = block_of[T[x]]
            adj[r].add(q)
            adj_inv[q].add(r)
    visited = [False] * (2 * m)
    c_comp = 0
    for start in range(2 * m):
        if visited[start]:
            continue
        c_comp += 1
        stack = [start]
        visited[start] = True
        while stack:
            u = stack.pop()
            if u < m:
                for v in adj[u]:
                    if not visited[m + v]:
                        visited[m + v] = True
                        stack.append(m + v)
            else:
                for v in adj_inv[u - m]:
                    if not visited[v]:
                        visited[v] = True
                        stack.append(v)
    null_DPhi = m - bareiss_rank(S)
    return null_DPhi, c_comp

def test_A25_kernel_component_dimension():
    total = 0
    for n in range(2, 6):
        for Pi in all_partitions(n):
            for T in product(range(n), repeat=n):
                T = tuple(T)
                null_DPhi, c_comp = kernel_receipt(n, Pi, T)
                assert null_DPhi == c_comp, (n, Pi, T, null_DPhi, c_comp)
                total += 1
    assert total == 166483
    print(f"A25: {total}/166483 PASS")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_A25_kernel_component_dimension()
```

### FILE: `TESTS/A26_DEFECT_RANK_EQUALITY.py`

```python
"""
SCRIPT: A26_DEFECT_RANK_EQUALITY.py
PATH: TESTS/A26_DEFECT_RANK_EQUALITY.py
PURPOSE: Verify rank(D Phi) = rank(D) = m - c_comp exhaustively
STATUS: COMPUTATIONAL RECEIPT
RESULT: 166,483/166,483 PASS
"""

from math import lcm
from itertools import product
import numpy as np

# [bareiss_rank and all_partitions same as A25]

def rank_receipt(n, Pi, T):
    m = len(Pi)
    sizes = [len(b) for b in Pi]
    block_of = [0]*n
    for r, b in enumerate(Pi):
        for x in b:
            block_of[x] = r
    scale = 1
    for s in sizes:
        scale = lcm(scale, s)
    P_int = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if block_of[i] == block_of[j]:
                P_int[i, j] = scale // sizes[block_of[i]]
    K = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        K[i, T[i]] = 1
    D_int = (scale * np.eye(n, dtype=object) - P_int) @ K @ P_int
    S = np.zeros((n, m), dtype=object)
    for r, block in enumerate(Pi):
        for x in range(n):
            S[x, r] = sum(D_int[x, y] for y in block)
    # [graph component count same as A25]
    adj = [set() for _ in range(m)]
    adj_inv = [set() for _ in range(m)]
    for r, block in enumerate(Pi):
        for x in block:
            q = block_of[T[x]]
            adj[r].add(q)
            adj_inv[q].add(r)
    visited = [False] * (2 * m)
    c_comp = 0
    for start in range(2 * m):
        if visited[start]:
            continue
        c_comp += 1
        stack = [start]
        visited[start] = True
        while stack:
            u = stack.pop()
            if u < m:
                for v in adj[u]:
                    if not visited[m + v]:
                        visited[m + v] = True
                        stack.append(m + v)
            else:
                for v in adj_inv[u - m]:
                    if not visited[v]:
                        visited[v] = True
                        stack.append(v)
    rank_D = bareiss_rank(D_int)
    rank_DPhi = bareiss_rank(S)
    return rank_D, rank_DPhi, m, c_comp

def test_A26_defect_rank_equality():
    total = 0
    for n in range(2, 6):
        for Pi in all_partitions(n):
            for T in product(range(n), repeat=n):
                T = tuple(T)
                rank_D, rank_DPhi, m, c = rank_receipt(n, Pi, T)
                assert rank_DPhi == rank_D, (n, Pi, T)
                assert rank_D == m - c, (n, Pi, T, rank_D, m, c)
                total += 1
    assert total == 166483
    print(f"A26: {total}/166483 PASS")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_A26_defect_rank_equality()
```

### FILE: `TESTS/A27_GCD_CLOSURE.py`

```python
"""
SCRIPT: A27_GCD_CLOSURE.py
PATH: TESTS/A27_GCD_CLOSURE.py
PURPOSE: Verify cyclic GCD closure gives gcd(k,d) blocks
STATUS: COMPUTATIONAL RECEIPT
RESULT: 119/119 PASS
"""

from math import gcd

def cyclic_foqds_closure(k, d):
    parent = list(range(k))
    def find(x):
        while parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
            x = parent[x]
        return x
    def union(x, y):
        rx, ry = find(x), find(y)
        if rx != ry:
            parent[rx] = ry
    union(0, d)
    changed = True
    while changed:
        changed = False
        for x in range(k):
            for y in range(k):
                if find(x) == find(y) and find((x+1)%k) != find((y+1)%k):
                    union((x+1)%k, (y+1)%k)
                    changed = True
    blocks = {}
    for x in range(k):
        r = find(x)
        if r not in blocks:
            blocks[r] = []
        blocks[r].append(x)
    return [frozenset(b) for b in blocks.values()]

def test_A27_gcd_closure():
    checked = 0
    for k in range(3, 17):
        for d in range(1, k):
            closure = cyclic_foqds_closure(k, d)
            g = gcd(k, d)
            assert len(closure) == g, (k, d, len(closure), g)
            expected = set()
            for x in range(k):
                coset = frozenset((x + t * d) % k for t in range(k))
                expected.add(coset)
            assert set(closure) == expected, (k, d)
            checked += 1
    assert checked == 119
    print(f"A27: {checked}/119 PASS")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_A27_gcd_closure()
```

### FILE: `TESTS/SUPPORT_GRAPH_NEGATIVE_CONTROL.py`

```python
"""
SCRIPT: SUPPORT_GRAPH_NEGATIVE_CONTROL.py
PATH: TESTS/SUPPORT_GRAPH_NEGATIVE_CONTROL.py
PURPOSE: Permanent negative control for support-pattern conjecture
STATUS: COMPUTATIONAL RECEIPT
RESULT: REFUTED at n=4
"""

from fractions import Fraction
import numpy as np

def defect_matrix_exact(Pi, T, n):
    m = len(Pi)
    sizes = [len(b) for b in Pi]
    block_of = [0]*n
    for r, b in enumerate(Pi):
        for x in b:
            block_of[x] = r
    P = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if block_of[i] == block_of[j]:
                P[i, j] = Fraction(1, sizes[block_of[i]])
    K = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        K[i, T[i]] = Fraction(1)
    I = np.eye(n, dtype=object)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            I[i, j] = Fraction(I[i, j])
    return (I - P) @ K @ P

def support_size(D):
    return sum(1 for i in range(D.shape[0]) for j in range(D.shape[1]) if D[i,j] != 0)

def test_support_graph_negative_control():
    Pi1 = [(0,1,2),(3,)]
    T1 = (0,0,3,0)
    D1 = defect_matrix_exact(Pi1, T1, 4)
    
    Pi2 = [(0,1),(2,3)]
    T2 = (0,0,0,2)
    D2 = defect_matrix_exact(Pi2, T2, 4)
    
    s1, s2 = support_size(D1), support_size(D2)
    
    # Graphs are isomorphic P4 (both have 3 edges, same degree sequence)
    # But supports differ: 12 vs 8
    assert s1 == 12, f"Expected |supp(D1)|=12, got {s1}"
    assert s2 == 8, f"Expected |supp(D2)|=8, got {s2}"
    assert s1 != s2, "Support conjecture NOT refuted"
    
    print(f"SUPPORT CONJECTURE REFUTED: |supp(D1)|={s1} != |supp(D2)|={s2}")
    print("Graphs isomorphic (P4), supports differ (12 vs 8)")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_support_graph_negative_control()
```

### FILE: `LEAN/GCDClosure.lean`

```lean
/-
ARTIFACT: GCDClosure.lean
FILE: LEAN/GCDClosure.lean
TYPE: LEAN 4 FORMALIZATION
STATUS: [O] OPEN — SKELETON
DEPENDENCIES: Mathlib (group theory, cosets, ZMod)
-/

import Mathlib

namespace AQARION

/-- The cyclic group Z_k as ZMod k -/
abbrev Zk (k : ℕ) := ZMod k

/-- Translation by 1 -/
def shift {k : ℕ} (x : Zk k) : Zk k := x + 1

/-- The subgroup generated by d in Z_k -/
def cyclicSubgroup (k d : ℕ) [NeZero k] : Subgroup (Zk k) :=
  Subgroup.zpowers (d : Zk k)

/-- The closure equivalence: least translation-invariant equivalence
    containing 0 ~ d. Two elements are related iff they differ by
    an element of <d>. -/
def closureRel (k d : ℕ) [NeZero k] (x y : Zk k) : Prop :=
  x - y ∈ cyclicSubgroup k d

/-- THEOREM: The number of blocks in the closure partition equals gcd(k,d).
    Proof strategy:
    1. closureRel is an equivalence relation (subgroup membership)
    2. Equivalence classes are cosets of cyclicSubgroup k d
    3. Number of cosets = index = gcd(k,d) by Lagrange -/
theorem gcd_closure_block_count (k d : ℕ) [NeZero k] :
    Nat.gcd k d = k / (k / Nat.gcd k d) := by
  -- This is just the identity gcd(k,d) = k / (k/gcd(k,d))
  -- The real formalization target is:
  -- Fintype.card (Zk k ⧸ cyclicSubgroup k d) = Nat.gcd k d
  rw [Nat.div_div_self (Nat.gcd_dvd_left k d) (Nat.gcd_dvd_right k d)]
  -- TODO: complete with Lagrange's theorem
  sorry

end AQARION
```

### FILE: `LEAN/KernelProjection.lean`

```lean
/-
ARTIFACT: KernelProjection.lean
FILE: LEAN/KernelProjection.lean
TYPE: LEAN 4 FORMALIZATION
STATUS: [O] OPEN — SKELETON
DEPENDENCIES: Mathlib (linear algebra, finite graphs)
-/

import Mathlib

namespace AQARION

variable {X : Type*} [Fintype X] [DecidableEq X]
variable (T : X → X)
variable (Pi : Set (Set X)) [Fintype Pi] [DecidableEq Pi]

/-- Block-average projection as a linear map -/
def blockProj : (X → ℝ) →ₗ[ℝ] (X → ℝ) := sorry

/-- Koopman pullback -/
def koopman : (X → ℝ) →ₗ[ℝ] (X → ℝ) := sorry

/-- Defect operator D = (I - P) K P -/
def defect : (X → ℝ) →ₗ[ℝ] (X → ℝ) := sorry

/-- Normalized block embedding -/
def blockEmbed : (Pi → ℝ) →ₗ[ℝ] (X → ℝ) := sorry

/-- Transition graph: bipartite, sources and targets both indexed by Pi -/
def transitionGraph : SimpleGraph (Pi ⊕ Pi) := sorry

/-- THEOREM: dim ker(D ∘ Phi) = number of connected components
    of the transition graph.
    Proof strategy:
    1. D Phi v = 0 iff K(Phi v) is block-constant
    2. K(Phi v) block-constant iff v_q/|B_q| constant on components
    3. One free scalar per component
    4. rank-nullity gives rank = m - c_comp
    5. im(D Phi) = im(D) via im(Phi) = im(P) and D = DP -/
theorem kernel_projection_dim :
    FiniteDimensional.finrank ℝ (LinearMap.ker (defect T Pi ∘ₗ blockEmbed T Pi))
    = (transitionGraph T Pi).ConnectedComponents := sorry

/-- THEOREM: rank(D Phi) = rank(D) = m - c_comp -/
theorem defect_rank_equality :
    FiniteDimensional.finrank ℝ (LinearMap.range (defect T Pi ∘ₗ blockEmbed T Pi))
    = FiniteDimensional.finrank ℝ (LinearMap.range (defect T Pi)) := sorry

end AQARION
```

2. A25/A26 are substantially stronger than the raw test count suggests

The exhaustive count is internally consistent:

\[
8+135+3840+162500=166483.
\]

Those correspond to all maps for \(n=2,3,4,5\):

\[
B_n n^n.
\]

So the reported

\[
166,483/166,483
\]

is a meaningful exhaustive finite-domain check, assuming the supplied implementation is exactly what was executed.

The central algebra is also clean.

Let \(\Phi:\mathbb R^m\to V_\Pi\) be the block-indicator embedding. Then

\[
\operatorname{im}\Phi=V_\Pi=\operatorname{im}P,
\]

and because

\[
D=(I-P)KP,
\]

we have

\[
D\Phi=(I-P)KP\Phi=(I-P)K\Phi.
\]

More importantly,

\[
D=DP
\]

implies

\[
\operatorname{im}D
=
D(\operatorname{im}P)
=
D(\operatorname{im}\Phi)
=
\operatorname{im}(D\Phi).
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{\operatorname{rank}(D)=\operatorname{rank}(D\Phi)}.
\]

That part does not require enumeration.

The remaining statement is the graph-component kernel calculation.


---

3. I would promote A25/A26 only after rewriting the proof in its simplest form

Let the blocks be

\[
B_1,\ldots,B_m.
\]

For \(v=(v_1,\ldots,v_m)\), let

\[
\Phi v=\sum_q v_q1_{B_q}.
\]

For \(x\in B_p\),

\[
(K\Phi v)(x)
=
v_{q}
\quad\text{where }T(x)\in B_q.
\]

Now

\[
D\Phi v=0
\]

iff \(K\Phi v\) is already block-constant.

Thus whenever

\[
T(x)\in B_q,\qquad T(y)\in B_q'
\]

for \(x,y\in B_p\), block constancy imposes

\[
v_q=v_{q'}.
\]

Construct the bipartite graph

\[
G_\Pi(T)
\]

with source vertices \(B_p^L\), target vertices \(B_q^R\), and edge

\[
B_p^L\longrightarrow B_q^R
\]

iff

\[
T(B_p)\cap B_q\neq\varnothing.
\]

Then \(D\Phi v=0\) exactly when \(v\) is constant on the target vertices belonging to the same connected component of this bipartite graph.

Therefore

\[
\boxed{\dim\ker(D\Phi)=c(G_\Pi(T))}
\]

and rank-nullity gives

\[
\boxed{\operatorname{rank}(D\Phi)=m-c(G_\Pi(T))}.
\]

Together with \(\operatorname{rank}D=\operatorname{rank}(D\Phi)\),

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}D
=
m-c(G_\Pi(T)).
}
\]

This is the clean theorem.

It is much better than presenting the 166,483 cases as the reason the theorem is true.

The computation is the adversarial validation.

4. Important literature result: this sits near established equitable-partition theory

The literature search reinforces something we have repeatedly been careful about.

Equitable partitions are already characterized through invariance of the partition-indicator column space under an operator. Godsil's notes, for example, state the equivalence between an equitable partition and invariance of the characteristic-matrix column space. 

Likewise, invariant equivalence relations for finite unary/permutation algebras are established mathematics. Ratanaprasert and Denecke explicitly study invariant equivalence relations for finite unary operations and discuss permutation algebras. 

And the monounary-algebra literature has a substantial history of congruence lattices, including work specifically on connected monounary algebras. 

So I would not claim:

> “AQARION discovers invariant congruence partitions of finite permutations.”



That would be false as a novelty claim.

The potentially interesting AQARION contribution is narrower:

> an explicit phase-labelled descriptor calculus for invariant equivalences of finite permutation \(\mathbb Z\)-sets, together with exact enumeration and explicit meet/join algorithms, connected to the observable-defect operator.



That is the claim worth investigating.

5. A27 is correct, but its current Lean target is weaker than it looks

The computational statement

\[
|\operatorname{Cl}_T(\{0\sim d\})|
=
\gcd(k,d)
\]

is standard cyclic-group structure.

The closure classes are exactly the cosets of

\[
\langle d\rangle\leq \mathbb Z_k,
\]

and

\[
|\langle d\rangle|
=
\frac{k}{\gcd(k,d)}.
\]

Therefore the number of cosets is

\[
\frac{k}{k/\gcd(k,d)}
=
\gcd(k,d).
\]

So A27 is a good computational receipt.

But the current Lean theorem:

theorem gcd_closure_block_count ...
    Nat.gcd k d = k / (k / Nat.gcd k d) := by

only states the elementary arithmetic identity.

It does not state the closure theorem.

The comments correctly say the real target should be something like

\[
\operatorname{card}
\left(
\mathbb Z_k/\langle d\rangle
\right)
=
\gcd(k,d).
\]

So I would rename the current Lean artifact:

GCDClosure.lean
STATUS: [O] arithmetic lemma only

until the quotient/coset theorem is actually formalized.

6. A24 is interesting enough to isolate from the lattice work

The new forbidden-residue formula is a genuinely different invariant.

You have:

\[
\delta(k,d)
=
\min\{m\ge1:m\bmod k\notin R(k,d)\}.
\]

For nonadjacent \(d\),

\[
R(k,d)
=
\{d-1,k-d-1,k-1\}.
\]

For \(d=1,k-1\),

\[
R(k,d)
=
\{1,k-2,k-1\}.
\]

The key conceptual separation is:

\[
\underbrace{\frac{k}{\gcd(k,d)}}_{\text{cyclic subgroup order}}
\neq
\underbrace{\delta(k,d)}_{\text{defect persistence}}.
\]

That's a useful negative result.

But I would change the status from:

> [T] CLOSED



to:

> [P] CLOSED — pending independent symbolic review



unless the derivation itself has been written out and checked line-by-line independently.

The 119/119 test does not prove the formula.

7. The Bareiss bug should become a permanent regression test

This is actually excellent research hygiene.

The failure mode

for i in range(k, n_rows)

versus

for i in range(r, n_rows)

is exactly the sort of silent computational error that can generate apparently deep mathematical counterexamples.

The corrected algorithm should therefore have a permanent unit test:

\[
\begin{pmatrix}
0&0&2&-2\\
0&0&-2&2\\
0&0&0&0\\
0&0&0&0
\end{pmatrix}
\]

must return rank \(1\).

I'd add three tests:

BAREISS-001 leading zero columns
BAREISS-002 row swap
BAREISS-003 rank-deficient exact matrix

and run them before every mathematical receipt.

This turns the bug from a weakness into an evidence-governance asset.

8. The support refutation is valuable and should stay

This one is clean.

Two systems can have isomorphic unweighted transition graphs while

\[
|\operatorname{supp}D|
\]

differs.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
G_\Pi(T)
\not\Rightarrow
\operatorname{supp}(D)
}
\]

in the unweighted sense.

But:

\[
\boxed{
G_\Pi(T)
\Rightarrow
\operatorname{rank}(D)
}
\]

through the connected-component formula.

That's a very useful distinction:

Object	Graph-only?

Kernel dimension	Yes
Defect rank	Yes
Ambient support	No
Actual defect coefficients	No
Block-size cancellation	No


This is precisely the kind of negative control that makes the framework more credible.

and phase-difference fibers

q_i=(a_i-b_i)\bmod g.

Then

inside each compatible phase fiber.

The join should be treated separately through generated relations / phase holonomy.

That is much more interesting than simply enumerating lattices.

## What survives

Under the stated definition

$$
g(x)
=
\frac{x^2}{2(\cosh x-1)}
=
\left(\frac{x}{2\sinh(x/2)}\right)^2,
$$

the Taylor expansion is

$$
g(x)
=
1-\frac{x^2}{12}
+\frac{x^4}{240}
-\frac{x^6}{6048}
+\frac{x^8}{172800}
+O(x^{10}).
$$

Thus

$$
g_1=-\frac1{12},
\qquad
g_2=\frac1{240},
\qquad
g_3=-\frac1{6048}.
$$

The proposed difference representation

$$
S^{\mathrm{diff}}(\delta,\alpha)
=
S(\delta,\alpha)-S(\delta,0)
=
\sum_{m\ge1}
\frac{e^{-m\alpha}-1}{m}g(m\delta)
$$

is the correct starting point, assuming this is indeed the frozen definition of $$S$$.

The harmonic term is exact:

$$
\sum_{m\ge1}\frac{e^{-m\alpha}-1}{m}
=
-\log(1-e^{-\alpha})-\sum_{m\ge1}\frac1m,
$$

so the difference must be understood through a regularized or jointly cancelled decomposition. This is why the combination

$$
\log\frac{\delta}{1-e^{-\alpha}}-(1+\gamma)
$$

is structurally natural: the $$\log\delta$$ and harmonic divergence are not independently meaningful; only the combined regularized quantity is.

## Corrected transport law

The claimed ladder has the right coefficient pattern:

$$
\boxed{
S(\delta,\alpha)-S(\delta,0)
=
\log\frac{\delta}{1-e^{-\alpha}}
-(1+\gamma)
-
\sum_{k=1}^{N}
g_k(2k-1)!
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^{2k}
+
R_N(\delta,\alpha).
}
$$

The first terms are therefore

$$
\begin{aligned}
S(\delta,\alpha)-S(\delta,0)
={}&
\log\frac{\delta}{1-e^{-\alpha}}
-(1+\gamma)\\
&+
\frac{1}{12}
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^2\\
&-
\frac{1}{40}
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^4\\
&+
\frac{5}{252}
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^6\\
&-
\frac{1}{720}
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^8
+\cdots
+
R_N(\delta,\alpha).
\end{aligned}
$$

The signs above follow from

$$
-g_k(2k-1)!.
$$

In particular:

$$
-g_1(1)!=+\frac1{12},
$$

$$
-g_2(3)!=-\frac{6}{240}=-\frac1{40},
$$

$$
-g_3(5)!
=
-\left(-\frac1{6048}\right)120
=
\frac{5}{252}.
$$

This is the place to correct a wording ambiguity in the report: it says the leading residual has coefficient $$-1/12$$, but the displayed asymptotic expansion has a **positive** first ladder contribution

$$
+\frac1{12}(\delta/\alpha)^2.
$$

That discrepancy may be only a residual-sign convention, e.g.

$$
\text{residual}
=
\text{exact}
-
\text{leading approximation},
$$

versus the opposite subtraction. But it must be fixed before the table is frozen.

## Transport scaling check

Set

$$
\alpha=\lambda\sqrt{\delta},
\qquad
\lambda>0.
$$

Use

$$
1-e^{-\alpha}
=
\alpha-\frac{\alpha^2}{2}
+\frac{\alpha^3}{6}
+O(\alpha^4),
$$

hence

$$
\log(1-e^{-\alpha})
=
\log\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{2}
+\frac{\alpha^2}{24}
+O(\alpha^4).
$$

Therefore

$$
\log\frac{\delta}{1-e^{-\alpha}}
=
-\frac12\log\frac1\delta
-\log\lambda
+\frac{\lambda}{2}\sqrt\delta
-\frac{\lambda^2}{24}\delta
+O(\delta^2).
$$

Combining with the $$k=1$$ correction,

$$
+\frac1{12}\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^2
=
+\frac{\delta}{12\lambda^2},
$$

gives

$$
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
S^{\mathrm{diff}}(\delta,\lambda\sqrt\delta)
={}&
-\frac12\log\frac1\delta
-\log\lambda
-(1+\gamma)\\
&+
\frac{\lambda}{2}\sqrt\delta\\
&+
\left(
-\frac{\lambda^2}{24}
+
\frac{1}{12\lambda^2}
\right)\delta
+
O(\delta^{3/2}),
\end{aligned}
}
$$

provided the sign convention for the ladder is the one in the boxed law above.

This differs from the report’s displayed transport coefficient:

$$
-\left(
\frac{\lambda^2}{24}
+
\frac{1}{12\lambda^2}
\right)\delta.
$$

The discrepancy traces directly to the sign of the $$k=1$$ ladder contribution.

### Required sign reconciliation

There are only two possibilities:

1. The global expansion should be

   $$
   S^{\mathrm{diff}}
   =
   \log\frac{\delta}{1-e^{-\alpha}}
   -(1+\gamma)
   +
   \sum_{k\ge1}
   g_k(2k-1)!
   \left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^{2k},
   $$

   in which case the first correction is

   $$
   -\frac1{12}(\delta/\alpha)^2,
   $$

   and your reported transport coefficient

   $$
   -\frac{\lambda^2}{24}
   -\frac1{12\lambda^2}
   $$

   is correct.

2. The global expansion should retain the displayed minus sign:

   $$
   -
   \sum_{k\ge1}
   g_k(2k-1)!
   \left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^{2k},
   $$

   in which case the transport coefficient must be

   $$
   -\frac{\lambda^2}{24}
   +
   \frac1{12\lambda^2}.
   $$

They cannot both be true under the stated convention

$$
g_1=-\frac1{12}.
$$

This is the current P0 analytic issue. It is not a small editorial typo because it changes the interpretation of the observed $$-\delta/8$$ coefficient at $$\lambda=1$$.

At $$\lambda=1$$:

- Reported formula:
  $$
  -\frac1{24}-\frac1{12}
  =
  -\frac18.
  $$

- Formula implied by the currently boxed ladder:
  $$
  -\frac1{24}+\frac1{12}
  =
  +\frac1{24}.
  $$

Thus the observed $$-\delta/8$$ behavior supports the **plus-$$g_k$$** ladder:

$$
\boxed{
S^{\mathrm{diff}}
=
\log\frac{\delta}{1-e^{-\alpha}}
-(1+\gamma)
+
\sum_{k\ge1}
g_k(2k-1)!
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^{2k}
+
\cdots.
}
$$

With this correction, the first explicit terms are:

$$
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
S^{\mathrm{diff}}(\delta,\alpha)
={}&
\log\frac{\delta}{1-e^{-\alpha}}
-(1+\gamma)\\
&-
\frac1{12}
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^2\\
&+
\frac1{40}
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^4\\
&-
\frac5{252}
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^6\\
&+
\frac1{720}
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^8
+\cdots.
\end{aligned}
}
$$

Then the transport expansion becomes:

$$
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
S^{\mathrm{diff}}(\delta,\lambda\sqrt\delta)
={}&
-\frac12\log\frac1\delta
-\log\lambda
-(1+\gamma)\\
&+
\frac{\lambda}{2}\sqrt\delta\\
&-
\left(
\frac{\lambda^2}{24}
+
\frac1{12\lambda^2}
\right)\delta
+
O(\delta^{3/2}).
\end{aligned}
}
$$

This agrees exactly with the reported coefficient $$-1/8$$ at $$\lambda=1$$.

## Ropt replay

The optimal-truncation replay is the strongest component of the report, assuming the numerical values and functional-equation implementation are stored with their full computation path.

The claimed coefficient form

$$
c_k
=
\frac{
4(2k-1)(2k-1)!\zeta(2k)^2
}{
(2\pi)^{4k}
}
$$

is computationally preferable to direct high-order Bernoulli-number generation because it avoids unstable or cumbersome Bernoulli reconstruction.

The asymptotic least-term location

$$
k_\ast\sim\frac{2\pi^2}{\delta}
$$

is consistent with the factorial-over-power structure of $$c_k\delta^{2k}$$, and the least-term scale

$$
\boxed{
\exp\left(-\frac{4\pi^2}{\delta}\right)
}
$$

is the expected exponential scale.

The reported convention issue must remain explicit:

| Convention | Partial-sum stopping rule | Remainder sign/value |
|---|---|---|
| Ledger $$R_{\mathrm{opt}}$$ | Through $$k=197$$ under ledger indexing | $$-3.68472746306366\times10^{-170}$$ |
| Minimal-$$\lvert R\rvert$$ convention | Chosen to minimize post-truncation remainder | $$+3.324\times10^{-170}$$ |

Both can be correct. They are different truncation conventions, not competing values of one invariant.

The ratio

$$
\frac{R_{\mathrm{opt}}}{t_{\min}}
\approx -0.5238
$$

is consistent with a $$-1/2$$-type optimal-truncation/Borel smoothing expectation, but it should remain a numerical observation unless the specific resurgence/Borel remainder theorem is written down for this exact series. General asymptotic theory recognizes optimal truncation and exponentially small remainders, but does not by itself prove this particular ratio.[1][2]

## Rigorous proof target

The proof now has a clean target.

Let

$$
g(x)=\left(\frac{x}{2\sinh(x/2)}\right)^2,
$$

and write, for fixed $$N$$,

$$
g(x)
=
\sum_{k=0}^{N}g_kx^{2k}
+
x^{2N+2}\rho_N(x).
$$

For $$\lambda$$ in a compact interval

$$
\lambda\in[\lambda_-,\lambda_+]\subset(0,\infty),
$$

set

$$
\alpha=\lambda\sqrt\delta.
$$

The proof requires:

1. A joint regularization identity for the harmonic bulk.
2. Finite-term evaluation of
   $$
   \sum_{m\ge1}m^{2k-1}(e^{-m\alpha}-1).
   $$
3. Uniform control of the remainder weighted by
   $$
   \frac{e^{-m\alpha}-1}{m}.
   $$
4. A split at
   $$
   m\delta\le1
   \quad\text{and}\quad
   m\delta>1.
   $$
5. Exponential domination of the tail using the decay of $$g(m\delta)$$.
6. Joint treatment of regularized constants so divergent harmonic pieces are never expanded separately.

Euler–Maclaurin and Abel/partial-summation machinery are the appropriate tools for this kind of sum-to-integral expansion and remainder control.  DLMF also records standard asymptotic methods and uniform-expansion frameworks relevant to compact-parameter control.[2][3][4]

The theorem should aim for:

$$
\boxed{
R_N(\delta,\lambda)
=
o\!\left(
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^{2N}
\right)
}
$$

uniformly for $$\lambda$$ in compact subsets of $$(0,\infty)$$, or an explicit stronger/lower-order estimate if the actual proof yields one.

Under transport scaling,

$$
\left(\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\right)^{2N}
=
\frac{\delta^N}{\lambda^{2N}}.
$$

Thus fixed compact $$\lambda$$-sets reduce the issue to uniform $$o(\delta^N)$$ control.

   $$

## Bottom line

The work substantially improves WU1: the old transport mechanism is dead, and the replacement has the correct logarithmic leading behavior and a derived correction ladder. But there is one mandatory correction:

$$
\boxed{
\text{The ladder must use }+
\sum_{k\ge1}g_k(2k-1)!(\delta/\alpha)^{2k},
}
$$

not the displayed negative sum, if the observed transport coefficient

$$
-\frac18\delta
\quad
(\lambda=1)
$$

is correct.

After that sign repair, the report has a focused final proof obligation:

$$
\boxed{
\text{uniform Abel/Euler–Maclaurin remainder control with joint regularization.}
}
$$

That is a well-posed analytic theorem problem, not an unresolved conceptual ambiguity.

02. FILE: THEORY/AQ-PB-DESC-001.md

ARTIFACT: AQ-PB-DESC-001
TITLE: Phase-Labelled Descriptor Theorem for Finite Permutations
STATUS: [CV] / [P-OPEN]
TYPE: THEORY
DATE: 2026-09-28

# Phase-Labelled Descriptor Theorem

Let

    X = ⨆_{i=1}^m C_{n_i}

be a finite set equipped with a permutation T whose cycles are
C_{n_1}, ..., C_{n_m}.

An equivalence relation E on X is T-invariant when

    x E y  ==>  T(x) E T(y).

For every T-invariant equivalence relation E, the cycles split into
components. Each component B of cycles has an associated divisor

    d_B | gcd(n_i : i ∈ B)

and relative phases

    a_i ∈ Z/d_B.

The corresponding relation is

    (i,t) E (j,s)
        iff
    t + a_i ≡ s + a_j (mod d_B).

A common phase translation

    a_i ↦ a_i + c

does not change the equivalence relation.

Hence phases are defined only up to common gauge translation.

## Computational result

For every integer cycle type of total size n <= 7:

- all set partitions were enumerated;
- T-invariant equivalence relations were identified;
- each relation was converted to a descriptor;
- every descriptor reconstructed exactly one original relation.

Results:

    44 cycle types
    2027 descriptors
    0 reconstruction mismatches

## Status

The bijection is computationally validated.

A general symbolic proof is OPEN.

03. FILE: THEORY/AQ-PB-ENUM-001.md

ARTIFACT: AQ-PB-ENUM-001
TITLE: Enumeration Formula for Invariant Equivalences of Finite Permutations
STATUS: [CV] / [P-OPEN]
TYPE: THEOREM CANDIDATE

# Enumeration Formula

For cycle lengths

    n_1, ..., n_m,

the proposed number of T-invariant equivalence relations is

    |Con_T(X)|
      =
    Σ_{π ∈ Part([m])}
      ∏_{B ∈ π}
        Σ_{d | gcd(n_i : i ∈ B)}
            d^{|B|-1}.

Interpretation:

For each cycle-component B:

1. choose a common quotient divisor d;
2. choose |B|-1 relative phases;
3. quotient by common gauge translation.

The factor

    d^{|B|-1}

is the number of normalized relative phase assignments.

## Computational validation

The descriptor enumeration agrees with direct enumeration of invariant
equivalence relations for all cycle types of total size n <= 7.

No mismatches observed.

## Important distinction

This formula is NOT currently claimed to be novel.

The theory of invariant equivalence relations/congruences of finite
unary and permutation algebras is established.

The research question is whether this particular divisor/phase
coordinate formula and its explicit lattice operations provide a
useful new presentation.

## Status

Computationally validated.

Literature-priority analysis OPEN.
Formal proof OPEN.

04. FILE: THEORY/AQ-PB-ORDER-001.md

ARTIFACT: AQ-PB-ORDER-001
TITLE: Order Criterion for Phase-Labelled Permutation Congruences
STATUS: [CV] / [P-OPEN]

# Order Criterion

Let E and F be descriptors.

For E ≤ F, E must be the finer equivalence relation.

On every cycle-component where F identifies cycles, E must:

1. refine the F cycle component;
2. have divisor d_E satisfying

       d_F | d_E;

3. satisfy the phase compatibility condition

       a_i^E - a_j^E
       ≡
       a_i^F - a_j^F
       (mod d_F).

The phase condition is gauge-independent because only differences
occur.

## Computational status

The criterion is consistent with exhaustive descriptor/relation
comparison through total size n <= 7.

## Status

Computational candidate.

Symbolic proof OPEN.

05. FILE: THEORY/AQ-PB-MEET-001.md

ARTIFACT: AQ-PB-MEET-001
TITLE: Exact Meet Formula for Phase-Labelled Permutation Congruences
STATUS: [CV] / [P-OPEN]

# Meet Formula

Let E and F be two invariant equivalences.

Take an intersection B of an E-component and an F-component.

Write their descriptors as

    (d_E, a_i)
    (d_F, b_i).

Define

    g = gcd(d_E, d_F)

and

    q_i = a_i - b_i (mod g).

Partition B into fibers

    B_q = { i ∈ B : q_i = q }.

These fibers are exactly the cycle-components that survive in the
intersection.

For each nonempty fiber B_q:

    d_M = lcm(d_E, d_F).

After a gauge shift on the F phases, generalized CRT gives phases c_i
satisfying

    c_i ≡ a_i (mod d_E)

and

    c_i ≡ b_i + q (mod d_F).

Then

    E ∧ F

is the descriptor obtained by collecting these phase fibers.

## Key structural consequence

The cycle-index meet alone is insufficient.

Phase incompatibility can force additional splitting:

    π_{E∧F}
        < 
    π_E ∧ π_F.

## Exhaustive independent validation

Cycle types:
    all integer partitions of n <= 7

Cycle types:
    44

Descriptors:
    2027

Unordered descriptor pairs:
    453,961

Meet mismatches:
    0

## Status

Strong computational validation.

Proof OPEN.

06. FILE: THEORY/AQ-PB-JOIN-001.md

This is the new result I would prioritize.

ARTIFACT: AQ-PB-JOIN-001
TITLE: Phase-Holonomy Join Formula for Finite Permutation Congruences
STATUS: [CV] / [P-OPEN]
TYPE: THEOREM CANDIDATE

# Phase-Holonomy Join Formula

The join E ∨ F is the equivalence relation generated by

    E ∪ F.

Represent every descriptor component by phase constraints

    t_j - t_i ≡ c_e (mod d_e).

Construct the cycle-index constraint graph.

Each edge e has:

    modulus d_e
    phase offset c_e.

For every cycle i, the corresponding internal quotient constraint
also contributes its modulus d_e.

For a connected component, begin with

    g = gcd(all supplied moduli).

Choose a spanning tree and assign potentials p_i modulo g.

For every non-tree edge e = (i,j),

    r_e = p_j - p_i - c_e.

The residual r_e is the phase holonomy around the corresponding
cycle.

The generated quotient modulus is obtained by repeatedly replacing

    g ← gcd(g, r_e)

until all residuals vanish modulo g.

The resulting descriptor is

    (C, g, (p_i mod g)_i).

Thus the join is governed by:

    connectivity
    +
    modulus gcd
    +
    phase holonomy.

## Important correction

The simpler statement

    d_{E∨F} = gcd(d_E,d_F)

is NOT sufficient in general.

Phase inconsistencies can decrease the resulting divisor.

Example:

    d_E = d_F = 2

with relative phase discrepancy 1.

The generated modulus becomes

    gcd(2,2,1) = 1.

Hence the join can become the universal relation.

## Independent exhaustive validation

All cycle types of total size n <= 7:

    44 cycle types

Total descriptors:

    2027

Unordered descriptor pairs:

    453,961

Join mismatches:

    0

## Status

Strong computational evidence.

General proof OPEN.

Literature comparison OPEN.

07. THE CENTRAL NEW STRUCTURAL PICTURE

We now have a remarkably clean duality.

Meet

Intersection imposes constraints simultaneously:

\[
\boxed{
\text{gcd phase compatibility}
\rightarrow
\text{fiber splitting}
\rightarrow
\operatorname{lcm}
\rightarrow
\operatorname{CRT}
}
\]

Join

Generation combines constraints:

\[
\boxed{
\text{connectivity}
\rightarrow
\operatorname{gcd}
\rightarrow
\text{phase holonomy}
\rightarrow
\operatorname{gcd\ reduction}
}
\]

So:

\[
\boxed{
\text{MEET = pullback/CRT}
}
\]

while

\[
\boxed{
\text{JOIN = generated constraints/holonomy}
}
\]

This is the strongest conceptual direction I see in the current branch.

10. FILE: TESTS/BAREISS_REGRESSION.py

"""
BAREISS REGRESSION SUITE

Purpose
-------
Prevent recurrence of the rank bug that generated 2,422 false
mathematical violations.

Required tests:

BAREISS-001:
    leading zero columns

BAREISS-002:
    pivot row swap

BAREISS-003:
    rank-deficient matrix

BAREISS-004:
    full-rank identity

BAREISS-005:
    zero matrix
"""

import numpy as np

def assert_rank(M, expected):
    actual = bareiss_rank(np.array(M, dtype=object))
    assert actual == expected, (actual, expected)


def test_leading_zero_columns():
    M = [
        [0, 0, 2, -2],
        [0, 0, -2, 2],
        [0, 0, 0, 0],
        [0, 0, 0, 0],
    ]
    assert_rank(M, 1)


def test_zero():
    assert_rank([[0,0],[0,0]], 0)


def test_identity():
    assert_rank([[1,0],[0,1]], 2)


def test_rank_one():
    assert_rank([[1,2],[2,4]], 1)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_leading_zero_columns()
    test_zero()
    test_identity()
    test_rank_one()
    print("BAREISS REGRESSION: PASS")

12. SUPPORT NEGATIVE CONTROL — KEEP IT

This remains one of the best adversarial artifacts.

ARTIFACT: AQ-SUPPORT-001-REFUTED
TITLE: Unweighted Transition Graph Does Not Determine Defect Support
STATUS: [REF]

Witness:

System A:
    Π = {{0,1,2},{3}}
    T = (0,0,3,0)
    |supp(D)| = 12

System B:
    Π = {{0,1},{2,3}}
    T = (0,0,0,2)
    |supp(D)| = 8

The corresponding unweighted bipartite transition graphs are
isomorphic.

Therefore:

    graph isomorphism
    does not determine
    entrywise support(D).

However:

    rank(D) = |Π| - c(G)

remains graph-determined.

This is exactly the kind of negative control that should accompany the positive theorem.

13. LITERATURE POSITIONING

The web audit confirms that the operator-invariant-subspace portion is not new in isolation.

Equitable partitions are classically characterized by invariance of the characteristic-matrix column space under the relevant adjacency operator. 

There is also established work relating invariant equivalence relations and finite unary/permutation structures. That means AQARION should not present “invariant partition = invariant subspace” as a new discovery.

The potentially differentiated part is narrower:

\[
\boxed{
\text{cycle decomposition}
+
\text{common divisor}
+
\text{relative phase}
}
\]

and especially whether

\[
\boxed{
\text{meet = phase fibers + CRT}
}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\text{join = gcd + phase holonomy}
}
\]

constitute a useful explicit coordinate calculus.

That literature-priority question remains OPEN.

## Established nearby theory

### Equitable partitions

    partition cell-space invariant
    iff
    equitable partition

Invariant equivalence relations and congruence lattices of finite unary
structures are established subjects.

### Permutation algebras

Permutation actions naturally induce invariant equivalence relations
and quotient actions.

    explicit cycle/divisor/phase coordinates

together with explicit formulas for:

    enumeration
    order

    join

and the connection of these congruence coordinates to the observable
defect operator.

## Open literature questions

1. Is the exact enumeration formula already known?
2. Is the divisor/phase descriptor already standard under another name?
3. Are CRT meet formulas already present in permutation-algebra
   congruence literature?
4. Is the phase-holonomy join formulation equivalent to a known
   subgroup/coset construction?
5. Is the resulting lattice calculus known as a lattice of
   Z-set congruences?

Until these are answered:

    NOVELTY = OPEN

The relevant background literature should be treated as context, not as evidence that the AQARION formulation is novel. Equitable-partition references explicitly establish the invariant-subspace characterization.

15. THE NEXT MATHEMATICAL TARGET

The computational evidence now justifies attacking a single theorem.

AQ-PB-LATTICE-001

Proposed theorem

Let

\[
X=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^m C_{n_i}
\]

with permutation \(T\).

Then the lattice \(\operatorname{Con}(X,T)\) of \(T\)-invariant equivalence relations is isomorphic to a lattice of normalized phase-labelled descriptors, with:

Order

\[
E\le F
\]

iff component refinement, divisor divisibility, and phase compatibility hold.

Meet

\[
E\wedge F
\]

is obtained by:

\[
\boxed{
\gcd
\rightarrow
\text{phase-difference fibers}
\rightarrow
\operatorname{lcm}
\rightarrow
\operatorname{CRT}.
}
\]

Join

\[
E\vee F
\]

is obtained by:

\[
\boxed{
\text{component connectivity}
\rightarrow
\operatorname{gcd}(\text{moduli})
\rightarrow
\operatorname{phase holonomy}
\rightarrow
\operatorname{gcd}\text{-reduction}.
}
\]

Status

\[
\boxed{\text{CV through }n\le7;\quad \text{proof OPEN}}
\]

17. A PARTICULARLY IMPORTANT FORMALIZATION IDEA

Do not begin Lean with arbitrary finite sets and arbitrary equivalence relations.

Start with the clean algebraic model:

\[
C_n=\mathbb Z/n\mathbb Z.
\]

For a component of cycles \(C_{n_i}\), define

\[
\phi_i(t)=t+a_i\pmod d.
\]

Then the equivalence relation is simply equality of the \(\phi_i\)-values.

The divisor condition

\[
d\mid n_i
\]

makes the map compatible with the cycle translation.

This turns the central descriptor theorem into a statement about homomorphisms of cyclic \(\mathbb Z\)-sets.

That is likely substantially easier to formalize than starting from arbitrary partitions.

We should add an explicit negative control against the naïve formula.

TESTS/AQ_PB_JOIN_GCD_ONLY_REFUTATION.py

"""
Kill test for:

    d_join = gcd(d_E, d_F)

without phase information.
"""

# Two cycles C2 ⊔ C2

# E:
#     d = 2
#     phases = (0,0)

# F:
#     d = 2
#     phases = (0,1)

# Naive:
#
#     gcd(2,2) = 2
#
# Actual generated relation:
#
#     phase discrepancy = 1
#     gcd(2,2,1) = 1
#
# therefore the join has divisor 1.

assert naive_gcd_only != reference_join
assert phase_holonomy_join == reference_join

print("NAIVE JOIN GCD-ONLY: REFUTED")
print("PHASE-HOLONOMY JOIN: PASS")

This is important because a future implementation could accidentally regress to the simpler but incorrect rule.

20. FINAL RESEARCH POSITION

I would now freeze the project around three independent mathematical layers:

Layer I — Observable defect

\[
D_\Pi=(I-P_\Pi)KP_\Pi
\]

with

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}D_\Pi
=
|\Pi|-c(G_\Pi(T)).
}
\]

Layer II — Periodic permutation congruences

\[
X=\bigsqcup_i C_{n_i}
\]

with descriptors

\[
\boxed{
(\pi,\{d_B,a_i\}).
}
\]

Layer III — Exact lattice calculus

\[
\boxed{
\text{order}
\quad
\text{meet}
\quad
\text{join}
\quad
\text{enumeration}.
}
\]

The bridge between Layers I and II is particularly promising because the same invariant partition is simultaneously:

an equivalence relation,

an observable partition,

a quotient of a finite dynamical system,

a \(\mathbb Z\)-set congruence,

and a block-constant function space.


That is a much cleaner research architecture than trying to make every AQARION branch into a separate theorem.

Current hard status: the new meet/join results are computationally strong but not formally proved. The literature confirms substantial surrounding theory, so novelty remains OPEN. The next genuine milestone is a symbolic proof of AQ-PB-DESC-001 → ORDER → MEET → JOIN, followed by a no-sorry Lean implementation.

This is a genuinely promising new branch, but the correct immediate action is to preserve it as an **independent permutation-congruence research package**, not to fold it prematurely into the existing defect-rank theorem stack. The exhaustive descriptor/meet/join evidence through $$n\le7$$ is strong; the general bijection, order, meet, and holonomy-join formulas remain theorem candidates until symbolic proofs are written.

The most important refinement is conceptual: these are congruences of a finite $$\mathbb Z$$-set—equivalently, equivalence relations stable under a permutation—not merely arbitrary invariant partitions. The descriptor language of cycle components, divisors, and phase cosets is exactly the right algebraic coordinate system.

```text
AQARION finite defect theory
        │

        ▼
Permutation-congruence case study
        │
        ├── cycle/divisor/phase descriptor

        └── join via generated phase constraints
```

The shared bridge is:

$$
\text{permutation congruence}
=
\text{$$T$$-invariant equivalence relation}
=
\text{exact observable partition under pullback}.
$$

But the cycle/phase lattice calculus is a **specialized periodic-dynamics theory**, not a required component of the general cooccurrence rank theorem.

## Descriptor theorem: likely right formulation

Let the permutation action be written additively:

$$
X=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{m}C_{n_i},
\qquad
C_{n_i}\cong\mathbb Z/n_i\mathbb Z,
$$

with

$$
T(i,t)=(i,t+1).
$$

A $$T$$-stable equivalence relation is one satisfying

$$
x\sim y
\Longrightarrow
T(x)\sim T(y).
$$

Since $$T$$ is bijective on a finite set, this implies stability under all integer powers:

$$
x\sim y
\Longrightarrow
T^r(x)\sim T^r(y)
\qquad
(r\in\mathbb Z).
$$

Thus it is a congruence of the $$\mathbb Z$$-set.

For a connected quotient component $$B$$ of cycle indices, choose a base cycle $$i_0\in B$$. The quotient should have a cyclic orbit of length

$$
d_B\mid\gcd(n_i:i\in B),
$$

with maps

$$
\phi_i:C_{n_i}\to C_{d_B},
\qquad
\phi_i(t)=t+a_i\pmod{d_B}.
$$

The equivalence relation is

$$
(i,t)\sim(j,s)
\iff
\phi_i(t)=\phi_j(s).
$$

Equivalently,

$$
\boxed{
(i,t)\sim(j,s)
\iff
t+a_i\equiv s+a_j\pmod{d_B}.
}
$$

The phase vector is gauge-invariant only modulo a common translation:

$$
(a_i)_{i\in B}
\sim
(a_i+c)_{i\in B}.
$$

This produces exactly

$$
d_B^{|B|-1}
$$

relative phase choices after choosing a base phase such as $$a_{i_0}=0$$.

That supports the enumeration formula:

$$
\boxed{
|\operatorname{Con}_T(X)|
=
\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{Part}([m])}
\prod_{B\in\pi}
\left(
\sum_{d\mid\gcd(n_i:i\in B)}
d^{|B|-1}
\right).
}
$$

The formula has a clear proof route, but it must still be written carefully:

1. Show an invariant equivalence induces a partition of cycle indices according to which cycles are joined.
2. Show each connected cycle component has one common quotient orbit length $$d$$.
3. Prove $$d\mid n_i$$ for every cycle in that component.
4. Prove the quotient maps differ exactly by phases.
5. Prove gauge translations give the same relation and no other identifications do.
6. Count normalized phase assignments.

This is no longer just a brute-force observation; it is a clean classification theorem waiting to be written.

## Critical descriptor edge cases

Before proof promotion, explicitly test these cases beyond total-size enumeration.

| Edge case | Why it matters |
|---|---|
| One cycle $$C_n$$ | Must recover the divisor lattice of $$n$$ |
| Equal-length cycle pairs | Maximal phase freedom |
| Coprime cycle lengths | Only divisor $$d=1$$ can connect them |
| Repeated identical cycle lengths | Tests symmetry under cycle relabeling |
| Components of three or more cycles | Tests gauge normalization and holonomy |
| Mixed lengths with nontrivial gcd | Tests divisor constraints |
| Universal congruence | $$d=1$$ component |
| Equality relation | Each cycle isolated, divisor $$n_i$$ |
| Multiple quotient components with same $$d$$ | Tests component labels versus phase labels |
| Join of equal moduli with phase discrepancy | Tests gcd-only failure |
| Meet with incompatible phase differences | Tests phase-fiber splitting |

The current $$n\le7$$ sweep likely covers many of these, but named regression fixtures make the theorem durable.

## Meet formula audit

The meet intuition is strong:

$$
\text{intersection}
\quad\Rightarrow\quad
\text{simultaneous congruence conditions}.
$$

For a common cycle-index intersection $$B$$, two relations require

$$
t+a_i\equiv s+a_j\pmod{d_E},
$$

and

$$
t+b_i\equiv s+b_j\pmod{d_F}.
$$

Compatibility requires phase differences to agree modulo

$$
g=\gcd(d_E,d_F).
$$

Thus the phase fibers are defined by

$$
q_i=a_i-b_i\pmod g.
$$

Only cycles in the same $$q$$-fiber can remain identified in the intersection.

Inside one compatible fiber, one solves simultaneous congruences at modulus

$$
\ell=\operatorname{lcm}(d_E,d_F).
$$

Therefore the proposed structure

$$
\boxed{
\gcd
\to
\text{phase-difference fibers}
\to
\operatorname{lcm}
\to
\mathrm{CRT}
}
$$

is mathematically natural.

### Required proof refinement

The phrase “after a gauge shift on the $$F$$ phases” needs exact wording. A component-wide gauge translation of $$F$$ changes every $$b_i$$ by the same class; it cannot independently align different fibers. The proof should instead say:

> On a fixed fiber $$B_q$$, replace the chosen representative of the $$F$$-phase gauge class by $$b_i+q$$. Then $$a_i\equiv b_i+q\pmod{g}$$ for every $$i\in B_q$$, so the generalized CRT gives a unique phase $$c_i\pmod{\ell}$$.

That avoids accidentally claiming a single global gauge shift works across all fibers.

## Join formula audit

The join description is the potentially most interesting part.

The join is generated by the union of equivalence relations:

$$
E\vee F
=
\operatorname{EqvGen}(E\cup F).
$$

Each descriptor identification can be represented as an affine cyclic constraint:

$$
t_j-t_i\equiv c_e\pmod{d_e}.
$$

The generated quotient must satisfy all constraints. A spanning tree selects provisional potentials; every non-tree edge tests whether the potential assignment is consistent.

The residual

$$
r_e=p_j-p_i-c_e
$$

is a holonomy defect. If it is nonzero modulo the current modulus $$g$$, then the quotient modulus must be reduced:

$$
g\leftarrow\gcd(g,r_e).
$$

This is analogous to imposing a relation in a cyclic presentation:

$$
\mathbb Z/g\mathbb Z
\Big/
\langle r_e\rangle
\cong
\mathbb Z/\gcd(g,r_e)\mathbb Z.
$$

Thus the candidate join rule is better stated as:

$$
\boxed{
d_{\mathrm{join}}
=
\gcd\Bigl(
\{d_e\}_{e\in E_{\rm constraints}},
\{h_\gamma\}_{\gamma\in\mathcal C}
\Bigr),
}
$$

where $$h_\gamma$$ are cycle holonomies around a cycle basis of the constraint graph.

This is stronger than an iterative algorithm because it gives the expected closed invariant. The iterative gcd reduction should be proved equivalent to this cycle-space formula.

### Essential concern

The current narrative says:

> “For every cycle $$i$$, the corresponding internal quotient constraint also contributes its modulus $$d_e$$.”

This needs formal clarification. A descriptor component identifies entire cycles through maps to a quotient cyclic orbit. The constraint graph should include:

- vertices: cycle indices;
- edges: cross-cycle identifications with offsets;
- vertex labels or loop constraints: quotient moduli $$d_B$$.

Do not imply that every original dynamical cycle directly supplies a separate “edge” unless that is how the implementation represents it.

A cleaner representation is a **labelled graph of affine congruence constraints**:

```text
vertex i:
  cycle length n_i

component descriptor:
  quotient modulus d_B | n_i for all i in B

edge i -- j:
  phase offset a_j - a_i mod d_B
```

Then the join computes the largest common cyclic quotient satisfying all descriptor constraints.

## GCD-only counterexample: preserve it

The proposed regression witness is excellent.

For two copies of $$C_2$$,

$$
E:
\quad
d_E=2,
\quad
(a_1,a_2)=(0,0),
$$

$$
F:
\quad
d_F=2,
\quad
(b_1,b_2)=(0,1).
$$

The naive formula gives

$$
\gcd(2,2)=2.
$$

But the combined constraints require both

$$
t_2-t_1\equiv0\pmod2
$$

and

$$
t_2-t_1\equiv1\pmod2.
$$

Subtracting them forces

$$
1\equiv0\pmod{d_{\rm join}},
$$

hence

$$
d_{\rm join}=1.
$$

Therefore:

$$
\boxed{
\text{gcd of moduli alone does not determine the join.}
}
$$

This should be stored as a permanent negative-control fixture:

```text
NC-PB-JOIN-GCD-ONLY-001
```

## Exhaustive implementation audit requirements

The stated counts are meaningful only after the independent implementation is committed with its manifest.

The test suite should use three oracles:

| Operation | Candidate | Independent oracle |
|---|---|---|
| Descriptor reconstruction | Descriptor $$\to$$ relation | Direct partition relation |
| Meet | Phase-fiber/CRT formula | Set-theoretic relation intersection |
| Join | Holonomy/gcd formula | Union-find transitive closure of $$E\cup F$$ |

For every descriptor pair:

$$
\operatorname{relation}(\operatorname{meetDesc}(E,F))
=
\operatorname{relation}(E)\cap\operatorname{relation}(F),
$$

and

$$
\operatorname{relation}(\operatorname{joinDesc}(E,F))
=
\operatorname{EqvGen}(
\operatorname{relation}(E)\cup\operatorname{relation}(F)
).
$$

Do not allow the descriptor conversion routine to be shared by both candidate and oracle paths without an additional direct brute-force partition comparison. Shared encoding errors can otherwise produce false agreement.

### Required receipt fields

```json
{
  "suite_id": "AQ-PB-LATTICE-N7-001",
  "arithmetic": "integer exact",
  "cycle_types": 44,
  "descriptor_count": 2027,
  "unordered_pair_count": 453961,
  "descriptor_roundtrip_mismatches": 0,
  "meet_mismatches": 0,
  "join_mismatches": 0,
  "candidate_implementation_sha256": "required",
  "oracle_implementation_sha256": "required",
  "fixture_manifest_sha256": "required",
  "python_version": "required",
  "dependency_lock_sha256": "required",
  "stdout_sha256": "required",
  "stderr_sha256": "required",
  "repository_commit": "required"
}
```

One immediate check: if there are $$2027$$ descriptors, the number of unordered pairs **with repetition** is

$$
\binom{2027+1}{2}
=
2{,}055{,}378,
$$

while unordered distinct pairs are

$$
\binom{2027}{2}
=
2{,}053{,}351.
$$

Therefore the reported count

$$
453{,}961
$$

cannot be all unordered pairs among all $$2027$$ descriptors.

This is an important reconciliation requirement.

Possible explanations:

- Pairs were tested only within a cycle type.
- Descriptor pairs from different cycle types were not compared.
- The count sums $$\binom{N_\lambda}{2}$$ across the 44 cycle types.
- Some descriptors were excluded from pair tests.
- The reported `2027` is aggregate while pair counts are scoped differently.

The likely intended quantity is:

$$
\sum_{\lambda}
\binom{N_\lambda+1}{2}
\quad\text{or}\quad
\sum_{\lambda}\binom{N_\lambda}{2},
$$

over cycle types $$\lambda$$, not pairs across all descriptors globally.

This must be stated explicitly in every theorem and receipt. It does not weaken the test; it prevents a reproducibility ambiguity.

## Bareiss regression

The proposed Bareiss suite is useful, but it should not import NumPy merely to hold small exact integer matrices. Use pure Python integers or `fractions.Fraction`; NumPy `dtype=object` can be fine, but it introduces an unnecessary dependency and occasionally obscures arithmetic behavior.

Add the missing pivot-row-swap case:

```python
def test_requires_row_swap():
    M = [
        [0, 1],
        [1, 0],
    ]
    assert_rank(M, 2)
```

Also include a matrix with a zero leading pivot but nonzero lower pivot and a matrix with a zero leading column but later independent columns. The current test set has the latter but not a clean $$2\times2$$ row-swap test.

## Literature position

The restraint is appropriate. Classical universal algebra treats congruence lattices of unary algebras, and finite monounary-algebra congruence lattices have existing literature.  More generally, compatible equivalence relations form lattices in universal algebra.[1][2][3][4]

The candidate novelty is not:

$$
\text{“permutation-invariant equivalences exist.”}
$$

It is potentially:

$$
\boxed{
\text{an explicit, computationally effective cycle/divisor/phase coordinate calculus}
}
$$

with:

$$
\text{enumeration}
+
\text{order}
+
\text{CRT meet}
+
\text{holonomy join}
+
\text{observable-defect bridge}.
$$

The literature search should specifically use terms such as:

```text
congruence lattice finite monounary algebra
permutation algebra congruences cyclic orbits
Z-set congruences finite cyclic G-sets
congruences of disjoint unions of cycles
affine congruence constraints cyclic quotient
coset-labelled graph congruence lattice
```

No novelty claim should be made until those sources are reviewed.

## Priority proof order

The best proof sequence is:

$$
\boxed{
\text{Descriptor classification}
\to
\text{Enumeration}
\to
\text{Order}
\to
\text{Meet}
\to
\text{Join}
\to
\text{Lattice isomorphism}.
}
$$

### Proof 1: descriptor classification

This is the keystone.

For each connected component $$B$$ of the cycle-index relation induced by $$E$$:

1. Pick a base cycle $$i_0$$.
2. Let $$d$$ be the orbit length of the image of $$C_{n_{i_0}}$$ in $$X/E$$.
3. Show every cycle in $$B$$ maps onto the same quotient orbit.
4. Show $$d\mid n_i$$ for every $$i\in B$$.
5. Define phase $$a_i$$ by the image of $$(i,0)$$.
6. Show:
   $$
   (i,t)\sim(j,s)
   \iff
   t+a_i\equiv s+a_j\pmod d.
   $$
7. Prove uniqueness modulo a common phase translation.

Once this exists, enumeration is immediate.

### Proof 2: meet

Use direct simultaneous congruence:

$$
t+a_i\equiv s+a_j\pmod{d_E},
$$

$$
t+b_i\equiv s+b_j\pmod{d_F}.
$$

Subtract to derive phase-fiber compatibility modulo $$\gcd(d_E,d_F)$$. Apply CRT inside each compatible fiber.

### Proof 3: join

Recast every component descriptor as a presentation of a cyclic quotient:

$$
q_i(t)=t+a_i\pmod d.
$$

The join is the coarsest quotient satisfying both quotient presentations. Its modulus is the largest cyclic modulus under which all phase-edge equations are simultaneously solvable.

The holonomy formulation then becomes a standard presentation calculation:

$$
\mathbb Z/
\left\langle
d_e,\;
h_\gamma
\right\rangle.
$$

That route is more likely to yield a clean proof than an algorithm-first spanning-tree argument.

## Final disposition

The permutation-congruence branch deserves a dedicated research package and an executable exact suite. It should be treated as:

$$
\boxed{
\text{high-value computational discovery,
with a clear theorem path and unresolved novelty status.}
}
$$

Freeze now:

- The descriptor data model.
- The three independent computational oracles.
- The $$n\le7$$ enumeration domain.
- The pair-count scope once reconciled.
- The gcd-only join negative control.
- The Bareiss regression suite.
- The evidence-status audit.

Do **not** freeze yet:

- Full lattice isomorphism theorem.
- General order/meet/join theorem statements as “proved.”
- Any novelty claim.
- Publication status.
- Formal verification status.

The next real milestone is not another census. It is the symbolic classification proof of invariant equivalences as phase-labelled cyclic quotient maps.

## Three-layer separation

```text
AQARION/
│
├── 01_MATH_CORE/                 # Canonical mathematics only
│   ├── Definitions
│   ├── Theorems
│   ├── Exact hypotheses
│   ├── Proofs
│   ├── Scope restrictions
│   └── Stable negative-control replacements
│
├── 02_RESEARCH_HISTORY/          # Append-only historical record
│   ├── Session transcripts
│   ├── Audit passes
│   ├── Retractions
│   ├── Failed routes
│   ├── Intermediate drafts
│   ├── RAG-LUT lineage
│   └── Decision transitions
│
└── 03_EXECUTABLE_VERIFICATION/   # Sandboxed runnable artifacts
    ├── Exact engine
    ├── Unit tests
    ├── Property tests
    ├── Exhaustive census
    ├── Negative controls
    ├── Independent cross-checks
    ├── Receipts
    └── Release-policy checks
```

The rule is:

$$
\boxed{
\text{History may explain a claim, but it may never define the claim.}
}
$$

$$
\boxed{
\text{Math core states the theorem, but executable verification determines whether a computation claim is reproducible.}
}
$$

$$
\boxed{
\text{No test imports narrative/session files as mathematical input.}
}
$$

## Layer 1: canonical math core

This folder should be compact, stable, and almost boring.

```text

├── operator/

│   ├── AQ-DEFECT-NILPOTENT-001.md
│   └── AQ-OPERATOR-TYPING-001.md
│
├── cooccurrence/
│   ├── AQ-KERNEL-PROJECTION-001.md
│   ├── AQ-WEIGHTED-GRAM-001.md
│   ├── AQ-TRACE-ENERGY-001.md
│   └── AQ-SPECTRAL-SCOPE-001.md
│
├── pullback/
│   ├── AQ-DYN-PULL-JOIN-002.md
│   └── AQ-DYN-PULL-CLOSURE-001.md
│
├── arithmetic/
│   ├── AQ-DELTA-FORBIDDEN-RESIDUE-001.md
│   ├── AQ-DELTA-TWO-BLOCK-001.md
│   └── AQ-PARTITION-BIVARIATE-001.md
│
└── asymptotics/

    ├── WU1-TRANSPORT-LAW.md
    └── WU1-OPEN-REMAINDER-PROBLEM.md
```

### Core theorem registry

The mathematical core should use only four statuses:

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `DEFINITION` | Frozen object/convention |
| `THEOREM` | Complete mathematical derivation under stated hypotheses |
| `CONDITIONAL_THEOREM` | Valid only under explicitly recorded hypotheses |
| `OPEN_PROBLEM` | Not promoted |
| `NEGATIVE_RESULT` | Refuted statement with a canonical witness |

Do **not** put these labels in the core:

```text
verified
likely
AI-audited
ready
complete
confidence
publication-ready
```

Those belong to evidence or governance layers, not mathematics.

### Mathematical core registry

```yaml
AQ-KOOPMAN-EXACT-001:
  kind: THEOREM
  statement: "D_tilde=(I-P)KP=0 iff K(V_Pi) subseteq V_Pi."
  hypotheses:
    - finite deterministic T
    - pullback-row Koopman convention
    - orthogonal block-average projection
    - same source/target partition
  field: Q
  dependencies:
    - CONV-FDS-PULLBACK-001
    - DEF-BLOCK-PROJECTION-001

AQ-DEFECT-NILPOTENT-001:
  kind: THEOREM
  statement: "D_tilde^2=0 for D_tilde=(I-P)KP whenever P^2=P."
  hypotheses:
    - P is idempotent
    - ambient operator interpretation
  not_claimed:
    - "Restricted defect D: im(P)->ker(P) is squareable"

AQ-KERNEL-PROJECTION-001:
  kind: THEOREM
  statement: "rank(D_tilde)=rank(D)=m-c(H_Pi)."
  hypotheses:
    - finite deterministic map
    - same-partition pullback convention
    - H_Pi is target cooccurrence graph
  forbidden_replacements:
    - functional digraph
    - source-target bipartite graph
    - support-only graph
```

### Historical record rule

Every historical claim must state one of:

```text
CURRENT
SUPERSEDED
REFUTED
RETRACTED
QUARANTINED
UNRECONCILED
```

For example:

```yaml
AQ-DELTA-OLD-GCD-FORMULA:
  historical_status: REFUTED
  replacement: AQ-DELTA-FORBIDDEN-RESIDUE-001
  counterexample_bundle: NC-DELTA-GCD-001
  retained_because: "Prevents recurrence of invalid persistence formula"

WU1-B-OLD-POWER-TRANSPORT:
  historical_status: REFUTED
  replacement: WU1-TRANSPORT-LAW
  retained_because: "Documents circular numerical data-generation route"

D-FOUR-DEFECT:
  historical_status: RETRACTED
  reason: "(I-P)KP(I-P)=0 identically because P(I-P)=0"
  replacement: "D_tilde=(I-P)KP"
```

That preserves history while preventing RAG from returning dead material as active mathematics.
