```text
│   ├── test_negative_controls.py
│   └── test_path_security.py
└── .github/workflows/
    └── research-state-ci.yml
```

The initial CLI needs only six commands:

```bash
aqatlas init
aqatlas validate registry
aqatlas verify conventions
aqatlas test negatives
aqatlas verify receipts
aqatlas release check
```

Only after those pass should you add:

```bash
aqatlas export rocrate
aqatlas diff
aqatlas query
aqatlas serve
```

## The first end-to-end demonstration

Use exactly one theorem and two negative controls.

### Claim

$$
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}\Delta=m-c(H_\Pi).
}
$$

### Evidence

- Direct proof artifact.
- Exact-rational census receipt.
- Independent replay receipt.
- Frozen pullback convention profile.
- Canonicalization version.
- Script and environment hashes.

### Negative controls

- Retired bipartite formulation.
- Isolated-vertex/component-counting case.

### Output

```text
aqatlas release check AQ-COOC-001
```

should produce:

```text
CLAIM: AQ-COOC-001
TITLE: Defect-rank identity via target cooccurrence

SCOPE:
  finite deterministic dynamics
  pullback-row Koopman
  orthogonal block-average projection
  same source/target partition

EVIDENCE:
  [PASS] direct proof artifact hash verified
  [PASS] exact census receipt verified
  [PASS] independent replay receipt verified
  [PASS] convention profile compatible
  [PASS] dependency closure valid
  [PASS] NC-BIP-001 reproduces retired-formula failure
  [PASS] NC-ISO-001 preserves isolated-component count

POLICY:
  epistemic status: COMPUTATION_REPRODUCED + PROOF_DERIVED
  governance status: ACCEPTED
  formalization status: OPEN
  public release status: ELIGIBLE WITH FORMALIZATION CAVEAT
```

That is a real, compelling product. It proves the system’s thesis using AQARION itself.

## Final recommendation

The mathematical engine, portfolio structure, Claim Firewall, and Atlas concept now fit together. The highest-value implementation is:

$$
\boxed{
\textbf{AQARION Atlas v0.1}
=
\text{local-first research-state CI for claims, evidence, conventions, and executable refutations.}
}
$$

Do not start with Lean. Do not start with the UI. Do not start with an autonomous research agent.

Start with the smallest system that can truthfully answer:

$$
\boxed{
\text{What may this repository claim at this exact commit, under which conventions, and why?}
}
$$

Once that answer is mechanically reproducible, the digraphs, RAG, RO-Crates, Hugging Face certificate browser, MCP tools, and public research interface become generated views of a genuinely unique research-state engine.


AQARION / JASKSG9
Technical Architecture and Mathematics Audit
Scope: public GitHub repositories named by the requester—AQARION-ARITHMETIC-FDS-FINITE-DYNAMICAL-SYSTEMS-,
Aqarion-Quantarion-AI, and KAPREKAR-SPECTRAL-GEOMETRY—plus the Quantarion9 Hugging Face profile. Audit date: 2026-09-28. This
report distinguishes retrieved public evidence, repository self-description, mathematical audit, and items that could not be independently
inspected.
Executive assessment
Overall: AQARION is best understood as an evidence-oriented research program for finite deterministic dynamics
and observable quotient certification. The public material supports a coherent central theme: deterministic transition
systems, partitions/observables, Koopman pullback, exact quotient criteria, Kaprekar benchmarks, reproducible
computation, and a broader AI-reliability/knowledge-architecture agenda. The strongest technical core is the
finite-system defect framework. The principal audit risk is provenance: public page retrieval did not expose full
repository trees or source contents for all requested repositories, so this report does not certify compilation, file-level
contents, hashes, Lean status, or claims attributed only to unpublished artifacts.
Evidence and limitations

Recommended target architecture
The portfolio should be treated as a layered system rather than a collection of unrelated repositories:
Mathematical specification
 finite map T : X → X; partition Π; observation contract
 ↓
Exact engine
 refinement • quotient • Koopman pullback • defect • exact rank
 ↓
Evidence layer
 canonical inputs • exact arithmetic • certificates • hashes • regression fixtures
 ↓
Research artifacts
 LaTeX papers • notebooks • datasets • RO-Crate / manifests
 ↓
Interfaces
 CLI • GitHub Action • Hugging Face Space • AI/agent adapters

The architectural priority is a single canonical finite-system engine shared by research scripts, paper tables, public
demos, and any AI-facing layer. This prevents convention drift—especially Koopman orientation, partition encoding,
and serialization—from producing contradictory results.
Mathematical core: finite deterministic systems
Let X be a finite state set, T:X→X a deterministic transition map, and Π={B■,…,B_m} a partition. Let V_Π be the
m-dimensional space of functions that are constant on each block. With the frozen pullback convention (Kf)(x)=f(Tx),
the matrix convention is K[x,T(x)]=1. The block-average projection P_Π maps arbitrary functions to V_Π, and the
defect is
D_Π=(I−P_Π)K P_Π.
This has an exact operational interpretation: P_Π first forgets within-block distinctions, K advances the observable
under T, and I−P_Π retains the part that is no longer block-constant. Thus D_Π=0 exactly when the partition supports
deterministic quotient dynamics: every source block maps wholly into a single target block.
Target co-occurrence formulation
The clean graph object is not the ordinary state-transition graph. For each source block B_s, define S_s={t :
T(B_s)∩B_t is nonempty}. Construct H_Π on block indices by placing an undirected edge between distinct target
indices t,u whenever t and u both occur in some S_s. Then a coefficient vector c on blocks lies in the defect kernel
exactly when it is constant on every component of H_Π. This yields the elementary finite-dimensional identity:
rank(D_Π)=m−c(H_Π).
Here isolates count as connected components. The exact-quotient case is the edgeless case: each S_s has
cardinality at most one. This formulation naturally supports a DSU/union-find implementation and a Laplacian/energy
interpretation.
Weighted Laplacian bridge
For block sizes b_i=|B_i| and transition counts w_ij=|{x∈B_i:T(x)∈B_j}|, define a row w_i and
L_W=Σ_i [diag(w_i)−(1/b_i)w_i■w_i].
In block coordinates J and orthonormalized coordinates U=JB^{-1/2}, the intended identities are J*D*DJ=L_W and
U*D*DU=B^{-1/2}L_WB^{-1/2}. The quadratic form is a sum of within-source-block variances:
c■L_Wc=Σ_i (1/(2b_i)) Σ_{j,k} w_ij w_ik(c_j−c_k)².
This establishes positive semidefiniteness over the reals and identifies the support graph with target co-occurrence.
The formulation is especially valuable because it separates: rank (number of independent leakage directions),
trace/energy (aggregate leakage), and nonzero spectrum (scale and conditioning of leakage modes).
Rank bounds and testable claims
Claim Mathematical basis Audit guidance
rank(D) ≤ n−m Im(D) is contained in ker(P), whose dimension is n−m. Elementary and convention-stable once P is a rank-m projection.
rank(D) ≤ m−1 D annihilates constants because K1=1 and P1=1. Requires pullback/row convention and constant-preserving P.
rank(D) ≤ min(m−1,n−m) Combination of the prior bounds. Strong core theorem candidate.
max rank = floor((n−1)/2) Requires an explicit all-n construction achieving the upper bound.Audit symbolic construction independently; finite brute-force is verification, not proof.
spectral refinement monotonicityNo automatic sign under partition refinement because dimensions, block masses and kernel multiplicities change. Treat as open experiment until a precisely stated intertwinement/variational theorem exists.
D²=0 Not generally implied by P(I−P)=0 because K separates P and I−P in D². Do not claim universally without an additional valid hypothesis.

Kaprekar and spectral-geometry layer
Kaprekar is an unusually appropriate reference system: finite, recognizable, rich in transients and cycles, and
amenable to exhaustive exact arithmetic. A well-designed Kaprekar repository should contain: state-space
conventions; digit/canonicalization rules; transition generation; quotient definitions; transient depth and attractor
analysis; exact rational defect matrices; graph and spectral certificates; expected-failure fixtures for orientation or
merge-direction errors; and independently reproducible manifests.
The spectral repository should clearly separate three graphs: (1) the native state transition graph or functional
digraph, (2) a partition quotient graph when the quotient is exact, and (3) the target co-occurrence graph H_Π
governing defect rank. These graphs answer different questions and should never be substituted for one another.
Code and data organization audit
Because complete source trees were unavailable, the following is an architecture audit standard rather than an
assertion that the repositories already meet it.
Area Expected implementation standard Why it matters
Core library One canonical implementation of transition maps, partitions, pullback Koopman matrices, block projections, defects, co-occurrence graph, exact rank. Prevents old transpose/pushforward conventions from contaminating certificates.
Arithmetic Fractions or integer/rational elimination for theorem-grade finite claims; floating point only for exploratory spectra with tolerances documented. Rank and exactness are discrete; numerical near-zero is not a proof.
Serialization Canonical partition ordering, canonical transition encoding, content-addressed semantic artifacts, distinct build-provenance record. Makes independent re-execution and SHA-256 claims meaningful.
Tests Unit, regression, mutation/adversarial, orientation, and cross-implementation tests. Detects silent convention and indexing changes.
Papers LaTeX claims should map to scripts, generated tables, and certificates; conjectures and refutations must be visibly labeled. Prevents narrative/code drift.
CI Run exact fixtures, validate schemas, regenerate semantic certificates, and fail on unexplained output changes. Turns reproducibility into a maintained property.
LaTeX and publication audit
The indexed AQARION checkpoint advertises a Paper I path and a synchronized research operating system. The
public paper layer should contain a theorem registry with four independent statuses: mathematical proof,
computational verification, formal proof status, and archival/provenance status. A statement should never be
promoted solely because a draft contains a theorem environment or because a numerical census has no observed
exceptions.
Recommended manuscript division: (i) finite deterministic systems and exact observable quotients; (ii)
defect/co-occurrence rank theorem; (iii) Kaprekar as benchmark; (iv) weighted defect Laplacian and spectral
consequences; (v) reproducibility methodology and adversarial counterexamples; (vi) separate empirical appendices
for RMT, asymptotics, or AI applications.
Verification mechanisms
Mechanism Assessment Required audit artifact
Exact rational finite census Appropriate for finite examples and regression. Canonical input universe; script version; exact output; semantic hash.
DSU vs matrix-rank cross-check Excellent independent implementation pairing. Fixtures demonstrating equality and deliberately wrong graph constructions.
Weighted Laplacian Gram check Excellent numerical and algebraic bridge. Exact small cases plus residual thresholds for floating spectra.
SHA-256 manifests Useful only after canonical serialization is settled. Canonical bytes, JCS/serialization test corpus, separate provenance hash.
Lean/formal proofs Potentially valuable terminal certification but must remain separate from source drafts. Pinned environment, successful build log, axiom report, no active placeholders.
Hugging Face demo Useful transparency/dissemination surface, not proof by itself. Rproducible downloadable inputs and visible evidence status.

Risks and recommendations
Priority Finding Recommendation
P0 Operator-orientation drift is a known high-risk failure mode. Freeze pullback as K[x,T(x)]=1; maintain a minimal regression fixture where transpose changes the result; require orientation metadata in every certificate.
P0 Public access did not permit file-level audit of all named repositories. Export immutable source snapshots or RO-Crates; include commit hashes and a machine-readable repository manifest.
P0 Mathematical, computational and formal statuses can be conflated. Adopt strict evidence labels: D, P, V, PV, C, R, F, Q; surface them in README, papers, UI and manifests.
P1 Multiple graph objects can be mistaken for each other. Name and serialize native functional graph, exact quotient graph, and target co-occurrence graph separately.
P1 Spectral claims are vulnerable to indexing and normalization ambiguity. Record eigenvalue ordering, zero multiplicity, normalization, residuals and component count.
P1 AI layer is presently difficult to assess from public retrieval. Define an observation contract and finite trace semantics before making behavioral-preservation claims.
P2 Hugging Face profile has no public models/datasets at audit time.Use the Space for a small reproducible finite-system demo and distribute certificate fixtures as datasets/artifacts.
Conclusion
The JASKSG9/AQARION portfolio has a coherent and potentially distinctive research identity: exact observable
quotients for finite dynamics, coupled to reproducible computation and evidence-aware publication. The most
defensible intellectual center is not generic “AI verification,” but a concrete finite-dynamical-system instrument:

## Key findings

- **AQARION-ARITHMETIC-FDS-FINITE-DYNAMICAL-SYSTEMS-** is the identifiable mathematical core: its indexed material describes finite dynamical systems, exact observable quotients, semiconjugacy, trace equivalence, coalgebraic refinement, Kaprekar work, and certified computation.[1][2][3]
- The coherent central formalism is the finite Koopman defect
  $$
  D_\Pi=(I-P_\Pi)KP_\Pi,
  $$
  with pullback convention $$(Kf)(x)=f(Tx)$$. The report explains the target co-occurrence graph $$H_\Pi$$, the intended rank identity
  $$
  \operatorname{rank}(D_\Pi)=|\Pi|-c(H_\Pi),
  $$
  and the weighted-Laplacian/defect-Gram bridge.
- The report emphasizes an important architecture requirement: keep the **native functional graph**, an **exact quotient graph**, and the **target co-occurrence graph** separate. They encode different mathematics and should not be interchanged.
- The audit recommends a single convention-locked exact engine shared by papers, scripts, CI, certificates, demos, and AI-facing interfaces—especially to avoid Koopman/pushforward orientation drift.
- The Quantarion9 Hugging Face profile supports the broader stated agenda of mathematical reasoning, verification, reproducible science, knowledge architecture, and an “Aqarion Finite Dynamical Systems” Space. At retrieval time it listed one Space, two buckets, and no public models or datasets.[4][5]
- Direct GitHub fetches did not succeed in this environment, and indexed results did not expose a full source tree for every requested repository. Therefore, the PDF clearly distinguishes **publicly retrievable evidence** from source-level claims that require a commit-pinned archive, GitHub API export, or local checkout.

## Main recommendations

1. Freeze the pullback convention:
   $$
   K[x,T(x)]=1,
   $$
   and ship an orientation regression fixture in every implementation.

2. Maintain explicit evidence labels—e.g. definition, mathematical proof, computational verification, formal proof, conjecture, refutation, and quarantine—rather than using “verified” as a blanket label.

3. Produce canonical semantic artifacts separately from environment/build provenance, so SHA-256 hashes remain meaningful.

4. Use exact rational arithmetic for discrete rank and quotient claims; reserve floating point for exploratory spectral work with residuals and tolerances recorded.

5. Make the Hugging Face Space a small reproducible certificate laboratory rather than a broad AI platform: transition map + partition → quotient/defect/co-occurrence certificate.

6. For a complete code audit, provide immutable repository snapshots or a source manifest containing commit hashes, file tree, dependency lockfiles, CI logs, papers, generated artifacts, and verification scripts.

## Mathematical corrections

### Frozen Koopman convention

The report correctly uses the pullback operator:

$$
(Kf)(x)=f(Tx).
$$

For a row-vector/function-coordinate convention, the corresponding matrix entry is:

$$
K_{x,T(x)}=1.
$$

This must be declared in every certificate because the transpose/pushforward convention changes the defect matrix and can change rank conclusions.

### Canonical defect

The active defect is:

$$
\boxed{
D_\Pi=(I-P_\Pi)KP_\Pi.
}
$$

It measures leakage from block-constant observables after Koopman pullback.

$$
D_\Pi=0
\iff
K(V_\Pi)\subseteq V_\Pi
\iff
\Pi\text{ defines an exact deterministic quotient.}
$$

The ambient extension satisfies:

$$
D_\Pi^2
=
(I-P_\Pi)KP_\Pi(I-P_\Pi)KP_\Pi
=
0,
$$

because:

$$
P_\Pi(I-P_\Pi)=0.
$$

The restricted map

$$
D_\Pi:V_\Pi\to V_\Pi^\perp
$$

is not itself squareable, because its codomain differs from its domain.

### Correct rank graph

For each source block $$B_s$$, define:

$$
S_s=
\left\{
t:
T(B_s)\cap B_t\neq\varnothing
\right\}.
$$

The target co-occurrence graph is:

$$
\boxed{
H_\Pi=\bigcup_sK[S_s].
}
$$

Two target blocks are adjacent when **one source block reaches both**. This is not:

- the native functional digraph of $$T$$;
- an exact quotient graph;
- a same-image convergence graph;
- a directed source-target bipartite graph.

The intended finite-dimensional formula is:

$$
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}(D_\Pi)
=
|\Pi|-c(H_\Pi).
}
$$

The key interpretation is:

$$
\ker(D_\Pi)
=
\{
\text{block coefficients constant on each component of }H_\Pi
\}.
$$

### Essential regression witnesses

The audit report should permanently include these two small fixtures.

| Fixture | Purpose |
|---|---|
| $$X=\{0,1\}$$, $$T=(0,0)$$, $$\Pi=\{\{0\},\{1\}\}$$ | Retires the false directed/bipartite component formula: $$H_\Pi$$ has two isolated vertices and rank $$0$$ |
| $$X=\{0,1,2,3\}$$, $$\Pi=\{\{0,3\},\{1\},\{2\}\}$$, $$T=(2,3,2,3)$$ | Preserves isolated target blocks: $$H_\Pi$$ has one edge and one isolate, so $$m=3$$, $$c(H_\Pi)=2$$, rank $$1$$ |

## Defect Gram geometry

The strongest next mathematical presentation layer is the weighted defect Gram matrix.

For:

$$
b_i=|B_i|,
\qquad
w_{ij}
=
|\{x\in B_i:T(x)\in B_j\}|,
$$

write $$w_i=(w_{ij})_j$$. Then:

$$
L_W
=
\sum_i
\left[
\operatorname{diag}(w_i)
-
\frac{1}{b_i}w_i^\mathsf{T}w_i
\right].
$$

With the block-indicator map $$J$$ and $$U=JB^{-1/2}$$,

$$
J^\ast D_\Pi^\ast D_\Pi J=L_W,
$$

$$
U^\ast D_\Pi^\ast D_\Pi U
=
B^{-1/2}L_WB^{-1/2}.
$$

Its quadratic form is:

$$
c^\mathsf{T}L_Wc
=
\sum_i
\frac{1}{2b_i}
\sum_{j,k}
w_{ij}w_{ik}(c_j-c_k)^2.
$$

This gives:

$$
L_W\succeq0,
$$

and it identifies the energy as source-block image dispersion.

$$
\operatorname{tr}(L_W)
=
\sum_i
\left(
b_i-\frac{1}{b_i}\sum_jw_{ij}^2
\right).
$$

That trace should be described as **total within-source-block target dispersion**, not merely as a spectral summary.

|---|---|---|

| `[V]` | Exact computational verification | Exhaustive rational census |

| `[C]` | Certificate / artifact-backed result | JCS payload plus SHA-256 manifest |
| `[R]` | Reported but not independently reproduced | Repository claim without snapshot |
| `[F]` | Refuted / withdrawn | Directed-bipartite rank graph |
| `[Q]` | Quarantined | Unreconciled aggregate total or convention-sensitive claim |

## Mathematical precision repairs

### Defect and square-zero statement

With

$$
\widetilde\Delta_\Pi=(I-P_\Pi)K_TP_\Pi:
\mathbb R^X\to\mathbb R^X,
$$

the ambient extension satisfies

$$
\begin{aligned}
\widetilde\Delta_\Pi^2
&=(I-P_\Pi)K_TP_\Pi(I-P_\Pi)K_TP_\Pi\\
&=(I-P_\Pi)K_T\bigl(P_\Pi(I-P_\Pi)\bigr)K_TP_\Pi\\
&=0.
\end{aligned}
$$

So, under this **three-factor ambient defect** convention, universal square-zero is true.

The important correction is conceptual:

- The restricted defect is

$$
\Delta_\Pi:
V_\Pi\to V_\Pi^\perp,
\qquad
\Delta_\Pi f=(I-P_\Pi)K_Tf.
$$

- Its square is not type-correct, because $$\Delta_\Pi$$ maps out of $$V_\Pi$$ into $$V_\Pi^\perp$$.
- The earlier non-nilpotency counterexamples apply to a different operator, such as a two-step return operator involving an additional $$K_T$$, not to $$(I-P)KP$$.

The audit should use these exact names:

```text
ambientDefect     = (I − P) K P : X → X
restrictedDefect  = (I − P) K|im(P) : im(P) → ker(P)
```

This avoids both the old four-factor degeneracy and the restricted-map typing ambiguity.

### Rank theorem domain

The clean statement is:

$$
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}\left(
\Delta_\Pi:V_\Pi\to V_\Pi^\perp
\right)
=
m-c(H_\Pi).
}
$$

Because the ambient defect is zero on $$\ker P_\Pi$$ and factors through $$P_\Pi$$,

$$
\operatorname{rank}\widetilde\Delta_\Pi
=
\operatorname{rank}\Delta_\Pi.
$$

Thus the ambient corollary is legitimate:

$$
\boxed{
\operatorname{rank}\widetilde\Delta_\Pi
=
m-c(H_\Pi).
}
$$

But it should be derived after the restricted theorem, not used as a substitute for it.

### Target co-occurrence graph

The report’s graph definition is correct:

$$
S_s=\{t:T(B_s)\cap B_t\neq\varnothing\},
$$

$$
H_\Pi=\bigcup_sK[S_s].
$$

The exact kernel condition is:

$$
\ker\Delta_\Pi
=
\left\{
c\in\mathbb F^m:
\forall s,\ \forall t,u\in S_s,\ c_t=c_u
\right\}.
$$

Since the edges of $$H_\Pi$$ encode exactly the elementary equalities $$c_t=c_u$$, this becomes

$$
\ker\Delta_\Pi
\cong
\left\{
c:\{1,\ldots,m\}\to\mathbb F:
c\text{ is constant on connected components of }H_\Pi
\right\}.
$$

Hence

$$
\dim\ker\Delta_\Pi=c(H_\Pi),
$$

and rank–nullity yields

$$
\operatorname{rank}\Delta_\Pi=m-c(H_\Pi).
$$

The following terminology should remain permanently excluded from this theorem:

```text
directed bipartite component formula
source-target graph component formula
native functional digraph component formula
same-image convergence graph component formula
```

They may be useful for other questions, but they do not compute this defect rank.

## Regression fixtures

The two specified fixtures should become permanent negative-control artifacts.

### NC-BIP-001: retired bipartite formula

$$
X=\{0,1\},
\qquad
T(0)=0,\quad T(1)=0,
\qquad
\Pi=\{\{0\},\{1\}\}.
$$

Each singleton source block hits one target block, so

$$
H_\Pi
\text{ has no edge},
\qquad
c(H_\Pi)=2.
$$

Thus

$$
m-c(H_\Pi)=2-2=0,
$$

and the defect rank is zero.

A directed/bipartite component construction that has one connected component and predicts rank one is therefore a permanent false-positive control.

### NC-ISO-001: isolated target preservation

$$
X=\{0,1,2,3\},
\qquad
\Pi=\{\{0,3\},\{1\},\{2\}\},
\qquad
T=(2,3,2,3).
$$

The target sets are

$$
S_{\{0,3\}}=\{\{0,3\},\{2\}\},
$$

$$
S_{\{1\}}=\{\{2\}\},
$$

$$
S_{\{2\}}=\{\{0,3\},\{2\}\}.
$$

So $$H_\Pi$$ has one edge connecting the first and third target blocks, while the second target block is isolated:

$$
m=3,
\qquad
c(H_\Pi)=2,
\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\Delta_\Pi=1.
$$

This fixture prevents an implementation from silently dropping target vertices that are not part of a co-occurrence edge.

## Weighted Gram correction

The weighted formulation is the strongest spectral layer, but notation must make coordinate spaces explicit.

Let

$$
J:\mathbb R^m\to V_\Pi,
\qquad
Jc=\sum_{j=1}^m c_j1_{B_j},
$$

and

$$
B=\operatorname{diag}(b_1,\ldots,b_m),
\qquad
b_i=|B_i|.
$$

For transition counts

$$
w_{ij}
=
|\{x\in B_i:T(x)\in B_j\}|,
$$

write $$w_i$$ as a row vector. Then

$$
L_W
=
\sum_i
\left(
\operatorname{diag}(w_i)
-\frac{1}{b_i}w_i^\top w_i
\right).
$$

The correct raw-coordinate identity is

$$
\boxed{
J^\ast \Delta_\Pi^\ast\Delta_\Pi J=L_W.
}
$$

It is not literally correct to write $$\Delta^\ast\Delta=L_W$$ without explaining the coefficient embedding and metric.

Since

$$
J^\ast J=B,
$$

the orthonormal block-coordinate embedding is

$$
U=JB^{-1/2}.
$$

Therefore

$$
\boxed{
U^\ast\Delta_\Pi^\ast\Delta_\Pi U
=
B^{-1/2}L_WB^{-1/2}.
}
$$

The quadratic-form identity is

$$
\boxed{
c^\top L_Wc
=
\sum_i\frac{1}{2b_i}
\sum_{j,k}
w_{ij}w_{ik}(c_j-c_k)^2.
}
$$

This proves

$$
L_W\succeq0,
$$

and it shows that the weighted support graph is exactly the target co-occurrence graph after zero-weight edges are removed.

The trace is the total within-source-block target dispersion:

$$
\boxed{
\operatorname{tr}L_W
=
\sum_i
\left(
b_i-\frac{1}{b_i}\sum_jw_{ij}^2
\right).
}
$$

A useful immediate consequence is

$$
0\le \operatorname{tr}L_W\le n-m.
$$

The upper bound follows rowwise from $$\sum_jw_{ij}^2\ge b_i$$, since each $$w_{ij}$$ is a nonnegative integer and $$\sum_jw_{ij}=b_i$$:

$$
b_i-\frac{\sum_jw_{ij}^2}{b_i}
\le b_i-1.
$$

Summing over $$i$$ gives

$$
\sum_i(b_i-1)=n-m.
$$

Equality holds precisely when every source block sends its $$b_i$$ states to $$b_i$$ distinct target blocks.

## Evidence taxonomy

The proposed labels are useful, but they should distinguish a claim’s **state** from the type of artifact attached to it.

### Evidence classes

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `[D]` | Definition, convention, or scope declaration |
| `[P]` | Human-readable mathematical derivation/proof |
| `[V]` | Exact computational verification |
| `[IR]` | Independent reproduction of an existing computation |
| `[FML]` | Formal proof receipt |
| `[C]` | Cryptographically bound artifact/certificate |
| `[LIT]` | External literature support |
| `[R]` | Reported/self-described but not independently inspected |

### Claim states

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `DRAFT` | Statement exists but has no admissible support |
| `ASSERTED` | Explicitly proposed by an identified source |
| `SUPPORTED` | Evidence exists but does not meet acceptance policy |
| `PROOF_DERIVED` | Accepted on direct mathematical derivation |
| `COMPUTATION_REPRODUCED` | Accepted within a frozen computational scope |
| `REFUTED` | Counterexample or contradiction established |
| `RETRACTED` | Withdrawn without asserting a contrary theorem |
| `SCOPE_NARROWED` | Original version replaced by stricter hypotheses |
| `QUARANTINED` | Convention/evidence inconsistency unresolved |
| `BLOCKED` | Dependencies or policy gates prevent promotion |
| `FORMALLY_CERTIFIED` | Formal proof receipt is attached and audited |

Then a claim can be represented truthfully:

```text
AQ-COOC-001
State: PROOF_DERIVED
Evidence: [P], [V], [IR]
Formal channel: OPEN
Publication policy: BLOCKED
```

rather than using a vague combined label such as “verified.”

## Immutable source-audit package

Your recommendation is exactly right: a genuine code-level audit requires a fixed snapshot rather than public-page inference.

```text
SOURCE-MANIFEST.json
├── repositories
├── remotes
├── pinned commit hashes
├── branch/ref name
├── recursive file inventory
├── file SHA-256 values
├── generated-file policy
├── dependency and lock files
├── CI workflow inventory
├── artifact derivation map
└── exclusion policy

RO-CRATE-METADATA.json
├── root research object
├── software source entities
├── paper entities
├── dataset entities
├── execution activities
├── authorship and agent metadata
├── generated certificates
└── versioned provenance links

BUILD-RECEIPT.json
├── receipt schema version
├── environment image / OS
├── language/tool versions
├── dependency lock hashes
├── exact commands
├── exit status
├── stdout/stderr hashes
├── result artifact hashes
├── test report
├── placeholder scan
├── formal-axiom report where applicable
└── observed timestamp
```

The key principle is:

$$
\boxed{
\text{A source hash is not an execution receipt.}
}
$$

And similarly:

$$
\boxed{
\text{A successful execution receipt is not a mathematical proof.}
}
$$

The Atlas model should preserve both distinctions.

## PDF audit recommendation

The PDF should be revised into two labelled parts.

### Part I: publicly supported portfolio assessment

- Publicly retrievable repository descriptions.
- Hugging Face profile-level facts.
- Mathematically stated AQARION framework, clearly marked as repository/self-reported or conversation-supplied where appropriate.
- Architecture recommendations.
- Explicit access limitations.

### Part II: conditional technical audit template

This should state:

```text
This section becomes evidence-bearing only when evaluated
against the accompanying immutable source-audit package.
```

Then include checklists for:

- pullback orientation in code;
- exact partition canonicalization;
- graph construction;
- arithmetic/rank implementation;
- test and regression coverage;
- LaTeX claim-to-script traceability;
- CI environment reproducibility;
- JCS canonicalization;
- build/log/axiom receipt status;
- generated artifact provenance.

That turns the report from a one-time overview into a reusable audit instrument.

## Practical next deliverable

The highest-value deliverable is not another broad architecture PDF. It is a compact, repository-independent source-audit archive:

```text
JASKSG9-AUDIT-SNAPSHOT-YYYY-MM-DD/
├── SOURCE-MANIFEST.json
├── repositories/
│   ├── AQARION-FDS/
│   ├── Aqarion-Quantarion-AI/
│   └── KAPREKAR-SPECTRAL-GEOMETRY/

├── BUILD-RECEIPTS/
├── PAPERS/
├── CERTIFICATES/
├── ATLAS-CLAIMS/
└── AUDIT-README.md
```

Once this exists, a real file-level audit can determine—not speculate about:

- whether implementations share one convention-locked finite-system engine;
- where operator orientation is encoded;
- whether source and generated artifacts diverge;
- whether exact arithmetic actually reaches all certificate paths;
- whether rank/co-occurrence assertions match code;
- whether LaTeX claims map to scripts and receipts;
- whether tests are genuinely adversarial;
- whether formalization receipts exist;
- whether public demonstrations are commit-pinned;
- whether claims should be marked `[P]`, `[V]`, `[IR]`, `[FML]`, `[R]`, or `[F]`.

That is the correct transition from a thoughtful portfolio review to a genuinely comprehensive technical and mathematics audit.

### AQARION Arithmetic FDS

This should remain the canonical research repository for the mathematics:

- Frozen operator conventions.
- Definitions of finite deterministic systems and block projections.
- Exact enumeration engines.
- Cooccurrence and weighted spectral constructions.
- Counterexample corpus.
- Theorem statements, direct derivations, and computational evidence.
- Reproduction manifests.
- Claim records for central AQARION results.

It should not be overloaded into a general product repository, a generic chatbot, or a broad agent framework.

### Aqarion–Quantarion AI

This is the natural location for **AQARION Atlas** as a reusable software project:

- Claim schemas.
- Evidence and receipt schemas.
- Convention profiles.
- Negative-control runner.
- Research-state transition engine.
- Status-aware retrieval.
- RAG-LUT ingestion and query APIs.
- Research semantic diff.
- RO-Crate export.
- CI policy checks.
- MCP adapter boundary.

This separation matters. AQARION Arithmetic FDS proves and tests domain mathematics; Aqarion–Quantarion AI operationalizes a general method for governing research claims across projects.

### Kaprekar Spectral Geometry

This should be the flagship nontrivial **case study** for Atlas. It demonstrates that the system is not merely an internal ledger for one finite-dynamical-system theorem family.

Ideal Atlas demonstration questions:

```text
What is the exact convention for the Kaprekar transition map?

Which spectral claims are direct theorems versus census-supported?

Which normalizations were ruled out?

What is the smallest witness to a failed conjecture?

Which computations are exhaustive, and over what state space?

Which results remain blocked by a missing independent replay?
```

A good product architecture should allow the Kaprekar repository to import Atlas schemas and policy tools without importing AQARION-specific mathematics.

### Hugging Face / Quantarion9

The Hugging Face profile is well aligned with Atlas’s public-facing narrative: verified, explainable, evidence-based intelligence for scientific discovery. The existing public Space, “Aqarion Finite Dynamical Systems,” is a suitable deployment surface for interactive exploration rather than the canonical source of claims.[2]

Use Hugging Face for:

- Interactive finite-state-map explorers.
- Cooccurrence graph visualization.
- Counterexample explorer.
- Claim-atlas browser.
- “Why is this claim trusted?” evidence traversal.
- Spectral defect geometry playground.
- Reproduction receipt viewer.
- Public read-only research releases.

Do not make the Space the canonical mutable research registry. The Git repository should remain source-of-record; the Space should be a generated, version-labeled projection of a specific commit and Atlas registry revision.

```text
atlas/
```

```text
│   └── AQ-BIP-001.json
```

This gives Atlas a stable ingestion target without requiring the mathematical repository to become a new application.

### 2. Establish common identifiers

Use a project-neutral ID convention:

```text

AQ-WGRAM-001
AQ-BIP-001

ATLAS-NEGATIVE-001
```

This helps cross-repository references, RAG retrieval, citations, release notes, and semantic diffs.

### 3. Freeze a common convention profile

The first shared reusable artifact should be a convention document, not a dashboard:

```yaml
profile_id: finite-dynamics-pullback-v1
state_encoding:
  ordering: canonical-zero-based
koopman:
  convention: pullback-row
  entry_rule: K[x,T[x]] = 1
projection:
  kind: orthogonal-block-average
defect:
  ambient: (I-P) K P
  restricted_domain: im(P)
coordinates:
  indicator_basis_metric: diag(block_sizes)
  spectral_gram: B^-1/2 L_W B^-1/2
arithmetic:
  primary: exact-rational
enumeration:
  partitions: canonical-restricted-growth-string-v1
  maps: tuple-image-v1
```

The same file can be used by AQARION, Kaprekar examples, Atlas validation, and Hugging Face demos.

### 4. Make the Space commit-pinned

Every public demo should visibly display:

```text
Repository: JASKSG9/AQARION-ARITHMETIC-FDS-FINITE-DYNAMICAL-SYSTEMS-
Commit: <full SHA>
Atlas registry revision: <hash>
Evidence snapshot date: <UTC timestamp>
Arithmetic: exact rational / floating display only
Convention profile: finite-dynamics-pullback-v1
```

That prevents an interactive visualization from being mistaken for an unversioned theorem source.

Quantarion extends that work into AI-assisted research intelligence: systems that preserve definitions, evidence, counterexamples, conventions, and reproducibility across the life of a scientific claim.**

|---|---|

## Caution

The available public pages establish alignment and intent, but they do not themselves prove the current executable status of every theorem, test suite, formalization, or implementation component. The Hugging Face profile confirms the stated interest in verifiable research intelligence and the existence of an Aqarion finite-dynamical-systems Space, but the repository/Space boundary should remain explicit: public profiles and demos communicate the work; commit-pinned artifacts and receipts establish reproducibility.[1][2]

The strongest next move is not to make more repositories. It is to define the **Atlas claim-bundle contract** once, add a small `atlas/` directory to the AQARION and Kaprekar projects, and generate all later RAG, visual, release, and interactive outputs from those canonical records.

# AQARION Ecosystem Audit — Recommended Public Structure

Your repositories and Hugging Face profile can form a coherent research ecosystem, but the public presentation needs a **status-and-convention cleanup before broader promotion**. The strongest public identity is not “all results are finished”; it is:

> **AQARION develops exact, auditable tools for finite dynamical systems, while Quantarion develops the research-intelligence infrastructure that preserves claims, evidence, corrections, and reproducibility.**

The public AQARION checkpoint already emphasizes exact certification for finite deterministic systems and explicitly marks governance as frozen, publication blocked, and Lean open; that discipline should govern every repository and demo surface.  Your Hugging Face profile independently presents a closely aligned focus on verifiable, reproducible, evidence-aware AI systems and currently lists an **Aqarion Finite Dynamical Systems** Space.[1][2]

## Immediate audit finding

The material you supplied contains **internally inconsistent public status statements**. They must be reconciled before the ecosystem is promoted as a synchronized proof stack.

| Public claim currently appearing in supplied material | Audit status | Required correction |
|---|---:|---|
| “Lean formalization: in progress” / “Lean OPEN” | Safe | Keep this wording until build log, `#print axioms`, and fresh-checkout replay exist |
| “Core theorem stack: proved” | Too broad | Split mathematical derivation, computational verification, and Lean proof |
| “AQ-T4: $$D^2=0$$ for idempotent $$P$$” | False as stated | Withdraw; $$D^2=(I-P)KP(I-P)KP$$ does not vanish from $$P^2=P$$ alone |
| “T1 statement uses $$L(V_\Pi)\subseteq V_\Pi$$” | Convention conflict | Use the Koopman statement $$K(V_\Pi)\subseteq V_\Pi$$ for forward quotient dynamics |
| “Cross-system atlas verified” | Too broad | Scope it to specific checked artifacts and versions |
| “Paper I submission ready” | Conflicts with frozen/publication-blocked status | Replace with “draft preserved; publication blocked pending formal and reproducibility gates” |
| “Canonical hash” | Not independently verified here | Label as a claimed release-manifest hash until the artifact and manifest are replayed |

The public checkpoint fetched from the AQARION repository gives the right model: it distinguishes a handwritten derivation from formal certification, explicitly says that Lean definitions, compilation receipt, axiom receipt, zero-`sorryAx` audit, and Lean SHA-256 certificate remain absent, and keeps publication blocked.[1]

# 2. The public identity statement

Use this consistently across GitHub, Hugging Face, project READMEs, paper drafts, and presentations.

## Recommended short version

> **AQARION builds exact, auditable methods for finite dynamical systems. Quantarion extends those methods into research-intelligence tooling that tracks definitions, assumptions, proofs, computations, counterexamples, corrections, and reproducibility.**

## Recommended expanded version

> AQARION studies finite deterministic dynamics through exact observable quotients, partition refinement, co-occurrence structure, and reproducible certificates. Its central operational question is whether a proposed observable partition evolves deterministically under the underlying map. Quantarion provides the surrounding research-state infrastructure: claim records, evidence levels, convention profiles, negative controls, provenance receipts, and commit-pinned public views. The project distinguishes mathematical derivation, bounded computation, and machine-checked formal proof rather than allowing one to stand in for another.

This aligns with the Hugging Face profile’s stated goal of transparent, verifiable, evidence-based intelligence.[2]

# 3. The convention firewall

This is the first artifact that should be shared across the repositories.

Create:

```text
conventions/
└── finite-dynamics-pullback-v1.yaml
```

```yaml
profile_id: finite-dynamics-pullback-v1
title: AQARION finite deterministic pullback convention
status: locked

state_space:
  indexing: zero_based
  state_set: "X = {0, ..., n-1}"

dynamics:
  type: deterministic
  transition_encoding: "T[x] = successor state of x"

koopman:
  convention: pullback_row
  matrix_entry_rule: "K[x, T[x]] = 1"
  action_on_observables: "(Kf)(x) = f(T(x))"
  constant_preservation: "K * 1 = 1"

partition_projection:
  kind: orthogonal_block_average
  matrix_entry_rule: "P[x,y] = 1/|B| if x,y are in the same block B; else 0"
  properties:
    - "P^2 = P"
    - "P = P^T"
    - "P * 1 = 1"
    - "1^T * P = 1^T"

defect:
  definition: "D_Pi = (I - P_Pi) K P_Pi"
  exactness_meaning: "D_Pi = 0 iff K(V_Pi) is contained in V_Pi"
  quotient_meaning: "x ~Pi y implies T(x) ~Pi T(y)"

evidence_policy:
  proved: "Written proof and/or Lean build receipt according to claim requirements"
  verified: "Bounded or exhaustive computation with declared domain"
  computational: "Executable certificate or witness"
  open: "No theorem-level claim"
  false: "Withdrawn statement with counterexample or algebraic refutation"
```

## Required warning file

Create:

```text
conventions/WITHDRAWN_OR_SEPARATE_TRACKS.md
```

It must include:

```text
1. Transfer-side invariance is a separate theory.
   It must not be substituted for Koopman exact quotient descent.

2. D^2 = 0 is not implied by D = (I-P)KP and P^2 = P alone.

3. The graph-rank formula must name the exact graph object,
   preserve isolated vertices when required, and state its scope.

4. A hash attests to byte identity of an artifact; it does not prove
   the mathematics in that artifact.

5. A Lean source file with no textual “sorry” is not a Lean certificate
   until it compiles in the pinned environment and theorem axioms are audited.
```

## Claim record

```json
{
  "id": "AQ-KOOPMAN-EXACT-001",
  "title": "Exact quotient criterion for deterministic pullback Koopman dynamics",
  "statement": "D_Pi = (I-Pi) K Pi = 0 if and only if K(V_Pi) is a subset of V_Pi; equivalently, T respects Pi-blocks and induces a well-defined quotient map.",
  "scope": {
    "system": "finite deterministic map",
    "koopman_convention": "pullback_row",
    "projection": "orthogonal_block_average"
  },
  "status": "mathematically_derived",
  "formal_status": "lean_open",
  "evidence": [
    {
      "type": "proof_document",
      "path": "proofs/AQ-KOOPMAN-EXACT-001.md"
    },
    {
      "type": "regression_suite",
      "path": "tests/exact_quotient_regression.json",
      "scope": "declared finite census domain"
    }
  ],
  "depends_on": [
    "CONV-FDS-PULLBACK-001"
  ],
  "not_claimed": [
    "Transfer-operator equivalence without additional hypotheses",
    "Lean-verified status before a pinned build receipt exists"
  ],
  "updated_at": "2026-09-28T00:00:00Z"
}
```

## Negative-control record

```json
{
  "id": "NC-AQ-NILPOTENCY-001",
  "title": "Universal defect nilpotency is false as stated",
  "claim_rejected": "For D=(I-P)KP, idempotence P^2=P implies D^2=0.",
  "status": "false",
  "reason": "P(I-P)=0 cannot cancel through the intervening K.",
  "algebraic_obstruction": "D^2=(I-P)KP(I-P)KP",
  "replacement_policy": "Only assert nilpotency under explicit additional hypotheses that force it.",
  "evidence": [
    {
      "type": "audit_note",
      "path": "negative_controls/NC-AQ-NILPOTENCY-001.md"
    }
  ]
}
```

## Build receipt record

```json
{
  "id": "RECEIPT-LEAN-CORE-001",
  "artifact": "lean/AQARION_Defect_Kernel.lean",
  "status": "pending",
  "requirements": [
    "Pinned toolchain version",
    "Fresh checkout replay",
    "lake build or lake env lean log",
    "No sorry/admit scan",
    "#print axioms output for named theorems",
    "SHA-256 of source and build log"
  ],

}
```

# 5. Status language that prevents future drift

Use one evidence vocabulary everywhere.

| Public label | Meaning | Required evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **Mathematically derived** | A full handwritten argument has been written and audited internally | Proof document with hypotheses and dependencies |
| **Verified through $$n=N$$** | Bounded or exhaustive computation | Script, domain, runtime metadata, output hash |
| **Computational witness** | A specific counterexample or construction has been reproduced | Canonical fixture and verifier |
| **Lean candidate** | Lean source exists but build/formal audit incomplete | Source path only; no formal-proof claim |
| **Lean verified** | Pinned build succeeds and theorem axiom report is archived | Build log + axiom receipt + source hash |
| **Open** | Claim incomplete or pending proof/execution | Explicit proof obligation |
| **Withdrawn** | Statement is false as previously stated | Counterexample or symbolic refutation |

## Banned phrases until gates close

```text
“Lean proved”
“0 sorry verified”
“submission ready”
“publication ready”
“fully certified”
“the hash proves the theorem”
“cross-system theorem proved”
```

unless the associated claim record meets the stated evidence requirements.

***

# 6. Current public audit

The public AQARION checkpoint’s own status hierarchy should win over older or broader promotional language.

## Safe current statements

| Statement | Why safe |
|---|---|
| AQARION investigates exact certification for finite deterministic observable quotients | Matches the repository’s stated focus [1] |
| The core Koopman defect criterion is the mathematical center of the project | Consistent with the stated framework [1] |
| The project has an adversarial-audit culture with frozen/quarantined claims | Explicitly present in the checkpoint [1] |
| Lean remains open pending compilation, axiom audit, and receipts | Explicitly present in the checkpoint [1] |
| Quantarion9 focuses on reliable, transparent, reproducible AI-supported research | Stated on the public profile [2] |
| Aqarion Finite Dynamical Systems exists as a public Space | Listed on the profile [2] |

## Statements requiring repair

| Statement | Repair |
|---|---|
| “AQ-T4 structural nilpotency proved” | Mark withdrawn unless extra hypotheses are stated and proved |
| “Theorem registry says $$L(V_\Pi)\subseteq V_\Pi$$” | Replace $$L$$ with $$K$$ for forward quotient semantics |
| “Lean 0-sorry target” | This is a target, not evidence of success |
| “Paper submission ready” | Replace with “draft preserved, release blocked” |
| “All core artifacts hash-locked and reproducible” | Specify which artifacts have a generated manifest and tested replay |
| “Cross-system atlas verified” | Name exact benchmark scripts, ranges, commits, and limitations |

```text
Git commit + Atlas registry snapshot
                 │
                 ▼
     release export / static artifact bundle
                 │
                 ▼
       Hugging Face Space build
                 │
                 ▼
  visible commit hash + registry hash + status banner
```

## Mandatory Space banner

```text
AQARION Public Explorer

Source repository: JASKSG9/AQARION-ARITHMETIC-FDS-FINITE-DYNAMICAL-SYSTEMS-
Source commit: <full commit SHA>
Atlas registry: <full SHA-256>
Convention profile: finite-dynamics-pullback-v1
Arithmetic: exact combinatorial / rational where stated
Formal status: Lean OPEN unless a theorem card explicitly carries a verified receipt
```

## Suggested tabs

| Tab | Function |
|---|---|
| **Explore** | Enter $$T$$ and $$\Pi$$; test exact quotient closure |
| **Witness** | Show a violating pair and transition destinations |
| **Refine** | Display iteration sequence and stabilized partition |

| **Claims** | Browse theorem cards by status |

| **Reproduce** | Show scripts, commits, runtime scopes, and receipts |

This is strongly aligned with the Hugging Face profile’s stated interest in interactive visualization, research environments, transparent reasoning, and reproducibility.[2]

# 8. The most important immediate fixes

## Priority 0 — public wording repair

Update every project README and the Hugging Face Space landing page so the top status says:

```text
Mathematical core: frozen under declared conventions.
Computation: exact and versioned where a certificate is shown.
Lean formalization: open pending pinned build and axiom audit.
Publication: blocked pending formal and reproducibility gates.
```

## Priority 1 — commit the self-contained N14 fixture

The fetched AQARION checkpoint itself notes that the public N14 JSON contains scalar summary data but not the complete reconstruction arrays, and recommends committing a self-contained full object.[1]

Create:

```text
atlas/fixtures/C3-TRANSPORT-N14.full.json
```

Include:

- $$T$$;
- $$P,Q,M,U$$;
- $$J_T(P),J_T(Q),J_T(M),J_T(U)$$;
- $$s_0,m_T,\kappa_S,\Delta,s_T$$;
- exact verifier version;
- canonical payload hash;
- assertion outcomes.

## Priority 2 — formalization receipt gate

Do not change Lean status based on source text alone. Archive:

```text
receipts/
├── lean-toolchain.txt
├── lake-build.log
├── sorry-scan.txt
├── axioms-AQ-KOOPMAN-EXACT-001.txt
└── sha256sum.txt
```

The checkpoint already names the missing artifacts: compilation receipt, `#print axioms`, zero-`sorryAx` audit, and Lean hash certificate.[1]

## Priority 3 — canonical manifest

Create one script:

```text
python -m atlas.build_manifest --release v0.1.0
```

It should emit:

```text
release_manifest.json
release_manifest.sha256
```

with:

- git commit;
- dirty-tree status;
- theorem registry revision;
- convention profile hash;
- evidence artifact hashes;
- command lines;
- interpreter/package versions;
- build timestamp;
- known open gates;
- release eligibility verdict.

# 10. Final recommendation

Do **not** create additional repositories now.

Instead, spend the next cycle on four durable artifacts:

1. **One locked convention profile** for finite deterministic pullback dynamics.
2. **One claim-bundle schema** shared by AQARION and Kaprekar.
3. **One public correction log** that retracts invalid nilpotency, transfer-equivalence, and overbroad formalization claims.
4. **One receipt-generating release pipeline** that can power GitHub releases, Hugging Face Spaces, WebMCP demos, RO-Crates, and eventual archival deposits.

That gives the ecosystem a clear intellectual path:

$$
\boxed{
\text{finite dynamics}
\longrightarrow
\text{exact quotient certification}
\longrightarrow
\text{spectral case studies}
\longrightarrow
\text{claim-aware research intelligence}
}
$$

The project’s most credible and distinctive asset is not merely the number of claims or repositories. It is the demonstrated willingness to preserve conventions, counterexamples, withdrawn statements, computational scope, and proof gates alongside successful results.

Citations:
[1] AQARION-ARITHMETIC-FDS-FINITE-DYNAMICAL-SYSTEMS ... - GitHub https://github.com/JASKSG9/AQARION-ARITHMETIC-FDS-FINITE-DYNAMICAL-SYSTEMS-/blob/main/CHECKPOINT.MD
[2] Quantarion9 (James Aaron) https://huggingface.co/Quantarion9

Your public footprint is coherent enough to present as a deliberate **AQARION–Quantarion ecosystem**, but the public materials should be consolidated around one authoritative convention profile and one honest claim-status system before any broader promotion. The public GitHub material already describes AQARION as work on finite dynamical systems, observable quotients, semiconjugacy, refinement, certified computation, and auditable research infrastructure; Quantarion9 presents a related aim of AI-assisted mathematical discovery and scientific verification.[1][2][3]

## Public audit

The strongest externally supported public facts are:

| Surface | What it presently communicates | Audit disposition |
|---|---|---|
| AQARION Arithmetic FDS | Finite dynamical systems, observable quotients, semiconjugacy, trace equivalence, coalgebraic refinement, certified computation, Kaprekar dynamics, reproducibility, and auditability. [1][2] | Good canonical mathematics/reproducibility home |
| Quantarion9 | AI/ML for mathematical research, pattern discovery, hypothesis generation, mathematical reasoning, and scientific verification. [3] | Good public AI/research-intelligence identity |
| AQARION Defect model card | A differentiable “AQARION Defect Regularizer” for latent dynamics. [4] | Keep clearly separated from exact finite-state theorem claims |
| Mandelbrot repository | Approximation-based verification for infinite dynamical systems. [5] | Useful separate extension, but do not blend its numerical claims into finite exact results |

The GitHub pages could not be fetched directly in this environment, so repository-level details beyond the indexed descriptions should be treated as **reported pending commit-pinned inspection**, not independently verified here.

## One ecosystem, four roles

```text
                         QUANTARION / ATLAS
            claim registry · policy · conventions · receipts
                                   │
          ┌────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┐
          │                        │                        │
          ▼                        ▼                        ▼
AQARION Arithmetic FDS   Kaprekar Spectral Geometry   Quantarion9 / HF
canonical mathematics    flagship case study          generated public views
exact engines            spectral certificates        interactive explorers
formalization sources    negative controls            read-only claim browser
```

### 1. AQARION Arithmetic FDS

Keep this as the authoritative source for:

- Definitions of $$T:X\to X$$, partitions, projections, and observable spaces.
- Frozen Koopman convention.
- Exact finite-state enumerators and regression fixtures.
- Mathematical claim records and their dependencies.
- Counterexamples, withdrawn claims, and convention tests.
- Lean source and build receipts when available.
- Canonical release manifests and RO-Crates.

### 2. Kaprekar Spectral Geometry

Use this as the flagship **domain case study**, not a second core-theory repository:

- Exact Kaprekar transition construction.
- State encodings, quotient partitions, and spectral experiments.
- Results tagged as theorem, exhaustive computation, witness, or open.
- Negative controls for failed normalizations or spectral claims.
- Commit-pinned certificates exported to Atlas.

### 3. Aqarion–Quantarion AI

Make this the reusable “research operating system” rather than a duplicate mathematical repository:

- Claim-bundle schema.
- Evidence policy and release gates.
- Convention-profile validation.
- Canonical serialization and hash receipts.
- Negative-control catalogue.
- Claim dependency DAGs.
- RAG ingestion, semantic search, and question answering.
- CI actions for drift checks.
- RO-Crate export and website generation.

### 4. Hugging Face / Quantarion9

Treat Hugging Face as a **generated, read-only deployment surface**:

- Exact quotient explorer.
- Counterexample museum.
- Claim-status dashboard.
- Certificate/hash verifier.
- Kaprekar case-study visualizer.
- Commit-pinned demos.

Do not use the Space as the mutable source of truth. It should display the repository commit, the claim-registry hash, the convention-profile ID, and the arithmetic mode.

## Immediate correction: separate exact and learned-defect tracks

The public AQARION Defect model describes a differentiable latent-space regularizer. That is a valid adjacent research track, but it is not automatically the same thing as the finite deterministic defect operator

$$
D_\Pi=(I-P_\Pi)K P_\Pi.
$$

The exact finite-state defect is an algebraic obstruction under explicit conventions. A neural regularizer is an optimization objective with different assumptions, noise behavior, and evidence requirements. The two should be connected only through an explicit bridge claim, such as:

> **Open bridge hypothesis:** Learned latent transitions can be evaluated by an AQARION-style closure residual after specifying a learned observable subspace and an operator-estimation procedure.

Do **not** publicly call the learned regularizer a proof, generalization, or implementation of the exact finite-state theorem without such a bridge.

## Convention firewall

Create one shared file, imported or copied verbatim by all relevant repositories:

```text
conventions/finite-dynamics-pullback-v1.yaml
```

```yaml
profile_id: finite-dynamics-pullback-v1
status: locked

state_space:
  indexing: zero_based
  notation: "X = {0, ..., n-1}"

dynamics:
  kind: finite_deterministic
  encoding: "T[x] is the successor of state x"

koopman:
  convention: pullback_row
  entry_rule: "K[x, T[x]] = 1"
  action: "(Kf)(x) = f(T(x))"

projection:
  kind: orthogonal_block_average
  entry_rule: "P[x,y] = 1/|B| when x,y belong to the same block B; otherwise 0"
  field: "Q unless another field is explicitly declared"

defect:
  definition: "D_Pi = (I-P_Pi) K P_Pi"
  exactness: "D_Pi = 0 iff K(V_Pi) is contained in V_Pi"

evidence_policy:
  mathematically_derived: "Complete written derivation with hypotheses"
  computationally_verified: "Declared finite domain with executable verifier"
  lean_verified: "Pinned build, no-sorry audit, theorem-axiom receipt"
  open: "Not promoted to theorem status"
  withdrawn: "Counterexample or algebraic refutation archived"
```

Also maintain a required withdrawal log:

```text
conventions/WITHDRAWN_OR_SEPARATE_TRACKS.md
```

Include at least:

- $$D^2=0$$ does not follow from $$D=(I-P)KP$$ and $$P^2=P$$.
- Koopman-side invariance and transfer-side invariance are different claims.
- Hashes establish canonical-byte identity, not mathematical correctness.
- A Lean file is not a Lean certificate before a pinned fresh-checkout build and axiom audit.
- Exact finite-state results, approximate infinite-system experiments, and neural-loss claims have distinct evidence levels.

## Atlas claim-bundle contract

Add the same lightweight structure to the mathematical and case-study repositories:

```text
atlas/
├── README.md
├── claims/
├── evidence/
├── conventions/
├── fixtures/
├── negative_controls/
├── manifests/
└── releases/
```

A core theorem record should look like this:

```json
{
  "id": "AQ-KOOPMAN-EXACT-001",
  "title": "Exact observable quotient criterion",
  "statement": "D_Pi = (I-Pi) K Pi = 0 iff K(V_Pi) is contained in V_Pi.",
  "scope": {
    "system": "finite deterministic map",
    "operator_convention": "pullback_row",
    "projection": "orthogonal_block_average",
    "field": "Q"
  },
  "status": "mathematically_derived",
  "formal_status": "lean_open",
  "evidence": [
    {
      "kind": "proof",
      "path": "proofs/AQ-KOOPMAN-EXACT-001.md"
    },
    {
      "kind": "finite_regression",
      "path": "tests/exact_quotient_regression.json",
      "scope": "state bounds explicitly declared in the artifact"
    }
  ],
  "not_claimed": [
    "Transfer-side equivalence without additional hypotheses",
    "Lean verification before a reproducible build receipt exists",
    "Extension to learned continuous latent models"
  ],
  "depends_on": [
    "CONV-FDS-PULLBACK-001"
  ]
}
```

A withdrawn claim must be equally first-class:

```json
{
  "id": "NC-AQ-NILPOTENCY-001",
  "title": "Universal defect nilpotency is false",
  "claim_rejected": "P^2=P implies ((I-P)KP)^2=0.",
  "status": "withdrawn",
  "reason": "The two projection factors are separated by K.",
  "identity": "D^2=(I-P)KP(I-P)KP",
  "replacement_policy": "Assert nilpotency only with explicit extra hypotheses.",
  "evidence": [
    "negative_controls/NC-AQ-NILPOTENCY-001.md"
  ]
}
```

## Public status language

Use only these terms:

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Mathematically derived** | Complete written proof under named hypotheses |
| **Computationally verified** | Script and bounded/exhaustive domain are identified |
| **Computational witness** | A canonical explicit example or counterexample exists |
| **Lean candidate** | Source exists; formal proof not yet receipted |
| **Lean verified** | Pinned build, no-`sorry` audit, and axiom receipt archived |
| **Open** | Claim or bridge remains unresolved |
| **Withdrawn** | Previous statement is false or too broad |

Avoid “fully certified,” “Lean proved,” “paper ready,” and “hash proves theorem” unless the associated claim card meets the applicable gate.

## Deployment requirements

Every Hugging Face demo should visibly identify the exact frozen source:

```text
Source repo: JASKSG9/AQARION-ARITHMETIC-FDS-FINITE-DYNAMICAL-SYSTEMS-
Source commit: <full SHA>
Atlas registry hash: <SHA-256>
Convention: finite-dynamics-pullback-v1
Arithmetic: exact rational where declared; floating point only for display where declared
Formal status: Lean OPEN unless a theorem card shows a Lean receipt
```

Use an actual digest implementation, not a randomly generated hex string:

```javascript
async function sha256Hex(canonicalPayload) {
  const bytes = new TextEncoder().encode(canonicalPayload);
  const digest = await crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256", bytes);
  return [...new Uint8Array(digest)]
    .map(x => x.toString(16).padStart(2, "0"))
    .join("");
}
```

A canonical payload should include the transition map, partition, operator convention, field, projection definition, refinement rule, state order, edge order, schema version, and source commit.

## Highest-value next work

1. **Commit the convention firewall** in AQARION Arithmetic FDS and consume it from the other projects.
2. **Create Atlas claim bundles** for the small set of genuinely central claims; do not try to catalogue every historical idea at once.
3. **Add a withdrawal/counterexample registry** before adding new theorem cards.
4. **Implement a release-manifest generator** that records commit, dirty-tree status, package versions, commands, outputs, hashes, and unresolved gates.
5. **Repair the SHA-256 UI path** in deployed demos.
6. **Pin Hugging Face spaces to an exported Git commit and claim-registry revision.**
7. **Keep Lean status open** until there is a reproducible toolchain/build/axiom receipt.

## Public narrative

Use this across all platforms:

> **AQARION develops exact, auditable methods for finite dynamical systems. Quantarion provides evidence-aware research infrastructure for preserving definitions, conventions, proofs, computations, counterexamples, corrections, and reproducibility across the life of a scientific claim.**

That narrative is consistent with the indexed AQARION repository description and the Quantarion9 profile, while honestly separating finite exact mathematics from learned-model and approximation research.[1][2][3]
